vector p has sum zero and ||Vp||^2=0, forcing p=0 by affine independence,
then nu=0. A feasible affine solution satisfying all vertex normals is
the projection by the distance expansion. A bounded active-set procedure
adds violated normals and moves toward affine optima until a first
blocking mass is zero; failure of any feasibility/normal/linear solve
test invalidates the case. No iteration-complexity theorem is assumed.

Writing y=x-g, a stable value formula is
F_scaled=||g||^2/2+<y,g>, with <y,g>>=0 by the normal at zero.
The code never obtains F by subtracting ||x||^2 and ||y||^2. Its alternate
dot-product formula <x,g>-||g||^2/2 is saved only for a cancellation check.
In the freshly checked core it uses g=x and F=||x||^2/2. For jets from
this single static function, the ordered convention of accepted fact
1252b8823bb5a290 becomes
Q_ij=F_i-F_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
    =<y_j,g_i-g_j><=0.
Thus exact feasibility and normals imply every original ordered row,
including zero optimum and terminal rows; floating checks remain evidence.
The physical GD identity, derived by expansion, is
||x_next||^2=(1-h)^2||x||^2+h(2-h)||y||^2-2h(h-1)<y,g>.
Both signed defect terms and its local residual are logged at every step.

Radial calibration: since all full leaves have norm one,
<v_left,v_s><=1. For t>=delta, g=delta*v_left has normals
delta*(t-delta)*(<v_left,v_s>-1)<=0 at x=t*v_left.
If 0<delta<=1/(1+S), all iterates have
x_i=(1-delta*sum_(t<i)h_t)*v_left and g_i=delta*v_left,
including the terminal query, since each coefficient is at least delta.
Consequently V=2delta-delta^2*(1+2S).
For the registered zeta=1, delta=1/(1+2S), this yields exactly
V=1/(1+2S), the accepted global Huber payoff. This is rederived for
this SAME scaled simplex, rather than imported from a different function.
The formula is not extended to zeta=4,8,16, where its hypothesis fails.
Job1 compares both independently evolved full/reduced trajectories AND
their every-query radial positions, gradients and values.

Accepted comparisons: fact b98bb807d514e80d gives the same-word Huber
lower witness 1/(1+2S). The unconditional original ALL-split conclusions
of 04219554e41d9750, proof(7.1), give B_1=4 and
B_(2n+1)>=lambda*B_n, so at n=2^m-1, m>=1, the permitted closed-form
lower bound is B_lower=4*lambda^(m-1). Its proof(9.3) gives
B_upper=4*lambda^m. These are evaluated only INSIDE the registered cases.
B_lower*V>1 would support success if the numerical value is accurate;
B_upper*V<1 would support failure for that finite case. B_upper*V>1
proves no success, and B_lower*V<1 proves no failure. Neither numerical
comparison is an interval proof, eventual law, or all-word result.

Literature background only: Matlas returned Moreau-envelope Lemma4.1
of arXiv:2303.08785 and unrelated projected-descent claims. No external
theorem is imported. Wang--Carreira-Perpinan arXiv:1309.1541, Sections1--3
and proof of Theorem1, was downloaded and read to check the distinction
between its Euclidean probability metric and this feature metric. Its
equal-active-derivative argument motivates the normal checks; its scalar
threshold formula does not transfer through the nonisometric feature map.
Every mathematical assertion used here has its derivation above or an
explicit accepted fact citation.

# Analytic interpretation after credit closure

Status: additional part of the SAME JOINT UNVERIFIED round55 draft.
This text uses symbolic reasoning and reads frozen records only; no
further model evaluation, arithmetic replay or new case was executed.
The body, normal condition and normalization are derived in the attached
DERIVATION.md as part of this joint object, not adopted from an earlier
unverified finding. No result here is a fact-graph brick.

There is a short exact explanation for the first major face transition
in both selected zeta4 trajectories, valid beyond the recorded depths.
It does not control their whole suffix or assert an asymptotic payoff.

Fix m>=2 and the SAME centered tree, weights, original word and unit
start as in DERIVATION.md, with zeta=4. Let
k=2^(m-1)-1, b=1+a*c^(m-1), S_left=sum H_(m-1),
D=1+2S=1+4S_left+2b and delta=4/D.
Every h_i is positive. Since beta>=1/2 and c>1,
b=1+beta*c^(m-2)>=3/2. For each i<=k the prefix sum s_i<=S_left,
and hence

  1-delta*s_i >= (1+2b)/D >= delta.

For any t>=delta, projection of t*v_left onto delta*K_m is
delta*v_left: its full-leaf normal is
delta*(t-delta)*(<v_left,v_s>-1)<=0, since both leaves have norm one.
Induction therefore proves at every query through the largest bridge

  x_i=(1-delta*s_i)*v_left, g_i=delta*v_left, 0<=i<=k,
  x_(k+1)=-(2b-1)*v_left/D.

These are actual physical queries and the actual largest bridge h_k=b.
Put T=(2b-1)/4, so x_(k+1)/delta=-T*v_left.
The first reduced vertex is b_0=-sqrt(w_0)*e_root. Its candidate
normalized residual is Y=-T*v_left+sqrt(w_0)*e_root. For 1<=ell<m,

  <Y,b_ell-b_0>
    =2w_0-T*(2w_0+sum_(1<=j<ell)w_j-w_ell).

The parenthesized quantity is smallest at ell=1, because successive
increments equal 2w_ell-w_(ell+1)=w_ell*(2-r)>0. The final all-left
vertex has a further positive increment 2w_(m-1). Thus every inactive
normal is strictly negative whenever

  T > 2/(2-r)=c/(c-1).

For m>=4, beta>=3/4, c>lambda and lambda^2>5, giving
b>1+(3/4)*5=19/4 and T>17/8>2; meanwhile c/(c-1)<2 because c>2.
Thus, for EVERY m>=4, the exact first post-bridge gradient is

  g_(2^(m-1))=delta*b_0.

It is a right-root SUBTREE AVERAGE, not an individual full leaf; its
unique full-leaf barycentric representation is uniform on that subtree.
The state has nonzero coordinates at every depth, whereas this gradient
has only its root coordinate. The deep state is retained. In the frozen
m8/m10 records this transition occurs respectively at queries128/512,
as the symbolic calculation predicts. This derivation does not fix the
next support: the recorded trajectories subsequently use several faces.

For the observed m8,zeta4 suffix all supports are contained in {0,1,2};
the m10,zeta4 suffix has the same containment, including {2} and {0,2}.
Since b_0,b_1,b_2 have no coordinates at depth>=3, every such recorded
GD update leaves those deep coordinates unchanged. Their terminal values
in the raw rows therefore equal the first post-bridge values. In these
cases the objective is largely the positive excess <x-g,g>, not the
tiny squared-gradient contribution. This explains why the new scale
avoids the old pilot's observed terminal core collapse. The containment
is a statement about the recorded runs, not an all-m induction.

Conversely the zeta8 and zeta16 m8 records end with long checked core
suffixes. There g=x at each queried state, so x_next=(1-h)x. To describe
the mechanism symbolically, define s2(i) as the binary digit sum and
Pi_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t). Because
1-h_t=-beta*c^(v2(t+1)-1) and
sum_(j=1)^i v2(j)=i-s2(i), one has

  Pi_i=(-1)^i*beta^i*c^(-s2(i)).

Whenever a suffix from q through n is actually inside the body, induction
in the physical updates gives

  V=||x_q||^2*beta^(2(n-q))*c^(2*s2(q)-2m).

This is a CONDITIONAL identity; it does not assert that such a suffix
exists. The frozen records checked all suffix queries, starting at90
for zeta8 and at85 for zeta16. No product or new payoff was evaluated
from this formula after closure. The identity explains the loss of
terminal value in those records without extending it to untested m.

The earlier core visit at zeta16 query49 is insufficient: its next
physical step is1.875, and query50 is outside. In the saved query50
inverse barycentric coordinates the last mass is about -0.19209828,
with squared projection residual about4.2509e-7. The actual projection
has support {0,1,2}. No closeness-to-boundary ambiguity explains this
exit. This reinforces the need to keep every physical query and to
check fresh membership, even for a step below2.

Two independent obstructions to a useful conclusion remain. First,
the best registered noncalibration value remains below the accepted
benchmark upper comparison and below the global Huber witness, despite
its much larger value than the old unit-body run. Second, the records
give no uniform all-m bound or useful lower law for the suffix face
sequence. The exact early bridge law above does not close that missing
interface. Other scales and other initial points were not scanned.
The original unrestricted all-word exact-profile problem remains OPEN.

# Full frozen preregistration

# Frozen scaled centered-tree discovery, max3 round55

Authority: TASK.md and master_guidance6216f7ee407cd7e9. Exactly one round
after completed54, ceilings completed55/attempted56, no automatic renewal.
Original Goal OPEN. No formal gate or fact_submit. Joint UNVERIFIED model
and radial calibration derivation: DERIVATION.md. No older draft is used
as an accepted premise. Accepted benchmark scopes are stated there.

The SAME original H_m, enclosing-m parameters, Haar weights and unit
all-left initial leaf are used throughout. The sole model change is the
body scale delta=zeta/(1+2S_m). L=1 and true R^2=1 remain mathematically
derived for this object. Physical states stay unscaled; the projection
solver works at X=x/delta with the unit Haar vertices for conditioning,
then g=delta*P_K(X) is the actual physical gradient. Every physical step
is taken. No face/core shortcut skips future queries.

Semantic slots, frozen before job1:
1. m2,zeta1, binary arithmetic calibration: independent full four-leaf
   and reduced trajectories at all four queries, with all15 nonempty
   full faces checked per query. Compare both with the rederived radial
   Huber identity at every query and terminal V=1/(1+2S).
2,3,4. Exactly m8 at zeta4,8,16 respectively, once each, only after a
   passing calibration. A failed case is invalid and cannot select zeta.
5. Exactly m10 at the zeta with greatest RECORDED VALID m8 terminal V;
   ties choose the smaller zeta. No interpolation or further tuning.
6. Independently recompute the entire m8 trajectory at that SAME chosen
   zeta with Decimal, precision80 significant decimal digits, including
   every constant and weight; compare every query with its saved primary.
   Alternatively this slot may be consumed ONCE by a documented necessary
   implementation/calibration correction, with a frozen amendment before
   execution and no changed parameter or initializer. It may precede
   discovery if needed. There is no second correction or extra run.
No valid m8 case retires slots5/6. Unrepaired calibration closes discovery.
Unused slots retire. All failures, timeouts, repairs and precision count.

Budget: six jobs, full8s reservation EACH, at most48 charged seconds.
The absolute canonical ledger is
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/scaled_tree_projection_20261001_budget.json.
All new public files are in the corresponding directory without _budget.
Main guard1083->1131 is a conservative reservation, NOT measured runtime.
The new runner durably charges before spawning the evaluator or importing
its mathematics. No evaluator import, root/weight evaluation, dry run,
mathematical postprocessing or checking occurs outside a charged job.
Only source editing, text reading, data copying/hashing and ledger
bookkeeping occur outside. Old runner and CLOSED ledgers are untouched.

Every invocation has external `timeout --signal=TERM --kill-after=0.1s 8s`.
The runner child timeout expires by runner-start+7.5s; evaluator soft
guard6.3s and alarm6.8s leave time to write failure and bookkeeping.
Maximum reduced active additions+deletions per projection is4(m+1)^2;
maximum affine solves is that bound+1. A per-query0.5s guard also applies.
Failures preserve prior compressed rows, summary/error and original
stdout/stderr. All exits consume the full8s, with no refund.

Primary arithmetic: actual Python float significand length is reported
from sys.float_info (expected53 binary bits), fsum for dot products.
It solves positive-definite face-edge systems by Cholesky, starts at
the nearest vertex and enters a maximally violated normal. Core masses
use the triangular q recursion. Calibration disables the reduced core
shortcut and uses independent full augmented Gram/equality systems with
pivoted Gaussian elimination, evolving a distinct full state.

Precision arithmetic:80 decimal significant digits, ROUND_HALF_EVEN,
Overflow and Underflow traps. Fresh constants use decimal square roots
and arithmetic, no float-derived constant. The affine systems are the
augmented Gram/equality equations, solved by pivoted Gaussian elimination;
start at vertex0, enter the first violated normal, use the explicit
inverse barycentric core formula. This is numerical evidence, NOT an
interval proof. Comparing saved float decimals to this run measures
agreement, not certified error. The baseline never supplies its faces.

Float tolerances: KKT, complementarity and reconstruction 2e-11 times
(1+||X||+||P_K(X)||); feasibility/sum2e-12; entering normal1e-13 times
the same scale; linear residual2e-11*(1+max|rhs|). Cholesky diagonal
residual>1e-18; Gaussian pivot magnitude>1e-20. Calibration absolute
comparison2e-10. Decimal: KKT/reconstruction/complementarity/linear1e-60,
feasibility and entering normal1e-65, both pivot cutoffs1e-75.
The same KKT check is repeated in physical coordinates. Fresh core
membership requires EVERY computed mass>=0 without tolerance. The core
gradient is assigned x only after that test and reconstruction checks.
An active-set first-blocking coefficient is assigned exact zero only
after its preassignment discrepancy is logged and <=feasibility tolerance;
all other negative masses are retained for the solve or invalidate it.
No clipping, regularization or fallback thresholding is allowed.

At every query save x, x/delta, g, g/delta, all group masses, F, physical
step, prefix sum, residual x-g, complete physical/normalized normals,
stationarity, complementarity, feasibility/reconstruction errors, core
candidate masses, strict/tolerance supports, signed norm-defect terms,
norm identity residual, pivot counts and all counters. Stable F is
||g||^2/2+<x-g,g>, or ||x||^2/2 in a freshly checked core. Also save the
alternate dot-product formula and relative discrepancy; never subtract
two large squared norms to obtain F. Save negative-excess/value flags;
negative F beyond KKT_tol*delta^2 or excess below
-KKT_tol*delta^2*(1+||X||) invalidates. Check ||g||^2<=delta^2*(1+10KKT_tol).

Nonfinite saved scalars invalidate. Count underflows in all dot-product
multiplications and GD products, all finite saved scalar checks, and
saved subnormals; record Decimal context flags. This detection is not
exhaustive binary arithmetic tracing or an error enclosure. Full m2
vertices, states, chosen weights and all60 face candidates are retained.
No full-space diagnostics are sampled: beyond m2 the exact symmetry
reduction covers all leaves through all m+1 normals. Raw logs include
every query including the terminal projection. Both trajectories and
every internal projection, pivot, support/KKT check and solve are counted.

Only registered cases evaluate Huber=1/(1+2S),
B_lower=4lambda^(m-1), B_upper=4lambda^m, and their V ratios/products.
No new benchmark DP, complete-complex optimization, external PEP/SDP,
stepsize optimizer, installations, consult or subagents. A B-upper product
above1 gives no success; a B-lower product below1 gives no failure.
Finite values establish no eventual asymptotic or all-word claim.

Source, this plan, derivation, model, precision, tolerances and branch
rule are hashed into FROZEN.json before job1. Every reservation checks
them. Any sole authorized correction preserves the original sources and
records its changes and new hash before using semantic slot6.
After closure only analytic interpretation and reading/copying of frozen
records are permitted. Publish the complete plan, report, raw manifest,
and CLOSED canonical ledger, then end this round.

# Frozen scaled centered-tree discovery, max3 round55

Authority: TASK.md and master_guidance6216f7ee407cd7e9. Exactly one round
after completed54, ceilings completed55/attempted56, no automatic renewal.
Original Goal OPEN. No formal gate or fact_submit. Joint UNVERIFIED model
and radial calibration derivation: DERIVATION.md. No older draft is used
as an accepted premise. Accepted benchmark scopes are stated there.

The SAME original H_m, enclosing-m parameters, Haar weights and unit
all-left initial leaf are used throughout. The sole model change is the
body scale delta=zeta/(1+2S_m). L=1 and true R^2=1 remain mathematically
derived for this object. Physical states stay unscaled; the projection
solver works at X=x/delta with the unit Haar vertices for conditioning,
then g=delta*P_K(X) is the actual physical gradient. Every physical step
is taken. No face/core shortcut skips future queries.

Semantic slots, frozen before job1:
1. m2,zeta1, binary arithmetic calibration: independent full four-leaf
   and reduced trajectories at all four queries, with all15 nonempty
   full faces checked per query. Compare both with the rederived radial
   Huber identity at every query and terminal V=1/(1+2S).
2,3,4. Exactly m8 at zeta4,8,16 respectively, once each, only after a
   passing calibration. A failed case is invalid and cannot select zeta.
5. Exactly m10 at the zeta with greatest RECORDED VALID m8 terminal V;
   ties choose the smaller zeta. No interpolation or further tuning.
6. Independently recompute the entire m8 trajectory at that SAME chosen
   zeta with Decimal, precision80 significant decimal digits, including
   every constant and weight; compare every query with its saved primary.
   Alternatively this slot may be consumed ONCE by a documented necessary
   implementation/calibration correction, with a frozen amendment before
   execution and no changed parameter or initializer. It may precede
   discovery if needed. There is no second correction or extra run.
No valid m8 case retires slots5/6. Unrepaired calibration closes discovery.
Unused slots retire. All failures, timeouts, repairs and precision count.

Budget: six jobs, full8s reservation EACH, at most48 charged seconds.
The absolute canonical ledger is
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/scaled_tree_projection_20261001_budget.json.
All new public files are in the corresponding directory without _budget.
Main guard1083->1131 is a conservative reservation, NOT measured runtime.
The new runner durably charges before spawning the evaluator or importing
its mathematics. No evaluator import, root/weight evaluation, dry run,
mathematical postprocessing or checking occurs outside a charged job.
Only source editing, text reading, data copying/hashing and ledger
bookkeeping occur outside. Old runner and CLOSED ledgers are untouched.

Every invocation has external `timeout --signal=TERM --kill-after=0.1s 8s`.
The runner child timeout expires by runner-start+7.5s; evaluator soft
guard6.3s and alarm6.8s leave time to write failure and bookkeeping.
Maximum reduced active additions+deletions per projection is4(m+1)^2;
maximum affine solves is that bound+1. A per-query0.5s guard also applies.
Failures preserve prior compressed rows, summary/error and original
stdout/stderr. All exits consume the full8s, with no refund.

Primary arithmetic: actual Python float significand length is reported
from sys.float_info (expected53 binary bits), fsum for dot products.
It solves positive-definite face-edge systems by Cholesky, starts at
the nearest vertex and enters a maximally violated normal. Core masses
use the triangular q recursion. Calibration disables the reduced core
shortcut and uses independent full augmented Gram/equality systems with
pivoted Gaussian elimination, evolving a distinct full state.

Precision arithmetic:80 decimal significant digits, ROUND_HALF_EVEN,
Overflow and Underflow traps. Fresh constants use decimal square roots
and arithmetic, no float-derived constant. The affine systems are the
augmented Gram/equality equations, solved by pivoted Gaussian elimination;
start at vertex0, enter the first violated normal, use the explicit
inverse barycentric core formula. This is numerical evidence, NOT an
interval proof. Comparing saved float decimals to this run measures
agreement, not certified error. The baseline never supplies its faces.

Float tolerances: KKT, complementarity and reconstruction 2e-11 times
(1+||X||+||P_K(X)||); feasibility/sum2e-12; entering normal1e-13 times
the same scale; linear residual2e-11*(1+max|rhs|). Cholesky diagonal
residual>1e-18; Gaussian pivot magnitude>1e-20. Calibration absolute
comparison2e-10. Decimal: KKT/reconstruction/complementarity/linear1e-60,
feasibility and entering normal1e-65, both pivot cutoffs1e-75.
The same KKT check is repeated in physical coordinates. Fresh core
membership requires EVERY computed mass>=0 without tolerance. The core
gradient is assigned x only after that test and reconstruction checks.
An active-set first-blocking coefficient is assigned exact zero only
after its preassignment discrepancy is logged and <=feasibility tolerance;
all other negative masses are retained for the solve or invalidate it.
No clipping, regularization or fallback thresholding is allowed.

At every query save x, x/delta, g, g/delta, all group masses, F, physical
step, prefix sum, residual x-g, complete physical/normalized normals,
stationarity, complementarity, feasibility/reconstruction errors, core
candidate masses, strict/tolerance supports, signed norm-defect terms,
norm identity residual, pivot counts and all counters. Stable F is
||g||^2/2+<x-g,g>, or ||x||^2/2 in a freshly checked core. Also save the
alternate dot-product formula and relative discrepancy; never subtract
two large squared norms to obtain F. Save negative-excess/value flags;
negative F beyond KKT_tol*delta^2 or excess below
-KKT_tol*delta^2*(1+||X||) invalidates. Check ||g||^2<=delta^2*(1+10KKT_tol).

Nonfinite saved scalars invalidate. Count underflows in all dot-product
multiplications and GD products, all finite saved scalar checks, and
saved subnormals; record Decimal context flags. This detection is not
exhaustive binary arithmetic tracing or an error enclosure. Full m2
vertices, states, chosen weights and all60 face candidates are retained.
No full-space diagnostics are sampled: beyond m2 the exact symmetry
reduction covers all leaves through all m+1 normals. Raw logs include
every query including the terminal projection. Both trajectories and
every internal projection, pivot, support/KKT check and solve are counted.

Only registered cases evaluate Huber=1/(1+2S),
B_lower=4lambda^(m-1), B_upper=4lambda^m, and their V ratios/products.
No new benchmark DP, complete-complex optimization, external PEP/SDP,
stepsize optimizer, installations, consult or subagents. A B-upper product
above1 gives no success; a B-lower product below1 gives no failure.
Finite values establish no eventual asymptotic or all-word claim.

Source, this plan, derivation, model, precision, tolerances and branch
rule are hashed into FROZEN.json before job1. Every reservation checks
them. Any sole authorized correction preserves the original sources and
records its changes and new hash before using semantic slot6.
After closure only analytic interpretation and reading/copying of frozen
records are permitted. Publish the complete plan, report, raw manifest,
and CLOSED canonical ledger, then end this round.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002853 -->
# Scaled centered tree: six-job discovery report, max3 round55

All six authorized jobs passed their frozen numerical checks. The best
registered m8 discovery case is zeta4, with V=1.98636964600805e-5; the
selected m10 case has V=2.78888925449087e-6. Their accepted benchmark
UPPER products are about0.09169 and0.07503, respectively, both below1.
The independent80-decimal-digit m8 run confirms the selected value and
every core/support classification. This is finite numerical evidence,
not an interval proof, a verifier acceptance or an eventual-family result.
The original unrestricted Goal remains OPEN.

The allocation is CLOSED:6 charged jobs,48 accounted seconds, no failed
case, no correction, no further evaluations, no formal call, no renewal.
Authority is TASK.md and master_guidance6216f7ee407cd7e9. The full shared
preregistration is finding850614a96145f4a0; PLAN.md, DERIVATION.md and
both sources were frozen before the first mathematical import and their
hashes remained unchanged. Completed baseline54, ceiling55, attempted
ceiling56; the outer loop records actual completion after worker return.

## Actual model, radius and calibration

The original enclosing-m word H_m, Haar tree weights and unscaled unit
all-left leaf start are unchanged. The gradient body is the CENTERED
full tree-leaf simplex multiplied by delta=zeta/(1+2S_m). It has no
carrier coordinate. The self-contained joint draft DERIVATION.md gives

  grad F_scaled(x)=delta*P_(K_m)(x/delta),
  sharp global L=1, argmin F_scaled={0}, true R^2=1,
  ||grad F_scaled||<=delta, V=2F_scaled(x_n).

The projection cap is attained along the outer all-left ray. The shape
is fixed and its size relative to x0 really changes; the old unit-body
pilot would have to start at v_left/delta to be a coordinate equivalent.
No physical step or smoothness normalization was changed. The projection
uses the true Haar metric, with all original full-leaf normals recovered
from the exact spine symmetry. All new analytic statements remain part
of this UNVERIFIED joint draft; they are not accepted premises.

For m2,zeta1, the independent four-leaf full-space and reduced trajectories
agree at every query with zero reported x,g,F discrepancy. Their maximum
deviation from the independently evaluated radial x formula is
1.1102230246251565e-16; the radial F discrepancy and terminal Huber
discrepancy are2.7755575615628914e-17. The full calibration enumerates
all15 nonempty full faces at each of four queries. Both independently
evolved trajectories stay exterior on the all-left ray. Its exact draft
formula is V=2delta-delta^2(1+2S), valid when delta<=1/(1+S); at zeta1
this equals the accepted Huber value1/(1+2S). This is a check of the
same scaled object, with its genuine closest radius, not a borrowed
normalization. Interior and changing-face behavior are covered by the
discovery cases and independent precision check, not by this calibration.

## Registered terminal comparisons

All numbers in this table were computed INSIDE the six reserved jobs.
The raw records carry full precision and every queried state. The
accepted original ALL-split bounds are
B_lower=4(1+sqrt2)^(m-1) and B_upper=4(1+sqrt2)^m from fact
04219554e41d9750, proof(7.1) and(9.3). The same-word Huber comparison
is1/(1+2S_m), from b98bb807d514e80d. No benchmark DP was performed.

| Job/case | V=2F/(LR^2) | V/Huber | B_lower V | B_upper V |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 1: m2,zeta1 calibration | 0.11692781861826862 | 0.9999999999999998 | 1.1291549021077014 | 2.7260210786884773 |
| 2: m8,zeta4 | 1.9863696460080494e-5 | 0.0585025352049771 | 0.03797955385434773 | 0.09169075400804563 |
| 3: m8,zeta8 | 7.048684447545368e-34 | 2.0759777057106038e-30 | 1.3477143648255677e-30 | 3.253670297766926e-30 |
| 4: m8,zeta16 | 2.256944096811907e-34 | 6.647149071410487e-31 | 4.315295999583185e-31 | 1.0418046127848086e-30 |
| 5: m10,zeta4 | 2.788889254490865e-6 | 0.053055200832781486 | 0.03107938585619392 | 0.0750322748442499 |
| 6: m8,zeta4,80 decimal digits | 1.98636964600806094447e-5 | 0.05850253520497743995 | 0.03797955385434796556 | 0.09169075400804621462 |

The rule selected zeta4 solely because job2 has the largest valid m8 V;
all three discovery cases passed. That choice was fixed for both jobs5/6.
At these noncalibration cases, even B_upper*V is below1, supplying
numerical evidence of being below1/B_n. A B-upper product above1 alone
would establish no success; a B-lower product below1 alone would establish
no failure. The calibration's B-lower product exceeds1, but it is one
small-horizon Huber case and supplies no useful eventual conclusion.
No complete-complex optimum or unrestricted worst-case upper bound is
estimated. The small terminal values for zeta8/16 received no independent
precision rerun; the single precision slot was correctly spent on zeta4.

## Actual face and core behavior

| Case | Exterior queries | Core queries | Distinct supports |
|---|---|---|---:|
| m2,zeta1 | all0..3 | none | 1 |
| m8,zeta4, both precisions | all0..255 | none | 5 |
| m8,zeta8 | 0..89 | 90..255 | 9 |
| m8,zeta16 | 0..48 and50..84 | 49 and85..255 | 14 |
| m10,zeta4 | all0..1023 | none | 7 |

The selected m8,zeta4 support sequence changes at exactly these queries:
0:{8};128:{0};160:{1};168:{0};172:{0,1};176:{1};177:{0,1};205:{0,1,2}.
Every transition agrees in the independent high-precision trajectory.
The all-left gradient lasts through the largest bridge. After that bridge,
the deep state is retained, and the terminal gradient has only its first
three coordinates nonzero. Its final masses are approximately
(0.51391272428017,0.28083067225172,0.20525660346811,0,...,0).
In particular avoiding terminal core collapse did not yield a competitive
payoff in this finite case.

At m10,zeta4 the first switch is likewise at query512, after the largest
bridge. Later the trajectory uses {2} at800 and {0,2} at803/805; holes
in the ordered support remain possible. It eventually uses {0,1,2}.
Both selected suffixes use only these first three groups, so their
depth>=3 state coordinates remain at their post-bridge values. This is
observed across every saved query, not an asserted all-depth theorem.

At m8,zeta16 query49 is interior with positive core masses, but its
physical step1.875 produces a noncore query50. The last unconstrained
mass there is approximately-0.1920982814. Its residual squared norm is
4.25086570758147e-7. A first-core-entry shortcut would erase a real part
of this trajectory. Later core suffixes explain the tiny zeta8/16 values,
but no permanent-core rule for untested inputs is inferred.

ANALYTIC_INTERPRETATION.md adds one joint symbolic observation after
closure, with no new numerical work. At zeta4, for every m>=2 the entire
first child and its largest bridge have gradient delta*v_left, with
x_(2^(m-1))=-(2b-1)v_left/(1+2S), where b is that largest bridge. For
every m>=4 the immediate next gradient is exactly delta*b_0, the
right-root subtree average. The proof checks all original normals. It
explains the observed first switch but leaves the whole suffix law and
asymptotic payoff unproved. The same note gives the conditional core
suffix identity while explicitly retaining its hypotheses.

## Precision, residuals and diagnostic scope

Primary arithmetic actually has53 binary significand bits. Job6 uses
80 significant decimal digits, recomputes lambda,c,a,weights,S,delta and
every state independently, and changes the affine system, solver, starting
vertex, entering-vertex rule and inverse core formula. It is not a face
sequence replay. The primary selected case takes134 pivots; precision
takes2200, including1033 deletions. Their final m8 values differ by
5.81184443588907e-15 relatively. Maximum physical state discrepancy is
3.2886143942049e-15; gradient3.08322300307797e-15; F5.03620462782711e-19;
group masses2.91376090144218e-12. Core and support disagreements: ZERO.

The greatest positive normalized normal residual is4.263256414560601e-14
in m10; the greatest physical positive normal residual is
1.3552527156068805e-20 in m8,zeta4. Maximum primary feasibility/sum
error is1.1102230246251565e-16. Maximum primary reconstruction error is
4.297457313094944e-19. Every selected projection passes its registered
normal, stationarity, complementarity, feasibility and reconstruction
checks, both in normalized and physical coordinates. The precision
normal maxima are2e-78 normalized and2.6e-84 physical; maximum
complementarity is about1.149e-78 and reconstruction2e-83.

Maximum per-projection pivots are0,2,7,7,2,16 in job order, all below
4(m+1)^2. The1033 precision boundary zero assignments implement first
blocking constraints, with saved preassignment discrepancies at most
5e-80. No arbitrary mass clipping was used. No finite-state, pivot,
solve-residual, time or gradient-cap guard failed. There are no negative
F or excess flags in any target trajectory, no detected scalar underflows,
and no saved subnormal outputs. Decimal Overflow/Underflow/Subnormal/
InvalidOperation flags are false; Inexact and Rounded are true, as
expected for finite-precision roots and arithmetic.

F was evaluated by ||g||^2/2+<x-g,g>, and by ||x||^2/2 at freshly checked
core states. It was never derived by subtracting large squared norms.
The largest primary discrepancy against the alternate dot-product value
is about4.063e-16 relatively. The forward norm-identity diagnostic can
have substantial cancellation at large physical bridges: its largest
absolute residual is3.521433145081687e-11 in m10. That diagnostic was
not used to obtain F or evolve the physical state. The precision version
has maximum norm-identity residual6.2025e-76.

Underflow detection covers all dot-product multiplications, explicit GD
products, finite saved scalars and Decimal flags. It is not exhaustive
tracing of binary operations or an interval error analysis. All queried
states are saved; no full-space diagnostic sampling is disguised as a
complete full-space computation. Only m2 was projected independently
in the original full space; other depths use the exact symmetry reduction.

## Budget closure and public records

The canonical ledger is
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/scaled_tree_projection_20261001_budget.json.
It has status closed_jobs_exhausted, closure CLOSED, no in-flight job,
six zero return codes,48 charged seconds and no unused or renewed slot.
The full8s was reserved before every mathematical import; each whole
runner invocation had external8s timeout enforcement. No retry occurred.
Main guard1083->1131 is a conservative reservation, not measured runtime.

| Job | Evaluator through print, seconds | Child transport, seconds | Runner before final ledger, seconds | Charge |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 1 | 0.037188381 | 0.067330462 | 0.091489906 | 8 |
| 2 | 0.131249177 | 0.167454989 | 0.191168867 | 8 |
| 3 | 0.090464981 | 0.117648946 | 0.142654954 | 8 |

| 5 | 0.377989614 | 0.419350247 | 0.450275706 | 8 |
| 6 | 0.353791545 | 0.418512233 | 0.449323512 | 8 |

Evaluator through-print sum1.0891084030154161s; child transport
sum1.3585019960009959s; runner-before-final-ledger sum1.5168640119809425s.
These times are NESTED, not additive; none replaces the full48s charge.

Actual internal work:2052 reduced projection calls plus4 full calls;
60 full calibration face candidates;3200 active pivots (2167 additions,
1033 deletions);2211 linear solves;5796 factorization/elimination pivots;
9142 support checks;79307 vertex-normal checks;41240 complementarity
checks;4104 core tests,338 core identities;222372 finite scalar checks.
All2052 reduced query records and four independent full states are saved,
including each terminal gradient. READBACK.json confirms complete ordered
query indices and records the original stdout/stderr without modification.

RAW_MANIFEST.json lists absolute paths, sizes and SHA256 identities for
the source, preregistration, complete gzip raw data, reservations,
summaries, original stdout/stderr, reports and CLOSED ledger. CLOSURE.json
records the resource totals. Frozen sources and all snapshotted old

# Two actual growing tree-simplex projection bodies

Status: COMPLETE JOINT RESEARCH DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. Worker max, round53.
Assignment TASK.md and master_guidance8e797e97b78dcebd authorize ONE
analytical round after completed52/attempted53, ceilings53/54. No formal
verification or fact_submit is authorized or performed. No new fact_id.

Two concrete bodies are selected, of the permitted maximum three. The
first has a rigorous scoped full-word obstruction. The second has actual
face changes, an explicit all-query state rule and a priced full-word
interface, but no proved useful terminal payoff. A representation or
first-H2 calculation is not claimed to solve that interface. All new
ingredients are proved jointly here; earlier unverified findings are
awareness only. No mathematical evaluator, numerical run, solver, DP
execution, machine algebra, replay, installation, consult, or subagent
was used.

## 1. Original words and complete reference scopes

Throughout fix an integer m>=128 and set

 lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/m, c=lambda(1+1/m), a=beta/c,
 H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
 N=2^m, n=N-1, h=H_m, S=sum_(i=0)^(n-1)h_i.

Every child retains these SAME enclosing-m parameters. In particular
h_0=1+a, h_1=1+beta, h_2=1+a. Counting each level gives

 1+S=N+(beta/c)(c^m-N)/(c-2)
     =(1-zeta)N+zeta*c^m<c^m,
 zeta=beta/[c(c-2)] in(0,1).                         (1.1)

Indeed c>lambda and lambda(lambda-2)=1, whereas beta<1.
For i>=0 let s2(i) be the number of ones in its binary expansion, with
s2(0)=0, and define the signed original prefix product

 Pi_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t)=(-1)^i beta^i c^(-s2(i)). (1.2)

To prove the identity, the original recursive word has
h_t-1=beta*c^(v2(t+1)-1), where v2(k) is the exponent of2 in k.
Counting multiples of powers of2 shows
sum_(k=1)^i v2(k)=sum_(j>=1)floor(i/2^j)=i-s2(i): for a binary
digit at position j its contribution to the floor sum is2^j-1.
Multiplication gives (1.2), including i=0. Consequently

 |Pi_i|<=a for 1<=i<=n,
 Qunit:=Pi_n^2=beta^(2n)c^(-2m).                    (1.3)

For a body K used below, the actual project function is f=L F_K and
the actual iterates obey x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i grad F_K(x_i). Define

 V_K=2F_K(x_n)/R_K^2,
 R_K=dist(x_0,argmin F_K).                           (1.4)

This is the fixed W=2R normalization. The sharp global smoothness of
each selected F_K is1, so no smaller local constant is substituted.

The accepted reference facts used are the following.

* b98bb807d514e80d: the COMPLETE single-cut functional

 K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2S), max_(0<=j<n)
  (h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+sum_(t<j)h_t)^2(1+2sum_(t>j)h_t)]}.

  It also specifies the ORIGINAL all-split recurrences

 C_0=B_0=1,
 C_t=max_(0<=k<t)Omega(C_k,C_(t-k-1)),
 B_t=max_(0<=k<t)G(C_k,B_(t-k-1)),
 Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,
 G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,
 U_t^ZJ=1/B_t.

* 1fca3e5dd555cb4a: F_ray_n(h) is its maximum over ALL feasible
  chronological checkpoint subsets, with the original retained offsets,
  value areas, same-ray monotonicity conditions and terminal q optimization.
  Its complete class contains K_n(h). No restricted checkpoint list is
  substituted here. The only comparison needed below is

 Huber(h):=1/(1+2S)<=K_n(h)<=F_ray_n(h).              (1.5)

* dfde2fa326c908bf: Poly_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z),
  xi_i=Poly_i(z), gamma_i=z Poly_i(z), together with the zero optimum.
  The FULL legal domain D_h consists of all z=alpha+i beta_complex
  such that (alpha-|z|^2) Energy_C>=2|beta_complex Area_C| for EVERY
  directed simple cycle of these original query indices and optimum;
  Energy_C=sum|xi_i-xi_j|^2 and
  Area_C=sum Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i) on the oriented edges j->i.
  If ell_i is the maximal path weight from i to the optimum for weights
  <gamma_j,xi_i-xi_j>+||gamma_i-gamma_j||^2/2 on j->i, its exact maximal
  gap is Gap_h(z)=-2ell_n. Write

 Cfull(h)=sup_(z in D_h)Gap_h(z).

  At z=1 all cycle conditions hold, and the upper and lower gap bounds
  of that accepted fact coincide. Hence

 Cfull(h)>=Gap_h(1)=Qunit.                           (1.6)

  This comparison uses a point in the FULL domain, not a claim that a
  lens or real slice exhausts it.

* 04219554e41d9750, proof(9.3), gives for the same original recurrence
  B_t<=4(t+1)^(log_2 lambda), in particular B_n<=4lambda^m. Its full
  statement and the proof's indices were read directly from the fact
  file. No new recurrence computation or finite-H6 transfer is used.

Thus Qunit<c^(-2m)<1/(2c^m)<Huber(h), by (1.1) and c^m>2.

## 2. Projection function, smoothness, minimum, and all original rows

Let K be any nonempty compact convex subset of a Euclidean space with
0 in K. Define the SAME finite function at every query by

 F_K(x)=max_(g in K){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.

Completing the square shows that its unique maximizing vector is the
Euclidean projection g=P_K(x). Existence follows from compactness and
uniqueness from strict convexity of squared distance. The exact normal
condition is

 <x-g,v-g><=0 for every v in K.                     (2.1)

Necessity follows by differentiating squared distance on the segment
from g to v. Sufficiency follows by expanding ||x-v||^2-||x-g||^2.
Adding (2.1) for two inputs proves
<x-x',g-g'>=||g-g'||^2, hence ||g-g'||<=||x-x'||.
The maximizing formulas at x and x+d give

 0<=F_K(x+d)-F_K(x)-<g,d><=<P_K(x+d)-g,d><=||d||^2.

Therefore F_K is finite, convex, differentiable, and grad F_K=P_K is
globally1-Lipschitz. Also

 2F_K(x)=||x||^2-||x-P_K(x)||^2.                    (2.2)

Its minimum is0, and its WHOLE minimizer set is

 K^polar={x:<x,v><=0 for all v in K}.                (2.3)

One direction follows directly from the maximizing formula. In the other
direction, if <x,v>>0 for some v in K, a sufficiently small positive
multiple epsilon*v is in K and has positive objective. This proves
that x is not a minimizer. It also proves (2.3) without identifying the
minimum with an arbitrarily selected point.

Write y_i=x_i-g_i, f_i=F_K(x_i), with g_i=P_K(x_i). The original
interpolation convention in accepted1252b8823bb5a290 is

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Substitution of f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 gives the exact identity

 Q_ij=<y_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                            (2.4)

The inequality is (2.1). It holds for ALL directed pairs, however
distant, and with any actual minimizer sample g_*=0,f_*=0. No sampled
values, remote rows, or terminal rows are fitted separately.

Both bodies below have nonempty Euclidean interior in their stated
ambient space. On their interiors P_K(x)=x, so their global smoothness
constant1 is sharp.

## 3. The explicit growing tree and exact symmetry reduction

Set

 r_tree=2/c, Zgeom=sum_(j=0)^(m-1)r_tree^j,
 w_j=r_tree^j/Zgeom, 0<=j<m.                        (3.1)

Use one orthonormal coordinate e_v for every binary word v of length
less than m. For a leaf s=(s_1,...,s_m) define

 a_s=sum_(j=0)^(m-1)sqrt(w_j)(-1)^(s_(j+1))e_(s_1...s_j).
                                                                    (3.2)

There are N leaves and N-1 coordinates. Each ||a_s||=1 and sum_s a_s=0.
Distinct coordinate rows of the feature matrix are orthogonal: disjoint
supports give zero, and every proper descendant row cancels within an
ancestor's constant-sign region. Every row has positive squared norm.
Thus the matrix has rank N-1, its kernel is exactly the constant leaf
vector, and the N features are affinely independent. In particular0 is
in the interior of their simplex. If two leaves first diverge at depth k,

 <a_s,a_t>=sum_(j<k)w_j-w_k.                       (3.3)

Let s_left=0^m, write a_left=a_(s_left), and let e_root=e_empty be the
root coordinate. The selected initial points have coordinates only on its
spine, and possibly one common carrier. The group of tree automorphisms
within every off-spine subtree acts orthogonally, fixes these points,
and permutes the leaf features. Projection onto either selected body
commutes with this group by uniqueness. Every non-spine coordinate of a
fixed vector is zero: swapping the children of its node reverses that
coordinate. The GD update therefore preserves this subspace at EVERY
original physical query, not just at word checkpoints.

Average the features in each first-right-turn group k, 0<=k<m, and
retain the all-left leaf as group m. Their spine vectors are

 b_k=sum_(j<k)sqrt(w_j)e_(0^j)-sqrt(w_k)e_(0^k), k<m,
 b_m=sum_(j<m)sqrt(w_j)e_(0^j).                     (3.4)

An off-spine group average has exactly these coordinates, since every
later coordinate cancels. Averaging any convex representation over the
group shows that the centered simplex's fixed section is EXACTLY
conv{b_0,...,b_m}. With a carrier, its fixed section is the convex hull
of0 and the corresponding carrier plus each b_k.

Here is an explicit constructive active-set rule, valid for every state
in this section, including every future state below. For a finite listed
simplex with vertices v_k, consider a nonempty subset I of its vertices.
Choose k0 in I, form the edge columns E_I=(v_k-v_k0)_(k in I\{k0}),
and set

 t_I=(E_I^T E_I)^(-1)E_I^T(x-v_k0),
 g_I=v_k0+E_I t_I.                                (3.5)

For a singleton take g_I=v_k0. The edge Gram matrix is positive definite
by affine independence. Every entry is specified by (3.1),(3.4) and the
chosen carrier; no unknown feature or Gram fitting is present. Retain a
subset exactly when its barycentric coefficients t_I and1-sum t_I are
nonnegative and

 <x-g_I,v_l-g_I><=0 for every listed vertex l.       (3.6)

At least one subset qualifies: use the positive barycentric support of
the true projection. Its within-face first derivatives vanish, giving
(3.5); its remaining inequalities are (2.1). Conversely any qualifying
subset gives the unique projection by (2.1). Degenerate supports can
repeat the same answer but cannot give different answers. Equations
(3.5),(3.6) are a finite symbolic characterization; no enumeration or
numerical evaluation was run. They specify all subsequent physical GD
queries by x_next=x-h*g and their values by the SAME F_K. The quantitative
conclusions below require additional analysis beyond this rule.

## 4. Candidate one: a common carrier stays in a screened cone regime

Add an orthogonal unit coordinate e_car and put

 kappa=sqrt(w_0/3), v_s=kappa e_car+a_s,
 K_car=conv({0} union {v_s}),
 C_car={sum_s t_s v_s:t_s>=0},
 v_left=v_(s_left), x_0=v_left.                     (4.1)

The v_s are linearly independent: a zero combination first has coefficient
sum0 by its carrier coordinate, then all coefficients zero by affine
independence of the a_s. Hence K_car has full dimension N and C_car is
a closed convex cone (an invertible linear image of the nonnegative
orthant). A vector in C_car has a unique total mass

 mass(g)=g_car/kappa=sum_s t_s.

It belongs to K_car exactly when mass(g)<=1. By (2.3), the whole minimum
set is C_car^polar. Because x_0 is in C_car, for any z in this polar
<x_0,z><=0, and ||x_0-z||^2>=||x_0||^2. Thus0 is the actual closest
minimum and the EXACT paid radius is

 R_car^2=1+kappa^2.                                 (4.2)

The first gradient is g_0=x_0, so x_1=-a x_0. Define the uniform
right-root subtree average

 u_R=kappa e_car-sqrt(w_0)e_root.

The actual next gradient is

 g_1=(a/2)u_R,      ||g_1||=a*kappa.                 (4.3)

Here is a full normal check. Set y_1=x_1-g_1. For every leaf whose
first bit is1, <y_1,v_s>=0, because
<v_left,v_s>=-2w_0/3 and <u_R,v_s>=4w_0/3. For a different leaf
first diverging from s_left at depth k>=1,

 <y_1,v_s>=-a(sum_(j<k)w_j-w_k)<0,

since w_k<=w_1<w_0. For s=s_left the inner product is -a<0.
Thus y_1 is in C_car^polar and <y_1,g_1>=0. For any v in C_car,
<y_1,v-g_1><=0, proving P_C_car(x_1)=g_1. Moreover its mass is
a/2<1, so (4.3) is also P_K_car(x_1). The mixture is origin plus
ALL right-root leaves, uniformly within that subtree. It is derived,
not prescribed. The noncore state y_1 is nonzero; for example every
deeper left-spine coordinate is -a sqrt(w_j).

We reproduce the precise cone mapping needed for the full word, rather
than using the earlier unverified C37 finding as a premise. For any
closed convex cone C and u=P_C(x), put z=x-u. Applying (2.1) to0 and2u
gives <z,u>=0 and z in C^polar. Conversely
<u,w-z><=0 for all w in C^polar, so z=P_(C^polar)(x). Therefore

 ||P_C(x)||=dist(x,C^polar),
 F_C(x)=||P_C(x)||^2/2,
 dist(x-hP_C(x),C^polar)<=|1-h|dist(x,C^polar).      (4.4)

The last bound uses the same old point z in the next distance minimization,
since x-hu=z+(1-h)u. The cone maximum defining F_C is finite by completing
the square, even though C itself is unbounded.

Run the cone dynamics from the actual x_1 and denote its projection norms
by d_i. By (4.3),(4.4),(1.2), for every i>=1,

 d_i<=a*kappa*product_(1<=t<i)|1-h_t|
     =kappa |Pi_i|<=a*kappa<kappa.                  (4.5)

Each cone projection has mass at most d_i/kappa<1. It is therefore in
K_car and is ALSO its projection. Induction identifies the entire cone
trajectory with the entire actual bounded-body trajectory. In particular
every original physical query and the terminal value come from the SAME
F_K_car. No changed function is substituted after query1. Formula (3.5)
with the carrier vertices, or the proved cone normal condition, selects
all faces without any cancellation/extinction requirement.

This trajectory has another genuine face change within the first H2.
At x_2=x_1-(1+beta)g_1, every right-root leaf has inner product
-2a beta w_0/3<0, whereas a leaf first diverging at depth1 has

 <x_2,v_s>=a w_0(r_tree-1+beta/3)>0.               (4.6)

For the last strict inequality it suffices to prove
6m^2>lambda(2m+1)(m+1). Using lambda<5/2 reduces this to
m^2>(15/2)m+5/2, which holds at m=8 and increases thereafter.
Some small positive multiple of this left vertex has positive objective,
so g_2 is nonzero. A mixture supported solely on the right-root leaves
and origin has nonpositive linear term and cannot attain this positive
objective. Thus g_2 has positive left-root mass. No assertion is made
that the right-root mass vanishes or that a fixed next face persists.

Despite these changing mixtures, (4.5) yields a COMPLETE full-word screen:

 0<=V_car<=kappa^2/(1+kappa^2)*Qunit
          =w_0/(3+w_0)*beta^(2n)c^(-2m).            (4.7)

Since 0<w_0/(3+w_0)<1, equations(1.5),(1.6) give the strict SAME-word
comparisons

 V_car<Qunit<Huber(h)<=K_n(h)<=F_ray_n(h),
 V_car<Cfull(h),
 B_n V_car<=4*w_0/(3+w_0)*(lambda/c^2)^m
                      *beta^(2n) ->0.              (4.8)

For the limit, lambda/c^2<1/lambda<1. Thus this entire explicit
unbounded-m family is weak, even though its faces change and its normal
state is retained. It has been mapped to the previously screened
squared-distance/cone class with its TRUE minimum distance. The radial
cap has zero multiplier at x_0 and is strictly inactive at every later
projection. Per the assignment, this candidate stops here. This is not
an upper bound for arbitrary initial points of K_car, other carrier
sizes, other truncations, or general simplices.

## 5. Candidate two: remove the carrier and retain the actual mixtures

Select the centered body with no carrier

 K_ctr=conv{a_s:s in {0,1}^m}, x_0=a_(s_left).        (5.1)

Adding an explicit origin to this convex hull changes nothing. Section3
shows0 is an interior point, so (2.3) gives the UNIQUE minimum0 and

 R_ctr^2=1.                                        (5.2)

The cone generated by K_ctr is the whole ambient space. At query1 the
finite boundary really matters, as demonstrated below, so the cone
mapping from Section4 cannot be carried over.

Write the actual spine coordinates as x_(i,j)=sqrt(w_j) z_(i,j).
If p_(i,k) are the unique barycentric masses of g_i in the vertices b_k,
define q_(i,j)=sum_(k>=j)p_(i,k). Then q_(i,0)=1 and
1=q_(i,0)>=q_(i,1)>=...>=q_(i,m)>=0, and

 d_(i,j)=2q_(i,j+1)-q_(i,j),
 g_(i,j)=sqrt(w_j)d_(i,j),
 z_(i+1,j)=z_(i,j)-h_i d_(i,j), z_(0,j)=1.          (5.3)

At every query the masses are exactly selected by (3.5),(3.6) with b_k.
Equivalently they uniquely minimize
sum_(j<m)w_j[z_(i,j)-(2q_(j+1)-q_j)]^2 on the displayed monotone
q simplex. The constructive rule, not this optimization notation alone,
specifies the answer. The exact values and normalized terminal score are

 F_K_ctr(x_i)=sum_(j<m)w_j[z_(i,j)d_(i,j)-d_(i,j)^2/2],
 V_ctr=sum_(j<m)w_j[2z_(n,j)d_(n,j)-d_(n,j)^2].      (5.4)

These are a persistent m-dimensional state and every original physical
step. There is no smaller reset state or new child clock.

## 6. Actual first-H2 projections for the centered body

Define for positive integers J

 Dsp_J=sum_(j=0)^(J-1)(2c)^j,
 sigma_3=(7a-1)/Dsp_3,
 sigma_4=(15a-2-beta)/Dsp_4.                        (6.1)

For m>=128 the elementary bounds

 2/5<a<5/12, 12/5<c<5/2, 127/128<=beta<1,
 1/20<sigma_3<1/15, 1/52<sigma_4<1/40              (6.2)

hold. The bounds on a,c follow from 12/5<lambda<29/12 and m>=128.
For sigma_3 its numerator is between9/5 and23/12, and its denominator
is between721/25 and31; in particular
9/155>1/20 and (23/12)/(721/25)=575/8652<1/15.
For sigma_4 the numerator is greater than3 and less than10/3; its
denominator is less than156 and greater than17429/125>400/3.

Initially g_0=x_0 and z_(1,j)=-a for EVERY depth. The exact projection
at query1 is

 d_(1,j)=-a+sigma_3 c^j, j=0,1,2,
 d_(1,j)=0, j>=3.                                  (6.3)

Its positive masses are exactly exit groups0,1,2. To verify this, take

 q_(1,1)=(1-a+sigma_3)/2,
 q_(1,2)=a-sigma_3 c^2, q_(1,3)=...=q_(1,m)=0.

The relation q_(1,3)=0 is equivalent to the definition of sigma_3,
and (5.3) gives (6.3). Moreover

 7/24<q_(1,1)<1/3, 0<q_(1,2)<1/20.

For the second bounds its numerator over Dsp_3 is
a(1+2c)+(1-3a)c^2. The numerator is at least
58/25-25/16=303/400>0, and at most337/250;
division by Dsp_3>721/25 gives an upper bound337/7210<1/20.
Consequently p_0=1-q_1,p_1=q_1-q_2,p_2=q_2 are all positive.

The residual y_1 has scaled coefficients -sigma_3 c^j for j<3
and -a for j>=3. Because r_tree*c=2, its inner product with b_0,b_1,b_2
is the common value w_0 sigma_3. Its inner product with b_3 is
w_0[-7sigma_3+a r_tree^3]. This is strictly smaller, because
sigma_3>a/c^3: the left side exceeds1/20 and a/c^3<1/30.
For subsequent first-exit vertices the residual inner products strictly
decrease: an increment inside the constant negative tail is
-a w_k(2-r_tree)<0. The final all-left vertex also has a smaller value
than its immediately preceding exit vertex, by -2a w_(m-1)<0.
Thus every normal condition(3.6) holds and the displayed projection is
the actual unique one.

The next ACTUAL state, after the original h_1=1+beta, is

 z_(2,j)=a beta-(1+beta)sigma_3 c^j, j=0,1,2,
 z_(2,j)=-a, j>=3.                                 (6.4)

Its next projection has positive masses exactly on exit groups0,1,2,3:

 d_(2,j)=z_(2,j)+sigma_4 c^j, 0<=j<4,
 d_(2,j)=0, j>=4.                                  (6.5)

To verify, note
1+sum_(j<4)2^j z_(2,j)=2+beta-15a,
so the definition of sigma_4 gives q_(2,4)=0. Define earlier q by
q_(2,0)=1 and q_(2,j+1)=(q_(2,j)+d_(2,j))/2.
All four barycentric masses are strictly positive, as follows.
The bounds(6.2) give

 1/4<d_(2,0)<1/2, 0<d_(2,1)<1/4,
 d_(2,2)=-a+(1+beta)q_(1,2)+sigma_4 c^2<-23/160<0,
 d_(2,3)=-a+sigma_4 c^3<-3/320<0.

For completeness, the lower bounds on the first two entries follow
from 127/320-2/15>1/4 and127/320-1/3>0. Their upper bounds follow
from5/12+1/40<1/2 and
5/12+1/16-(255/128)(1/20)(12/5)<1/4.
It follows that 5/8<q_(2,1)<3/4 and
p_1=(q_(2,1)-d_(2,1))/2>0, while p_0=1-q_(2,1)>0.
Since q_(2,4)=0, q_(2,3)=-d_(2,3)>0 and
p_2=q_(2,2)-q_(2,3)=q_(2,3)-d_(2,2)>0; finally p_3=q_(2,3)>0.

The residual at query2 has scaled coefficients -sigma_4 c^j for j<4
and -a thereafter. Its values on the four active vertices are all
w_0 sigma_4. The first inactive normal inequality is exactly
sigma_4>=a/c^4, which is strict because sigma_4>1/52 and
a/c^4<1/75. The negative tail then makes all further normal values
strictly decrease, with the same last-leaf check. This proves(6.5).

The common active normal values also give the exact actual conjugate
excesses cex_1=<y_1,g_1>=w_0 sigma_3>0 and
cex_2=<y_2,g_2>=w_0 sigma_4>0. Thus the values from the SAME function are

 F_K_ctr(x_0)=1/2,
 F_K_ctr(x_1)=(1/2)sum_(j<3)w_j(-a+sigma_3 c^j)^2+w_0 sigma_3,
 F_K_ctr(x_2)=(1/2)sum_(j<4)w_j d_(2,j)^2+w_0 sigma_4.

No free height or separate value assignment is used. The residuals are
nonzero at both queries, including their retained deep components.

The third physical update in the first H2 is therefore exactly

 z_(3,j)=z_(2,j)-(1+a)d_(2,j).                       (6.6)

For every j>=4 it is STILL -a. Equations(6.3)-(6.6) are actual
projections and updates; they do not prescribe a rank-one law. In
particular the face changes between queries1 and2, and the full deep
noncore state is retained. At query3 and every subsequent query the
all-support rule(3.5),(3.6),(5.3) applies. No assertion that these four
groups remain active is made.

## 7. Two direct continuation attempts and their limits

The first attempt was to propagate a fixed exposed face, or treat a
visit to the quadratic core as permanent. Both shortcuts fail on these
very data. Equations(6.3),(6.5) give different positive supports at
queries1 and2. Also x_0 lies in K_ctr, but x_1=-a a_left does NOT.
Its unique full-leaf barycentric coefficients are

 p_s=(1+a)/N for s!=s_left,
 p_(s_left)=(1+a)/N-a<0.                            (7.1)

The strict inequality follows from a>2/5 and N>=4. Consequently
P_K_ctr(x_1)!=x_1. An initial core visit already fails to persist through
the next original step. No remote interpolation defect occurs: (2.4)
holds, and the true projection supplies the required repair automatically.

The second attempt was to simplify the full simplex projection by
thresholding its unconstrained leaf masses with the ordinary Euclidean
probability-simplex formula. This is false for the selected Haar metric.
Applying that threshold formula to (7.1) would give mass1/(N-1) on all
non-left leaves, mass0 on the left leaf, and hence

 g_wrong=-a_left/(N-1).

All those non-left leaves would have positive weights, so their normal
inner products with y_wrong=x_1-g_wrong must be identical at a genuine
projection. The values for leaves first diverging at depths0 and1 differ
by

 (-a+1/(N-1))*(2w_0-w_1),                          (7.2)

which is nonzero. This directly violates the within-face stationarity
condition. The inverse feature map changes the Euclidean metric; ordinary
thresholding is not invariant under that change of coordinates.

These are concrete failures of two attempted reductions, not a proof
that a competitive centered-tree trajectory is impossible. In particular
there is no proof that the support remains a prefix of exit groups after
query2, and no failure of such a later-only prefix conjecture is asserted.
The full state rule retains arbitrary later supports.

## 8. Full-word accounting and a quantitative conditional screen

The centered candidate has a fully paid initial radius, but its terminal
estimate remains substantive. Here is exact full-word accounting that
retains the noncore state. Set

 y_i=x_i-g_i, cex_i=<y_i,g_i>>=0.

Nonnegativity follows from (2.1) with v=0. Since
x_(i+1)=(1-h_i)g_i+y_i, direct expansion gives

 ||x_(i+1)||^2=(1-h_i)^2||x_i||^2
   +h_i(2-h_i)||y_i||^2-2h_i(h_i-1)cex_i.

None of the Pi_i is zero. Dividing by Pi_(i+1)^2 and telescoping gives
the exact quantitative terminal identity

 V_ctr=Pi_n^2*[1+sum_(i<n)
  {h_i(2-h_i)||y_i||^2-2h_i(h_i-1)cex_i}/Pi_(i+1)^2]
  -||y_n||^2.                                     (8.1)

It uses every original physical query. The bracket is not set to1.
For h_i>=2 both displayed contributions are nonpositive. For 1<h_i<2
the squared-residual contribution can be positive, and its size must
be controlled jointly with the negative terms and the terminal residual.
For this candidate the exact benchmark-matching requirement is

 Pi_n^2*[1+sum_(i<n)
  {h_i(2-h_i)||y_i||^2-2h_i(h_i-1)cex_i}/Pi_(i+1)^2]
  -||y_n||^2 >=1/B_n.                              (8.2)

No inequality(8.2), useful constant fraction of it, or converse for all
m has been proved. Equation(8.1) is an identity, not a lower bound.

A second direct continuation attempt uses a rigorously testable core
criterion instead of assuming persistence. If at an actual power-of-two
query k=2^j with 1<=j<=m-1 one has

 x_k in K_ctr and -x_k/c in K_ctr,                  (8.3)

then every later query is exactly in the core, and

 x_i=(Pi_i/Pi_k)x_k for k<=i<=n,
 V_ctr=||x_k||^2 beta^(2(n-k))c^(2-2m).             (8.4)

Indeed Pi_k=beta^k/c is positive. For even i>=k the ratio Pi_i/Pi_k
lies in[0,1], since s2(i)>=1. For odd i>=k the bit count is at least2
(the only odd positive integer with a single one is1), so the ratio
lies in[-1/c,0]. Convexity and(8.3) put every candidate iterate in K_ctr.
Induction then gives its actual gradient equal to itself at each step.

This criterion has a quantitative full-word consequence. Every gradient
of F_K_ctr has norm at most1, since it is a convex combination of unit
features. Hence ||x_k||<=1+sum_(t<k)h_t<=1+S<c^m. From(8.4), for any
k in the stated range,

 V_ctr<c^2 beta^(2(n-k))<=c^2 exp(-2(n-k)/m),
 B_n V_ctr<4lambda^m c^2 exp[-(2^m-2)/m] ->0.       (8.5)

We used log(1-1/m)<=-1/m and k<=2^(m-1). The limit follows since
2^m/m^2 tends to infinity; for example its ratio at consecutive m is
2(m/(m+1))^2, eventually at least3/2. This is a CONDITIONAL screen for
actual states satisfying(8.3), not a statement that(8.3) occurs. It is
not inferred from a single core membership or from the first-H2 data.

The first direct attempt at a global law failed by the exact face and
core counterexamples. The independent mass-threshold attempt failed
by(7.2). The remaining route is to control the true active-set evolution
in(5.3), or establish(8.3), or obtain a competitive lower estimate in
(8.2). All are genuine all-history estimates. Replacing them by a fixed
label, exact extinction, discarded tail, or an unproved induction would
change the construction.

## 9. Literature and resource record

Search stage: active_program. Program: actual growing tree-simplex
projection dynamics. Missing mechanism: an analytic all-query active-set

arXiv:2204.08153, Proposition2.1. Neither is imported as a black-box result.

The exact primary paper read is Weiran Wang and Miguel A. Carreira-Perpinan,
"Projection onto the probability simplex: An efficient algorithm with a
simple proof, and an application", arXiv:1309.1541, 2013, Sections1-3
and Theorem1 with proof. Its problem is min||p-y||^2/2 subject to p>=0,
sum p=1 in the STANDARD Euclidean probability coordinates. It characterizes
p_i=max(y_i+lambda_threshold,0), with the threshold fixed by the sum and
an explicit sorted-support test. The relevant proof idea is equality of
normal derivatives on the positive support, used directly in(3.5),(3.6)
and to refute the unjustified transfer in(7.2). The paper's scalar
threshold itself does not apply to the non-isometric Haar feature map.

# Centered tree projection: fixed discovery batch, max54

Status: all five authorized jobs completed and passed their registered
numerical checks. Discovery only; NO verifier call, NO fact_submit,
NO new fact. The original exact-profile Goal remains OPEN. This closes
the ONE round allocated after completed53/attempted54; actual completion
of this worker turn will be recorded by the outer loop after return.

Authority: TASK.md and latest master_guidance5017191b944783b7.
Prior centered construction aa3c5e1d91baf405 and shortcut warning
b671acaef6faf790 were recalled, not imported as accepted facts.
Full preregistration is PLAN.md; self-contained analytic prerequisites
are DERIVATION.md. Those files and both source files were hashed before
the first mathematical import and remained unchanged through all jobs.

## 1. Frozen object and actual function

For the enclosing integer m set beta=1-1/m,
c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+1/m), a=beta/c, r_tree=2/c, and
w_j=r_tree^j/sum_(k<m)r_tree^k. Set H_0=() and
H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j); every child keeps the same m.
The physical length is n=2^m-1. Each full leaf feature is the signed
sum of sqrt(w_j) times its orthonormal internal-node coordinates.
K is the centered convex hull of all2^m leaf features, and
F_K(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2).

The actual initial point is the all-left leaf. The joint derivation
proves that the full feature matrix has rank2^m-1, the leaves form a
simplex with0 in its interior, grad F_K=P_K, smoothness1 is sharp,
the unique minimizer is0, and the true initial squared distance is1.
Thus the reported normalized value is V=2F_K(x_n)/(L R^2)=2F_K(x_n).
The actual GD step is x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i P_K(x_i), with L=1.

Off-spine automorphisms leave the trajectory invariant. Their fixed
section is exactly the m-dimensional simplex with vertices b_k: positive
sqrt(w_j) coordinates before the first-right exit k, negative sqrt(w_k)
at k, zero later; b_m is the all-left leaf. A residual in this spine has
the same normal on every full leaf in an exit group as on its b_k.
Every group weight lifts to a uniform nonnegative mixture of that group's
leaves. Thus reduced barycentric feasibility and all m+1 reduced normals
are precisely the original full-body conditions, not a substituted metric.

DERIVATION.md gives the weighted q-coordinate objective and two independent
inverse barycentric formulas. Ordinary probability-simplex thresholding
is not used. Every query receives a fresh core test. A core identity is
used only at that tested input; no suffix is skipped and no permanent
core claim is inferred from an entry time or the two-sided diagnostics.

## 4. Face and core history relevant to a future full-history bound

Every query including the terminal one was evaluated and logged.

|---|---|---|---|
| 6 | 1 through7 | 8 through63 | 6 |

These final segments are checked finite histories, not a permanence
theorem. In particular m8 enters the core at31, leaves at32, enters

48.612488871744068...; the minimum represented core mass is about

The later faces are not always prefixes of exit groups. At m8 query8,

The exact forward identity rederived in DERIVATION.md is

 ||x_(i+1)||^2=(1-h_i)^2||x_i||^2
       +h_i(2-h_i)||y_i||^2-2h_i(h_i-1)<y_i,g_i>.

The largest discrepancy of the iterated forward norm accounting was

Fresh two-sided diagnostics x_k in K and -x_k/c in K passed at sampled

For m8 query64, the smallest displayed mass of -x_k/c is about

# Joint analytic prerequisites (UNVERIFIED, max54)

These are rederived here as one draft. No unverified earlier finding is
used as an accepted premise, and no formal gate is authorized this round.
All m used below are integers >=2, so beta,c,a,w are positive.

For each internal node v of the full binary tree of depth m let e_v be
an orthonormal vector. The leaf vector a_s is the sum over its ancestors
at depths j of (-1)^(next bit)*sqrt(w_j)e_v. Its norm squared is sum w_j=1.
Every coordinate row of the leaf matrix has zero sum. Distinct rows are
orthogonal: disjoint supports have zero product; on a nested pair the
descendant's two signs cancel where the ancestor is constant. Each row
has positive norm. The matrix consequently has rank2^m-1 and kernel the
constant leaf vector. Thus the leaves are affinely independent, average0,
and their convex hull K has0 in its full Euclidean interior.

For any x the unique maximizer of <x,g>-||g||^2/2 over compact K is the
minimizer of ||x-g||^2. Its characterization is
g in K and <x-g,v-g><=0 for every vertex v. Necessity follows by the
one-sided derivative along g+t(v-g); sufficiency by expansion of distance.
Adding these inequalities for two projections yields
||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>, hence the projection is1-Lipschitz.
The two maximizing formulas sandwich the first-order error of F between
0 and <P_K(x+d)-P_K(x),d>, bounded by||d||^2. This proves convexity,
differentiability and grad F=P_K. On the interior of K, grad F(x)=x,
so smoothness1 is sharp. F>=0. If x!=0, a sufficiently small positive
multiple t*x belongs to K and <x,t*x>-||t*x||^2/2>0. Thus the unique
minimizer is0. At x0=a_(0^m) the true squared minimum distance is1.

Permuting the two children of off-spine tree nodes is an orthogonal signed
coordinate transformation preserving K and fixing every spine vector.
Uniqueness makes projection commute with these transformations. Their
fixed subspace consists exactly of the m coordinates e_(0^j): swapping
the children at any off-spine node negates that coordinate. GD from x0
therefore stays in this subspace at every physical query.

Group leaves by their first right turn k<m, plus the final all-left group
k=m. Averaging a group cancels every off-spine coordinate and gives b_k,
whose j coordinate is sqrt(w_j) for j<k, -sqrt(w_k) for j=k<m,
and0 for j>k; b_m has all coordinates sqrt(w_j). Averaging any convex
representation shows K intersect spine=conv{b_0,...,b_m}. Conversely each
b_k is such an average, so lies in K. They are affinely independent.
For a spine residual y, <y,a_s> depends only on the first-right group and
equals <y,b_k>. Therefore checking all m+1 reduced normals checks every
one of the2^m original full-leaf normals, without assuming sparsity.
A group mass p_k is expanded uniformly over2^(m-k-1) leaves for k<m;
p_m is the last leaf mass. This gives full feasibility from reduced masses.

Write x_j=sqrt(w_j)z_j and let q_j=sum_(k>=j)p_k, with q_0=1.
Then g_j=sqrt(w_j)(2q_(j+1)-q_j), and its exact projection uniquely
minimizes sum_j w_j[z_j-(2q_(j+1)-q_j)]^2 subject to
1=q_0>=q_1>=...>=q_m>=0. This is the true feature metric; ordinary
Euclidean probability-simplex thresholding changes it.

For fresh core membership solve q_(j+1)=(q_j+z_j)/2 and take
p_j=q_j-q_(j+1), p_m=q_m. An independent equivalent formula is
p_k=2^(-k-1)[1+sum_(j<k)2^j z_j-2^k z_k] for k<m,
p_m=2^(-m)[1+sum_(j<m)2^j z_j]. Affine independence proves membership
iff every p is nonnegative. Only at that input does this imply g=x;
it says nothing by itself about the next input or any later face.

For any nonempty candidate face I with base vertex v0 and edge matrix E,
the affine closest point is v0+E t with (E^T E)t=E^T(x-v0).
E has independent columns, so its Gram is positive definite. Alternatively
the barycentric affine solution solves [V^T V,1;1^T,0](p,nu)=(V^T x,1).
This augmented system is nonsingular: a homogeneous solution has1^T p=0
and p^T V^T V p=0, which forces p=0 by affine independence, then nu=0.
An affine solution with nonnegative masses and all vertex normals<=0
is the true projection by the distance expansion. An active-set search
starts feasible, descends to an affine face minimizer, stops at a first
zero weight if needed, and adds a vertex with violated normal. We do
not rely on a complexity/termination theorem: a cap invalidates the case,
and only checked feasible/KKT outputs can be used. The small full-body
calibration instead enumerates its entire finite face list independently.

For y=x-g, F=||g||^2/2+<y,g>, with <y,g>>=0 from the vertex-normal
condition at0. The equality also gives2F=||x||^2-||y||^2. For sampled
jets of this SAME function, the original interpolation convention of
accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 reduces exactly to
Q_ij=F_i-F_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
    =<y_j,g_i-g_j><=0.
Full normals therefore control every past/future directed pair, without
fitting values. Floating-point checks are evidence, not exact certification.

The physical step is h_i=1+a*c^(v2(i+1)), where v2 is the exponent of2.
This follows by induction from H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j).
Every child uses the same enclosing m. Set A_i=||x_i||^2 and cex_i=<y_i,g_i>.
The directly expanded GD identity is
A_(i+1)=(1-h_i)^2 A_i
        +h_i(2-h_i)||y_i||^2-2h_i(h_i-1)cex_i.
The pilot logs both defect terms and all states to locate the remaining
full-history issue. It uses this forward identity for diagnostics, avoiding
division by tiny prefix products. A finite observed final core segment
is not declared permanent for untested inputs or m.

Only two accepted comparisons are evaluated: Huber=1/(1+2sum H_m), from
b98bb807d514e80d (or its predecessor's direct Huber construction), and
B_(2^m-1)<=4(1+sqrt2)^m from04219554e41d9750 proof(9.3).
Thus the latter is an UPPER multiplier, not the exact benchmark B.
No new benchmark recurrence is computed, no full-complex optimum is
estimated, and no analytic all-m terminal estimate is asserted.
