# Two seven-step departures with the full primitive cone retained

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. Worker max2, round46,
under TASK.md and master_guidance4572ef0ed580856f; plan3be4ac482d512899.
No fact_submit is authorized or used. The original all-h/all-n Goal is OPEN.

This document screens exactly TWO physical departure constructions. It gives
no above-threshold seed. Both selected paths are excluded by explicit static
smooth convex functions, even though every global cut and the unit-quadratic
test pass at the relevant positive parameters. Their exclusion leaves all56
ordered multipliers, every original flow equation, and arbitrary PSD
remainders on the entire R8 available. Restrictions on the physical paths
are search choices, not lossless reductions of the seven-step problem.

The only mathematical predecessors are accepted facts04219554e41d9750
(the primitive identity, composition, and integer-ray results in its proof
Sections7 and9--13) and1252b8823bb5a290 (the interpolation convention).
All new algebra and all static-function constructions used here are proved
jointly. Shared drafts ff8b941536cb035e,1f135237f7524f69,268dc507a7c26073
and392fb5bb2ef7e543 were read for awareness, not used as truth premises.
No phase-cover draft, numerical record, or unverified seed is a predecessor.

## 1. Original quantities and the seven-step physical lift

For a nonnegative word h=(h_0,...,h_6), put s=sum h_i and A=1+s.
Normalize smoothness to1. Retain all eight formal gradients g_0,...,g_7,
the original positions x_i=x_0-sum_(k<i)h_k g_k, and the signed residuals

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

The optimum jet is x_star=g_star=f_star=0 when an actual witness is given.
Set

 V_h=2sum_(i<7)h_i(f_i-f_7)
       +sum_(i<7)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_7||^2.

For the full finite-node array Lambda require Lambda_ii=0 and
Lambda_ij>=0 for EVERY i!=j in {0,...,7}. Let e be the column of eight
ones and define

 T_ii=1, T_ik=h_k for k<i, T_ik=0 for k>i,
 L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
 omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_6,2s)^T,
 D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_6(2-h_6),-s(s+2)),
 M_prim=D_prim+sym(L_flow T),  sym(X)=(X+X^T)/2.

The original certificate is L_flow^T e=omega and M_prim>=0 on ALL R8.
Accepted04219554e41d9750 gives, on the full original formal space,

 V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij
       =<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_7)>.                         (1)

The last pairing is the matrix trace inner product. An actual smooth
convex trajectory with V_h<0 therefore contradicts every such certificate:
all Q_ij<=0 and all Lambda_ij>=0 would make the left side negative, while
full PSD makes the right side nonnegative. No dual-attainment assertion is
needed. Repeated ACTUAL gradients in a witness do not identify any formal
certificate coordinates.

Here is the requested lift, derived directly from the accepted integer-ray
theorem. In the accepted normalization, W_n(h) is the supremum of
2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite-dimensional convex
L-smooth objectives with a minimizer and x_0!=x_star, under the specified
GD updates h_i/L. Write U_n^ZJ=1/B_n for the original all-split benchmark:

 C_0=B_0=1,
 C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
 B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),
 Omega(a,c)=(a+c+sqrt((a+c)^2+4ac))/2,
 G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2,
 lambda=1+sqrt2.

All split indices are integers. The accepted fact proves the positive
finite limit Z_b=lim_j lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1) for every integer b>=1,
and the SAME limit after any fixed nonnegative integer offset is subtracted
from the horizon. Reindexing this definition, without any phase theorem,
gives

 Z_(8b)=lim_j lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^(j+3)-1)=lambda^3 Z_b.     (2)

Conditionally suppose an actual length7 seed satisfies the full certificate
above. Replace the n primitive empty leaves of any fully expanded maximizing
B_n tree by this actual seed, retain all internal nodes, and keep its unique
terminal dominant empty leaf EMPTY. The accepted composition identities
recompute each primitive bridge as 1+d, where
d(A_left+A_right+d)=A_left*A_right, and each dominant bridge as
1+A_left/zeta, where zeta=(1+sqrt(1+8A_left/D_right))/2.

The resulting actual word Hsub_n has exactly8n physical steps and mass
Dsub_n=1+2sum Hsub_n. The general-length identities proved in the accepted
fact give

 W_(8n)(Hsub_n)=1/Dsub_n,
 [sqrt(A B_n)-(sqrt(A)-1)]^2 <= Dsub_n <= A B_n.           (3)

This is the full objective certificate, with a matching Huber witness.
For n_j=b*2^j-1 the TRUE horizon is

 N_j=8n_j=8b*2^j-8=(8b)*2^j-1-7.

Apply the accepted offset result with offset7 to its numerator. Equation(3)
implies lambda^(-j)Dsub_(n_j)->A Z_b: the difference from A B_(n_j)
is at most2(sqrt(A)-1)sqrt(A B_(n_j)), whose scaled value tends to zero.
Consequently

 lim_j B_(N_j)W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j))
       =Z_(8b)/(A Z_b)=lambda^3/A.                       (4)

Thus A>lambda^3 gives a fixed strict asymptotic improvement along EVERY
fixed integer b ray at its actual horizons. If epsilon=1-lambda^3/A>0,
then eventually W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j))<(1-epsilon/2)U_(N_j)^ZJ.
This implication supplies no seed, and is not an all-n profile theorem.

## 2. PSD coordinates do not remove the coefficient signs

We reproduce the small portion of the PSD construction needed to interpret
the two screens; it is not an invocation of draft ff8b941536cb035e.
The matrix T is invertible because it is triangular with unit diagonal.
Let v=T^(-1)e. Forward substitution gives

 v_0=1, v_(i+1)=(1-h_i)v_i,
 chi=1-A^2 v_7^2.                                      (5)

For the actual unit quadratic f(x)=x^2/2, x_0=1, the trajectory is
x_i=g_i=v_i, f_i=v_i^2/2, every ordered residual (including optimum
residuals) is zero, and V_h=chi. To see the last assertion directly,
h_i(2-h_i)v_i^2=v_i^2-v_(i+1)^2 telescopes.

For any real flow array one has e^T M_prim e=0 and v^T M_prim v=chi.
Indeed e^T D_prim e=-sum h_i^2-s^2 and omega^T T e=sum h_i^2+s^2;
also L_flow e=0 and Tv=e. PSD forces M_prim e=0 by applying positivity
to e+t z for arbitrary real t and arbitrary column z. If h is nonzero,
e and v are independent: v=c e would have c=v_0=1 and would force every
h_i=0 in the recursion. Individual zero, unit, or subunit steps do not
cause this independence to fail for a nonzero word.

For chi>0 set P_0=I-ee^T/8, u=P_0v, nu=u^T u>0, and

 M=(chi/nu)P_0.                                        (6)

This is a rank7 PSD matrix on the full R8, with Me=0 and v^T Mv=chi.
There is an explicit REAL equality array producing it. Put

 mu=e^T v/8,
 a_sk=-D_prim e-T^T omega,
 b_sk=(D_prim-M)v,
 c_sk=b_sk-mu a_sk,
 d_sk=c_sk+e(a_sk^T u)/8,
 K_sk=(a_sk e^T-e a_sk^T)/8+(d_sk u^T-u d_sk^T)/nu,
 L=(M-D_prim+K_sk)T^(-1).                              (7)

The scalar identities above give e^T a_sk=0, v^T b_sk=0, and
e^T b_sk+v^T a_sk=0. Hence e^T d_sk=u^T d_sk=0. Multiplication now
gives K_sk e=a_sk and K_sk v=b_sk. Thus Le=0,
L^T e=omega, and D_prim+sym(LT)=M. Set Lambda_ij=-L_ij off the diagonal
and Lambda_ii=0. Since Le=0, L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda exactly.
Every original diagonal and flow equation has been respected.

One may add ANY skew J with Je=Jv=0 to K_sk in(7), and the same
identities hold. More generally the same calculation works for every
symmetric M with Me=0 and v^TMv=chi. These equations do not guarantee
Lambda_ij>=0. The witnesses below show that NO such choice of M, rank,
or homogeneous skew can make all56 signs hold on the excluded words.
This includes the fully nonzero, rank7 PSD opportunity in(6), not merely
a zero or rank-one remainder. At the first path's t=0, chi=0 and its
ordinary basic certificate has M_prim=0. No positive-rank claim is made
at that singular endpoint, and no inverse of1-h_i occurs anywhere.

## 3. Static functions, every interpolation residual, and global cuts

For any compact convex K containing0 define the single static function

 F_K(x)=max_(g in K){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.                   (8)

Completing the square identifies its unique maximizer as the Euclidean
projection P_K(x). A proposed g in K is that projection exactly when
<x-g,z-g><=0 for all z in K. Adding this inequality at two queries
shows ||P_K(x)-P_K(y)||^2<=<P_K(x)-P_K(y),x-y>, hence the projection
is1-Lipschitz. The two maximizing inequalities also give

 0<=F_K(x+z)-F_K(x)-<P_K(x),z>
       <=<P_K(x+z)-P_K(x),z><=||z||^2.

It follows that F_K is convex, differentiable and1-smooth, with gradient
P_K. If0 is interior to K, it is the unique minimizer: F_K(0)=0, and
for x!=0 a sufficiently small positive multiple of x belongs to K and
has positive objective in(8).

At every actual query define y_i=x_i-g_i and
f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2. Direct expansion gives the complete residual
formula, in the ORIGINAL orientation,

 Q_ij=<y_j,g_i-g_j>,  including i or j equal to star.    (9)

Checking the normal against every vertex of K therefore checks every
directed finite, remote, terminal and optimum comparison. In particular
Q_(i,star)=0; the other optimum direction is also included because0 is in K.
The norm telescope along actual GD is

 ||x_i||^2-||x_(i+1)||^2
       =2h_i f_i+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2.

Using the actual terminal value gives

 V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_7+s g_7||^2.                        (10)

In particular, if x_7=g_7 the second square is A^2||g_7||^2.

For reference, the original global-cut screen at index j is

 A(h_j-1)_+ <= P_j^+ D_j^+,
 P_j^+=1+sum_(i<j)h_i, D_j^+=1+sum_(i>j)h_i.           (11)

Here these superscript-plus quantities include1, to distinguish them from
unshifted prefix/suffix sums in other project findings. To derive(11) when
h_j>1, set eta=(h_j-1)/D_j^+ and K=[-eta,1]. Start at x_0=P_j^+.
All queries through j have gradient1; query j equals1. Its step lands at
-D_j^+ eta. Every suffix gradient is-eta, and the terminal equals-eta.
This uses one static function(8), with unique minimizer0 and true initial
distance P_j^+. Equation(10) gives V_h=(P_j^+)^2-A^2 eta^2, proving
necessity. At h_j<=1 the displayed screen is automatically satisfied.

## 4. Departure I: a nonzero-moment path out of the balanced word

Write r2=sqrt2, u2=r2-1=1/lambda. For0<=t<=1/100 set

 a=r2-t, b=2+3t, c=a-1=u2-t, B=b-1=1+3t,
 h^I(t)=(a,b,a,1+lambda,a,b,a),
 P=1+2a+b=lambda^2+t,
 A=lambda^3+2t=2P+lambda.                              (12)

This is one selected symmetric physical path. Its equality of entries and
its fixed middle step are not restrictions on any competing certificate.
At t=0 the actual word is obtained by three levels of the accepted balanced
primitive join: singleton sqrt2, three-step(sqrt2,2,sqrt2), then(12).
Its capacity is lambda^3 and the embedded zero remainders remain zero.

For every t>0 in the displayed interval its capacity exceeds lambda^3.
Nevertheless it is excluded in the ENTIRE original cone, as shown below.

### 4.1 Both cheap screens pass strictly

For the center j=3, both shifted side sums in(11) equal P, and

 P^2-lambda A=2(lambda^2-lambda)t+t^2>0.

Every step equal to a has a<3/2 and A>14. Its shifted side sums satisfy
P_j^+D_j^+>=P_j^++D_j^+-1=A-a>A/2>A(a-1).
At either step b, the smaller side sum exceeds2 and the other exceeds10:
lambda-t>2 and A-a-b>14-3/2-203/100>10. Thus their product exceeds20,
whereas A(b-1)<15*(103/100)<20. This checks every index of this word.

The absolute unit-quadratic terminal product is lambda c^4 B^2. At t=0,
A lambda c^4 B^2=1. Its logarithmic derivative for0<=t<=1/100 is

 2/A-4/c+6/B <1/7-8+6=-13/7<0,

using A>14, 0<c<1/2, B>=1. Hence A|v_7|<1 and chi>0 whenever t>0.
In particular a rank7 REAL equality remainder(6) exists; signs remain
the essential condition, rather than the unit-quadratic moment.

### 4.2 One exact quadrilateral and all eight actual queries

Define

 R2=lambda(1-t-t^2/2)/(lambda-t)
       =[c lambda^2+(lambda/2)(2-a^2)]/(a+1),
 U=(1,0), V=(-1/2,sqrt(R2-1/4)),
 G4=-(lambda U+V)/a,
 K=conv{U,G4,V,-B V}.                                  (13)

The two expressions for R2 agree because lambda u2=1, a+1=lambda-t,
and2-a^2=2sqrt2*t-t^2. We have R2<=1, with equality only at t=0.
For a useful lower bound, since7/5<sqrt2<3/2,

 R2=1-[(1-u2)t+t^2/2]/(1-u2 t)
       >1-(61/100)t >=9939/10000>1/4.                  (14)

Indeed (1-u2)+t/2<3/5+1/200 and1-u2t>199/200; their ratio is less
than121/199<61/100. Thus U,V are independent. The positive relation
lambda U+a G4+V=0 makes0 interior to their triangle, hence interior to K.
Function(8) consequently has unique minimizer0.

Start at x_0=P U. The actual queries and gradients are

 x_i=(1+sum_(k=i)^2 h_k)U, g_i=U, 0<=i<=3,
 x_4=-lambda U,             g_4=G4,
 x_5=V,                    g_5=V,
 x_6=-B V,                 g_6=-B V,
 x_7=c B V,                g_7=c B V.                  (15)

An empty sum in the first line is zero. Every GD equality follows directly
from(12)--(13); in particular -lambda U-a G4=V. Since0<cB<103/200<1,
the terminal belongs to the segment from0 to V and therefore to K.
The true initial distance is exactly P, not a surrogate radius.

Here is the full normal check. For0<=i<=3, y_i is a nonnegative multiple
of U, and

 <U,G4>=-(lambda-1/2)/a<0,
 <U,V>=-1/2,
 <U,-B V>=B/2<=103/200<1=<U,U>.

So every prefix normal supports all vertices. The only other nonzero
normal is y_4=(-c lambda U+V)/a. Its product with V-G4 is

 [-c lambda^2+lambda(2-a^2)<U,V>+(a+1)R2]/a^2=0.       (16)

Use this equality to compare the remaining vertices:

 <y_4,U-G4>=<y_4,U-V>
       =[-3c lambda/2-1/2-R2]/a<0,
 <y_4,-B V-G4>=-(B+1)<y_4,V>
       =-(B+1)(R2+c lambda/2)/a<0.

The normal comparison with G4 itself is zero. At5,6,7 the normals are
zero because x_i=g_i. This proves every query in(15) is a projection onto
the SAME static K. Formula(9) is the exact value of every one of its
original interpolation residuals; all56 finite directed residuals and all
optimum residuals have the required sign. No terminal or remote row is
missing. Values are explicitly f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.

### 4.3 A finite strict negative-energy bound, not just a derivative

Equation(10) gives the exact energy

 V_(h^I(t))=P^2-A^2 c^2 B^2 R2.                         (17)

For0<t<=1/100 set X=(A/P)cB. Since A/P=lambda-u2*t/P,

 X=1+(3-lambda)t-3lambda t^2-(u2*t/P)cB.

Use lambda<5/2, P>5, u2<1/2, cB<3/5, and t<=1/100 to obtain

 X>1+(1/2-3/40-3/50)t
     =1+(73/200)t>1+(9/25)t.

Combining this with(14), all factors being positive, yields

 X^2 R2>(1+(18/25)t)(1-(61/100)t)
        =1+(11/100)t-(549/1250)t^2
        >1+t/10.

The last strict inequality follows from t<=1/100, since
11/100-549/125000=13201/125000>1/10. Therefore

 V_(h^I(t))<-P^2 t/10<0,  EVERY0<t<=1/100.              (18)

At t=0 the same actual construction has energy exactly zero, R2=1,
chi=0, and the center cut is an equality. All formulas stay defined there.
The positive interval passes BOTH cheap screens but has a negative full
primitive energy. Thus no choice among all56 coefficients and no nonzero
or higher-rank PSD remainder can certify ANY point of this interval.

## 5. Departure II: an asymmetric subunit path from a full-rank remainder

The second, genuinely different physical construction is the affine path

 h^II(t)=(1+t/2, 1+3t/2, 1-t/20, 1+23t/10,
           1+t/2, 1+3t/2, 1+4t/5),  0<=t<=1,
 A(t)=8+(141/20)t.                                     (19)

For t>0, the third step is subunit, all steps are positive, and the word
is asymmetric. At t=0 it is the all-ones word. We first give its ACTUAL
full-rank starting certificate rather than assuming feasibility.

### 5.1 The exact starting array and complete PSD remainder

For i=0,...,6 set

 Lambda_(i,i+1)=2(i+1)^2,
 Lambda_(i+1,i)=2(i+1)(i+2),

and set every other off-diagonal entry and every diagonal to zero.
These specify ALL56 entries. At node0 the outflow minus inflow is-2;
at each node i=1,...,6 it is
2[(i+1)^2+i(i+1)-(i+1)(i+2)-i^2]=-2;
at node7 it is112-98=14. Thus the original flow holds for h_i=1.

For a unit step the quadratic part of Q_(i+1,i) is
(||g_i||^2+||g_(i+1)||^2)/2, and

 Q_(i,i+1)+Q_(i+1,i)=||g_(i+1)||^2-<g_i,g_(i+1)>.

After adding2sum_(i=0)^6(i+1)Q_(i+1,i) to V_h, the value terms cancel,
and the quadratic is
sum_(i=0)^6(i+1)(||g_i||^2+||g_(i+1)||^2)-56||g_7||^2.
Add2sum_(i=0)^6(i+1)^2[Q_(i,i+1)+Q_(i+1,i)]. The coefficient of g_0^2
is1; for1<=i<=6 it is2i+1+2i^2=i^2+(i+1)^2; at7 it is-49+98=49.
Every cross coefficient is-2(i+1)^2. Hence the ENTIRE remainder is

 V_h+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij
       =sum_(i=0)^6(i+1)^2||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2.             (20)

It is PSD on R8, has rank7 and kernel exactly the common-gradient line.
The starting support is only a concrete initialization; it is not imposed
on any later certificate in our exclusion of the path.

### 5.2 Every global cut and the unit-quadratic screen along the path

Here |v_7|=(207/4000)t^7, so throughout0<=t<=1,

 A|v_7| <= (301/20)(207/4000)=62307/80000<1.             (21)

In particular chi>0 everywhere, including the unit-step endpoint t=0.
No zero terminal product is incorrectly inverted.

For each superunit coordinate, the exact cut margin
P_j^+D_j^+-A(h_j-1) is in the following table. Index2 has h_2<=1 and
thus passes its cut without this formula.

 j | exact margin
 --|------------------------------------------------------
 0 | 7+(51/20)t-(141/40)t^2
 1 | 12+(11/10)t-(161/20)t^2
 3 | 16+(3/5)t-(2151/200)t^2
 4 | 15+(81/4)t+(25/4)t^2
 5 | 12+(23/10)t-(271/40)t^2
 6 | 7-(3/20)t-(141/25)t^2

Each row except4 is concave in t and hence at least the lesser of its
two endpoint values on[0,1]. The values at1 for rows0,1,3,5,6 are,
respectively,241/40,101/20,1169/200,301/40,121/100, all positive;
their values at0 are positive as displayed. Row4 is at least15.
Consequently EVERY original global cut holds strictly along this path.

### 5.3 The complete last-pair static witness

For17/20<=t<=1 define

 P=1+sum_(i<5)h_i=6+(19/4)t,
 B=h_5-1=(3/2)t, C=h_6-1=(4/5)t,
 U_tail=B+C+2=A-P,
 H_def=BC[1+B(P+A)/P^2]-1.                              (22)

In this interval P>10, P<11, P+A>=2403/100>24, B>=51/40>5/4, and
BC>=867/1000. Therefore

 H_def>(867/1000)(1+30/121)-1
       =9917/121000>2/25.                              (23)

First suppose BC<1. Set

 kappa=B^2 C+U_tail(1-BC)>0,
 tau_tri=B^2 C/kappa in(0,1),
 u=(1,0), w=(tau_tri,sqrt(tau_tri(1-tau_tri))),
 v=-(B u+w)/(1+C),  K=conv{u,v,w}.                     (24)

The positive relation B u+(1+C)v+w=0 and independence of u,w put0
strictly inside the triangle. Its static function(8) has unique minimizer0.
Start at x_0=P u. All original prefix queries0,...,5 have gradient u and
positions x_i=(1+sum_(k=i)^4 h_k)u. The remaining actual data are

 x_6=-B u, g_6=v;    x_7=w, g_7=w.                    (25)

The last update holds because -B u-(1+C)v=w. The prefix includes the
original subunit step h_2; none of its queries is omitted. The true initial
distance is P. Give every query its actual value x_i dot g_i-||g_i||^2/2.

Prefix normals are nonnegative multiples of u, and compare negatively
with w-u and v-u because <u,w>=tau_tri<1 and
<u,v>=-(B+tau_tri)/(1+C)<0. At6, y_6=(-BC u+w)/(1+C). Direct expansion
using <u,w>=||w||^2=tau_tri gives

 <y_6,w-v>=(-B^2 C+kappa*tau_tri)/(1+C)^2=0,
 <y_6,u-v>=-BC(1-tau_tri)/(1+C)<0.                     (26)

The coefficient of tau_tri in the first numerator is
B+1+C+1-BC(1+C+1)=U_tail-BC(C+2)=kappa;
the equality to kappa follows also by expanding(24). The terminal normal
is zero. Thus these are projections onto ONE static K. Equation(9) supplies
ALL56 directed finite residuals and all optimum comparisons with their
actual nonpositive signs.

Using(10), the exact primitive energy is

 V_h=P^2-A^2 tau_tri=-U_tail*P^2*H_def/kappa.            (27)

For an algebra check,
A^2 B^2 C-P^2 kappa
=U_tail{BC[P^2+B(P+A)]-P^2}.
Furthermore kappa=U_tail-BC(C+2)<U_tail, so(23) and P>10 imply

 V_h<-P^2 H_def<-8.                                    (28)

If BC>=1, including the singular boundary BC=1, instead use the scalar
static function F(x)=x^2/2 for x<=1 and F(x)=x-1/2 for x>=1.
Its derivative min(x,1) is continuous, nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz;
its unique minimizer is0. Start at x_0=P. The original prefix through5
has gradient1 and query5 equals1. Query6 is-B with gradient-B, and
query7 is BC>=1 with gradient1. Every query is an actual query of this
ONE function. Their values again equal x_i g_i-g_i^2/2. Formula(9) is
valid by expansion: the only nonzero normals are nonnegative scalars
at gradients1, which support every actual gradient and the optimum0.
Thus all ordered residuals, including terminal and optimum ones, have
the correct sign. Equation(10) now gives

 V_h=P^2-[P+(1+B)(1+C)]^2<-21<-8,                      (29)

because P>10 and(1+B)(1+C)>1. The true initial distance remains P.
No degenerate triangle is used at BC=1, and no formal coordinate is
removed or identified at that boundary.

Together(28)--(29) give an exact strict original-cone obstruction for
EVERY17/20<=t<=1. Before that interval the capacity is at most

 A(17/20)=5597/400<14<lambda^3,

whereas A(1)=301/20>lambda^3. Therefore every point on the WHOLE selected
path(19) with capacity above lambda^3 lies in the excluded interval.
The full-rank initialization and the passing screens do not reach a seed.

## 6. What these two obstructions leave open

The first construction genuinely crosses from chi=0 into chi>0, while
increasing physical capacity above lambda^3. The second starts with the
explicit full-rank original certificate(20), permits a subunit physical
step, is asymmetric, and reaches capacity301/20. In both cases the complete
static witnesses prohibit every possible original nonnegative coefficient
array, including all homogeneous skew repairs and full nonzero PSD
remainders. No prescribed rank-one rate mechanism was reused.

These are restrictions on TWO declared physical families only. They do not
prove full seven-step impossibility, a maximum-capacity theorem, or the
original all-h/all-n asymptotic profile. A subthreshold starting certificate
and the conditional lift(4) are not substitutes for the missing seed.

A specific direction outside the exclusions remains: vary the two ordered
three-step blocks independently together with the central bridge, break
the first path's repeated leaves and equal side peaks, and adjust both
terminal steps so that the last-pair defect(22) is not positive. For example,
the seven independent physical coordinates can be written as
(sqrt2+p0,2+p1,sqrt2+p2,1+lambda+p3,sqrt2+p4,2+p5,sqrt2+p6), subject only
to nonnegativity and actual screens. This is a remaining coordinate domain,
not a third construction attempted here and not a claimed feasible family.
It must still solve all56 signs with full PSD; neither draft supplies this.

All algebra above was derived textually. No parameter scan, numerical
evaluation, solver, symbolic-evaluation program, benchmark replay, formal
verification, installation, consult, or native subagent was used.
The ONE assigned round's two-construction allowance is consumed. It is
not renewed by this handoff. No process-exit or external counter observation
is asserted. Every new result in this document remains UNVERIFIED.

# C101: a whole-word positive-reserve triangle and its complete scoped obstruction

Status: COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED DRAFT. Worker max3, allocation round1
under guidance2765a48d4ff7259e, completed baseline31, completed ceiling33,
attempted ceiling34 including the historical interruption. This return
completes analytical round32. No formal verification is allocated or used.
All new arguments below are one joint candidate, not accepted facts.

The original goal remains the all-nonnegative-word, all-sufficiently-large-
horizon inequality W_n(h)>=(1-o(1))/B_n, in the W=2R normalization. It is
OPEN. The result below excludes a new specified family, not unrestricted W.

Accepted premises:1252b8823bb5a290 for smooth-convex interpolation and W;
b98bb807d514e80d for K_n and the original benchmark recurrences;
1fca3e5dd555cb4a for the COMPLETE accepted alternating-ray family.
The earlier block law b0bf06c26eb801e6, the saturated-first-plateau proof
d9dcffd1ecce8822, the fixed-past obstruction48bf4f8bbf4e2b1d, and C99/C100
are unverified context only. Every reused scalar comparison is reproved here.

## 1. Concrete family, exact score, and main obstruction

Write the supplied physical word, without changing its order, as

    h=(block_0,b,block_1,d,block_2),
    S_j=sum block_j, j=0,1,2,       b>1, d>1.

Each block is an arbitrary finite list of nonnegative steps, including
empty lists. The full length n counts every one of these physical steps.
Let e,w be the standard orthonormal basis of R^2. Use the following LOCAL
scalar abbreviations throughout Sections1--7:

    T=1+S_0, M=1+S_1, N=1+S_2, sigma=1+2S_2,
    A=b-1, 0<=zeta<d-1, D=M+zeta, B=d-1-zeta,
    u>=0, v>0, ell>0,
    delta=A-Du, Z=Bv-N ell.

The symbol B in these sections is this positive scalar, not B_n.
The declared family consists of ALL choices of these parameters satisfying

    delta>=0,
    D v^2 <= (1+u)delta,                         (1)
    ell Z >= Bu.                                (2)

Define the three real gradients and ONE static function

    p=e, q=-u e-v w, r=ell w,
    C101=conv{0,p,q,r},
    F101(x)=max_(z in C101) {<x,z>-||z||^2/2},
    X_0=(T+delta)e-Dv w.                         (3)

The actual gradients are p throughout block_0 and at its following
checkpoint query, q throughout block_1 and at its following checkpoint
query, and r throughout block_2, including the terminal sample. Every
ordinary step in every block is executed separately. The exact true
initial squared distance and terminal normalized error are

    R101^2=(T+delta)^2+D^2v^2,
    V101=[2 ell Bv-sigma ell^2]/R101^2.           (4)

The three endpoint affine reserves, meaning f-||g||^2/2 at the last
point of each ordinary block, are

    delta, zeta(u^2+v^2), ell Z.                  (5)

In particular they can all be strictly positive. These are actual
function-value reserves. The homogeneous supporting planes have zero
intercepts, and the optimum upper rows are tight. Arbitrary nonzero plane
intercepts or arbitrary optimum slack are NOT part of this family.

Let i<j be the two actual checkpoint indices, and define using their
ORIGINAL preceding/intermediate/following masses

    P=sum_(t<i)h_t=S_0,
    S=sum_(i<t<j)h_t=S_1,
    U=sum_(t>j)h_t=S_2,
    Cut_j=(h_j-1)^2/[(1+P+h_i+S)^2(1+2U)],
    Pair_ij=(h_i-1)(h_j-1)/[(1+P)(1+S)].

For EVERY member of the declared family, without optimizing only a
particular example or assuming an attainable relaxation, we prove

    V101 <= max{Cut_j, Pair_ij^2/(1+2U)}.         (6)

Now fix the ORIGINAL enclosing-m parameters and word

    m>=128, lambda=1+sqrt(2),
    beta_m=1-1/m, c_m=lambda(1+1/m), a_m=beta_m/c_m,
    H_0=(), H_(k+1)=(H_k,1+a_m c_m^k,H_k),
    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, M_k=1+sum H_k.

The same a_m,c_m are retained in every recursive child. In particular
neither block grouping nor a two-checkpoint selection changes these
parameters, the physical horizon n, or B_n.

With gamma101=5949/6088<1, (6) implies for ALL checkpoint pairs and ALL
parameters in the declared family

    V101 <= gamma101/(2M_m-1)
          = gamma101 K_n(H_m)
          = gamma101 F_ray_n(H_m),               (7)
    limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1)
         sup_(C101 family on H_m) V101
          <= 2 gamma101/exp(1) < 1.              (8)

Thus joint selection of the initial anchor, positive reserves, two
checkpoint positions, three block contents, and all the displayed real
parameters cannot meet the original profile in this whole family.
Randomization supported on this family cannot improve the bound, since
it holds for every realization after its own true-radius normalization.

## 2. Static regularity, actual minimum, and all directed rows

For any compact convex set C containing0, maximizing
<x,z>-||z||^2/2 is equivalent to projecting x onto C. Its unique maximizer
g is characterized by

    <x-g,z-g><=0 for every z in C.                (9)

Necessity follows by differentiating squared distance on the segment
g+t(z-g) at t=0. Sufficiency follows from
||x-z||^2-||x-g||^2=||z-g||^2-2<x-g,z-g>.
Adding (9) at x and x' gives

    ||g-g'||^2 <= <g-g',x-x'>.

The two maximizing inequalities give

    0<=F(x')-F(x)-<g,x'-x>
       <=<g'-g,x'-x><=||x'-x||^2.

Therefore F is differentiable with gradient g, and the preceding
monotonicity inequality makes that gradient1-Lipschitz. F is convex as
a maximum of affine functions of x, and finite because C is compact.

Its minimum is0 and its minimizer set is the polar cone

    C^polar={x:<x,z><=0 for all z in C}.           (10)

A polar point makes every displayed maximand nonpositive and z=0 gives
zero. Conversely, if <x,z>>0 for some z in C, a sufficiently small
positive multiple of z belongs to C and has strictly positive maximand.

If u>0, a positive linear combination of p,q,r is0, namely
u p+q+(v/ell)r=0. The two independent directions p,r imply from (10)
that the minimizer is uniquely0. If u=0, (10) is exactly
{t e:t<=0}, since q and r are opposite nonzero w-directions. In that
case X_0 has positive e-coordinate T+A, so its closest minimizer is also
0. This proves the exact radius in (4), including the degenerate u=0
case. No arbitrary included minimizer is silently treated as closest.

For any actual sample write y_i=x_i-g_i and
f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2. Expanding the residual in accepted
1252b8823bb5a290 gives

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<y_j,g_i-g_j>.                          (11)

Consequently checking (9) at every sample proves ALL directed
interpolation rows, both orientations, within each block and between
all different blocks. The optimum sample x_star=g_star=f_star=0 is
included by the same formula. No terminal or earlier remote row is
deleted in this argument.

## 3. Every actual query, and necessity of the displayed family conditions

At cumulative ordinary mass t in block_0, 0<=t<=S_0, the proposed point
and proximal point are

    x=(T+delta-t)e-Dv w,
    y=(delta+S_0-t)e-Dv w.

At its final point y_0=delta e-Dv w, the p-plane has value delta,
the r-plane has value -ell Dv, the q-plane has value -u delta+Dv^2,
and the zero plane has value0. They are all dominated by the p-plane
exactly when (1) holds. Earlier points add a nonnegative multiple of p;
p's scalar product with itself is1 and its products with q,r,0 are
-u,0,0. Thus these earlier points satisfy every comparison as well.

The first checkpoint sends the last block_0 point to

    X_1=(T+delta-S_0-b)e-Dv w
        =-Du e-Dv w=Dq.                          (12)

At cumulative mass t in block_1 the point is (D-t)q and its proximal
point is (S_1+zeta-t)q. Its coefficient is nonnegative. Since
<q,p>=-u, <q,r>=-ell v, and <q,q>=0, (9) holds against all vertices.
The first point of the next block is exactly

    X_2=(D-S_1-d)q=-Bq=B(u e+v w).               (13)

At cumulative mass t in block_2 the proximal point for r is

    y=Bu e+[Bv-(1+t)ell]w.

At the endpoint t=S_2 its r-plane height is ell Z. Its p-plane height
is Bu, and its q-plane height is -Bu^2-vZ. Condition (2), with u>=0,
implies Z>=0, so the q- and zero-plane comparisons hold. Earlier
points add a nonnegative multiple of r; the r-plane increases while
the p-plane is unchanged and the q-plane decreases. Hence (9) holds
at every actual earlier tail query too.

These arguments prove sufficiency. Conversely, (9) at the last first
block query against0 and q forces (1), and (9) at the terminal sample
against p forces (2). Thus (1)--(2) are EXACT for the declared gradient
and point formulas. They are not merely local necessary tests.

At the terminal point

    x_n=Bu e+(Bv-S_2 ell)w,
    F101(x_n)=ell Bv-(S_2+1/2)ell^2.

Together with Section2 this proves (4) and the reserves (5).
If a block contains zero steps, the relevant point is repeated and the
same proof applies. Large ordinary steps cause no difficulty: their
cumulative masses still lie between0 and their original block sum.

## 4. A concrete selection rule from the whole supplied word

For every pair of checkpoint indices with b,d>1 and given block sums,
the following explicit choice is feasible with STRICTLY positive
reserves at all three block endpoints:

    zeta=(d-1)/2, B=(d-1)/2, D=1+S_1+zeta,
    v=sqrt(A/(4D)),
    u=min{A/(4D), Bv^2/(8N)},
    ell=Bv/(2N).                                (14)

All these parameters are positive. The first bound on u gives
delta>=3A/4, whereas Dv^2=A/4, so (1) is strict. Also

    Z=Bv/2,
    ell Z=B^2v^2/(4N)>=2Bu>0,                    (15)

which verifies (2) and makes its old-plane margin positive. The middle
reserve is zeta(u^2+v^2)>0. Thus this is an actual paid construction,
not an assumption that a feasible parameter choice exists.

For a fully specified word-dependent partition, consider the finite set
of pairs1<=i<j<=n-2 with h_i,h_j>1. For each such pair calculate the
EXPLICIT algebraic value (4) with (14). Choose its largest value and
break ties lexicographically. This is a finite mathematical selection
among explicit real functions, with no Gram feasibility oracle or
numerical search. If this set is empty the rule is not used; the accepted
Huber witness is already available. Every original H_m with m>=3 has
such pairs. The whole-family bound (7) applies regardless of which
pair is selected; it does not posit a favorable partition.

There is also a completely explicit partition requiring no maximization
on original H_m: choose i=1,j=n-2 for m>=3. Then

    b=d=1+beta_m, S_0=S_2=1+a_m,
    S_1=M_m-1-2(1+a_m)-2(1+beta_m).

The last quantity is the sum of the actual middle subword and is
nonnegative. Formula (14) applied to these displayed masses is an
explicit static function for the entire original physical word.

On these original H_m instances, the initial block contains the first
physical shortest step h_0=1+a_m and its following query. Both gradients
are p, so their inner product is1>0. This explicitly leaves C100's
nonpositive-adjacent-QUERIED-correlation hypothesis on ACTUAL data.
For the same first query,

    (F101(X_0)+||p||^2/2)/||p||^2
       =T+delta=1+S_0+delta>h_0.

Here1<h_0<2, so it also leaves C99's short-step value hypothesis.
The first endpoint reserve delta>0 and the transverse initial
coordinate -Dv are both paid in R101^2. The first checkpoint query is
not the saturated point p. These are exact scope escapes, not a claim
that escaping either old hypothesis alone produces a strong payoff.

Spatial scaling x->x/R101 and F->F(R101 x)/R101^2 gives a function with
true initial distance1 and the same normalized payoff. Multiplying by
any L>0 gives the original L-smooth convention without changing any
physical step of h. A point-mass probability distribution is therefore
a concrete law; common orthogonal rotations give randomized laws if
desired, with no change in (4) or the upper bound.

## 5. The exact literal continuation price

Keep the SAME function, all parameters, and all old jets fixed. Append
any ordinary word of total nonnegative mass t_extra, and require its
gradient to remain r, including its final sample. Then this is possible
in the declared law if and only if

    0<=t_extra<=Tcarry101,
    Tcarry101=(ell Z-Bu)/ell^2.                   (16)

Indeed the new proximal points have the form

    Bu e+(Z-t ell)w, 0<=t<=t_extra.

The current r-plane dominates the old p-plane exactly when
ell Z-t ell^2>=Bu. This condition implies Z-t ell>=0, so it also
dominates the zero and q-planes by the same calculation as Section3.
It therefore proves all old/new and new/new directed rows. If it fails,
the p-plane violates (9), so the actual gradient cannot remain r.

The true minimizer and radius are unchanged, and the exact new payoff is

    V101(t_extra)=V101-2t_extra ell^2/R101^2.       (17)

This is paid continuation, not a repeated reset of the function or a
free claim of arbitrarily many future checkpoints. A supplied continuation
with total mass exceeding (16) requires a changed gradient law and all
its new-old constraints. Positive reserve ell Z alone is insufficient.

## 6. Complete parameter-family upper bound

Completing the square in ell in (4) gives

    V101 <= (B^2/sigma) v^2/[(T+delta)^2+D^2v^2].  (18)

No attainment of this relaxed bound is assumed: (2) can make the
unconstrained maximizer in ell illegal.

Write Lc=D+A and t=delta=A-Du. Then0<t<=A for any feasible v>0,
and (1) is

    D^2v^2 <= (Lc-t)t.

For fixed t, the ratio in (18) increases with v^2, so it is at most

    Psi(t)=t(Lc-t)/[D^2(T^2+(2T+Lc)t)].           (19)

The derivative has the sign of

    Lc T^2-2T^2 t-(2T+Lc)t^2.

Its unique positive zero is t0=T Lc/(2T+Lc). Thus the maximum on[0,A]
is at min(A,t0). Two explicit branches yield the needed comparison.

If A>=t0, then

    max Psi = Lc^2/[D^2(2T+Lc)^2]
             <= A^2/(D^2 T^2).                   (20)

The last inequality is exactly A>=t0. If A<=t0, then

    max Psi = A/[D((T+A)^2+DA)]
             <= 1/(T+A+D)^2.                    (21)

To verify the last inequality, put X=T+A. Its positive-denominator
difference is

    D(X^2+DA)-A(X+D)^2
      =X[D(T-A)-A(T+A)]>=0,

because A<=T(D+A)/(2T+D+A) is equivalent to the last nonnegativity.
Consequently all parameters satisfy

    v^2/R101^2 <= max{A^2/(D^2T^2),1/(T+A+D)^2}.

Since D>=1+S_1 and B<=d-1, substituting in (18) proves (6), with the
original physical masses in both terms. This upper bound includes all
zeta,u,v,ell in (1)--(2), not only the safe rule (14), all pairs chosen
with full word knowledge, and all positive endpoint reserves.

## 7. A complete small example and a reserve-only continuation failure

Use the actual word h=(1,5,1,5,1) and choose

    S_0=S_1=S_2=1, zeta=1, u=1/4, v=1, ell=1/2.

Then T=2,D=3,A=4,B=3,N=2,delta=13/4,Z=2.
The first budget is3<65/16; the terminal old-plane comparison is
ell Z=1>Bu=3/4. The gradients are

    p=(1,0), q=(-1/4,-1), r=(0,1/2).

The exact entire trajectory and values are

    index  point                  gradient  value
    0      (21/4,-3)              p         19/4
    1      (17/4,-3)              p         15/4
    2      (-3/4,-3)              q         85/32
    3      (-1/2,-2)              q         51/32
    4      (3/4,3)                r         11/8
    5      (3/4,5/2)              r         9/8.

All directed rows follow from the explicit vertex comparisons already
proved. The minimum is uniquely0, R101^2=585/16, and V101=4/65.
The three endpoint reserves are13/4,17/16,1, all strictly positive.
Adjacent queried inner products at the ordinary steps are1 and17/16.

The COMPLETE same-word accepted K_5 is max{1/27,4/15,1/12}=4/15.
For the ray family, checkpoints of size1 cannot appear before a later
checkpoint in a feasible positive-gradient construction. A size1 final
checkpoint also gives no positive improving term. The only two useful
checkpoints are the two5's. Their accepted recurrence gives
m_0=1/2,m_1=1,O_1=1/2,z_term=7/2,e_term=1/4,q_opt=7/6,
and exact score55/12. Together with the zero- and single-checkpoint
cases this proves F_ray_5(h)=55/12. In particular the example is weaker
than the full K and full ray references. Its role is nonvacuity and a
continuation counterexample, not benchmark progress by itself.

Formula (16) gives Tcarry101=1, whereas exhausting only the current
reserve against the zero plane would incorrectly allow mass4.
Appending the single actual step2 sends the point to(3/4,3/2). Keeping
the proposed gradient r would give proximal point(3/4,1), where the
old p-plane is3/4 and the r-plane is1/2. Thus the original remote
residual Q_(0,6)=1/4>0 for that proposed extension, despite its positive
remaining reserve1/2 and perfect local holding identities.

For the SAME static function the actual gradient there is instead
(1/5,2/5), the projection onto the edge joining p to r: its difference
from the point is(11/20,11/10), orthogonal to that edge and having
nonpositive products with differences to0 and q. Its actual value is
13/20. No illegal extension is described as a legal trajectory.
This refutes only the reserve-only holding-continuation rule.

## 8. Original-ruler comparisons, proved for every checkpoint pair

Abbreviate a=a_m,c=c_m,rho_c=c-2 for this section. These are not new
word parameters. The mass recurrence and its explicit solution are

    M_0=1, M_(k+1)=2M_k+a c^k,
    M_k=[a/(c-2)]c^k+[1-a/(c-2)]2^k.             (22)

For m>=128 the following rational bounds suffice:

    12/5<lambda<c<49/20,
    0<a<lambda-2<rho_c<=9/20,
    M_k<=c^k, M_(k+1)<=c M_k,
    a c^k<=rho_c M_k.                            (23)

For c<49/20, use sqrt(2)<10/7 and
c<(17/7)(129/128)=2193/896<49/20. The other scalar bounds follow
from a=(m-1)/[(m+1)lambda] and (22).

Each occurrence of a level-k checkpoint h=1+a c^k is immediately
preceded and followed, in its local recursive copy, by H_k. Hence
its original prefix and suffix masses plus1 are at least M_k.

First consider all single cuts of a fixed level with step h and total
mass S_total=M_m-1. Their denominator is

    (1+P)^2[1+2(S_total-h-P)].

Its derivative has the sign of2(S_total-h)-3P. Thus its minimum
over any interval is at one of the endpoints, so it suffices to use
the first and last occurrence of that level. The first has prefix
mass M_k-1 and suffix mass at least M_(m-1)-1; its score is at most
rho_c^2/(2M_(m-1)-1). For the last, the roles are reversed; use
monotonicity of x^2/(2x-1) for x>=1 to get the same bound. Therefore
every single cut obeys

    Cut_i / [1/(2M_m-1)]
      <=rho_c^2(2c-1)<=3159/4000.                 (24)

Here (2cM-1)/(2M-1)<=2c-1 for M>=1. The canonical Huber term
therefore gives exactly K_n(H_m)=1/(2M_m-1).

Next take ANY i<j, with levels k,l. Write a_k=a c^k, a_l=a c^l and
P,S for the original preceding/intermediate masses. We show

    Pair_ij<=q_pair:=c rho_c^2<1/2.               (25)

If i lies before the entire copy H_l immediately preceding j,
then1+P>=M_k and1+S>=M_l, giving the stronger bound rho_c^2.
This includes k>=l: a level at least l cannot occur inside H_l.
Otherwise k<l and i is inside that copy H_l. Let P_loc be its mass
before i within this copy. The adjacent copies of H_k give

    x=1+P_loc>=M_k, y=1+S>=M_k,
    x+y=M_l-a_k.

As (x-M_k)(y-M_k)>=0, the original denominator is at least
M_k(M_l-a_k-M_k). Put theta=a_k/M_k<=rho_c and z=M_l/M_k.
Then z>=2+theta and a_l<=rho_c M_l, so

    Pair_ij<=theta rho_c z/(z-1-theta)
       <=theta rho_c(2+theta)
       <=rho_c^2(2+rho_c)=q_pair.

The middle step follows by differentiating z/(z-1-theta), whose
derivative is negative. Finally
q_pair<=(49/20)(9/20)^2=3969/8000<1/2.

This already compares the COMPLETE accepted ray family. If an accepted
ray selection had at least two checkpoints i,j, its first two magnitudes
would be m_0=1/(1+P), m_1=(h_i-1)m_0/(1+S), and its first old offset
would be P m_0. The second-return budget is

    (h_j-1)m_1-Pm_0=m_0[Pair_ij(1+P)-P]<m_0,

because Pair_ij<1. With exactly two checkpoints the terminal old-slope
condition requires at least m_0; with more checkpoints the next
nonterminal magnitude would be less than m_0, violating same-ray
monotonicity. This uses the full recurrence and feasibility conditions
of accepted1fca3e5dd555cb4a. Thus no selection with two or more
checkpoints is feasible, and

    F_ray_n(H_m)=K_n(H_m)=1/(2M_m-1).             (26)

To retain the tail in the stronger two-cut comparison, write

    p0=1+P, x0=1+S, t0=1+U, Rpair=Pair_ij.

These are local positive scalars, not trajectory coordinates. Since
H_m is a palindrome, (25) in reverse order gives
(h_i-1)(h_j-1)/(t0 x0)<=q_pair. Also the local subwords give
h_i-1<=rho_c p0 and h_j-1<=rho_c t0. Consequently

    Rpair<=q_pair,
    p0<=q_pair t0/Rpair,
    x0<=rho_c^2 t0/Rpair.

The EXACT mass identity is

    M_m=p0+x0+t0+(h_i-1)+(h_j-1)
        <=(1+rho_c)(p0+t0)+x0.

Substitution yields

    Rpair <= min{q_pair,D_pair t0/M_m},
    D_pair=2(1+rho_c)q_pair+rho_c^2.              (27)

For M=M_m>=8, the crossover t*=q_pair M/D_pair exceeds1 because

    q_pair D_pair<=661/800,
    D_pair/q_pair=2(1+rho_c)+1/c<=199/60<4.

The first inequality follows from q_pair<1/2 and
D_pair<29/20+81/400=661/400. The functions t^2/(2t-1) and1/(2t-1)
are respectively increasing and decreasing for t>=1. Applying (27)
on the two sides of t* therefore gives

    Rpair^2/(2t0-1)
      <=q_pair D_pair/(2M-D_pair/q_pair),
    [Rpair^2/(2t0-1)]/[1/(2M-1)]
      <=(661/800)(2M-1)/(2M-199/60)
      <=5949/6088.                               (28)

The last rational function decreases with M and its value at M=8 is
5949/6088. This proves (7) from (6), (24), and (26), for EVERY original
pair and every member of the declared family. In particular the result
does not merely compare to an arbitrarily small subset of ray witnesses.
Any accepted reference augmented by the Huber witness is at least this
same Huber value and also dominates the whole C101 family here.

## 9. The original all-split benchmark comparison

We rederive the required estimate from the recurrences in accepted
b98bb807d514e80d. Let rho=log_2(lambda)>1, and let C_n=1+S_circle(n).
The mass forms of the accepted joins are

    Omega(A0,A1)=(A0+A1+sqrt((A0+A1)^2+4A0A1))/2,
    G(A0,D0)=2A0+D0/2+sqrt(D0^2+8A0D0)/2.

Set Fsc(t)=(1+t)^rho-1 for t>0. Its logarithm as a function of log t
has second derivative

    rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)
      [(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/Fsc(t)^2>0.

The bracket is positive by integrating the positive derivative of
(1+t)^rho-1-rho t. Convexity at log t and -log t gives
Fsc(t)Fsc(1/t)>=Fsc(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2. Hence

    Omega(A0,A1)<=(A0^(1/rho)+A1^(1/rho))^rho

for positive A0,A1: substituting D0=(A0^(1/rho)+A1^(1/rho))^rho makes
(D0-A0)(D0-A1)>=2A0A1, so D0 is above the positive root defining Omega.
Zero arguments follow by continuity. Induction in the original all-split
recursion yields C_n<=(n+1)^rho. Since G(A0,D0)<=4A0+D0, another
induction gives

    B_n<=1+4n^rho.                               (29)

For a split k+l+1=n use (k+1)^rho+l^rho<=n^rho; this follows from
the increasing derivative of x^rho. Thus no selected split or fitted
dyadic benchmark has replaced B_n.

Equation (22), with its ACTUAL enclosing-m parameters, gives

    M_m/lambda^m
      =(1+1/m)^m[a_m/(c_m-2)
          +(1-a_m/(c_m-2))(2/c_m)^m]
        ->exp(1).

Here a_m/(c_m-2)->1 and2/c_m is uniformly below1. Combining (7) and
(29), with n=2^m-1, proves the exact finite upper bound

    B_n sup V101
      <=gamma101[1+4(2^m-1)^rho]/(2M_m-1),        (30)

whose right side tends to2gamma101/exp(1)<1. This is (8).
For context the COMPLETE scalar/ray reference itself has limsup
B_n K_n(H_m)<=2/exp(1)<1. Thus this is a comparison precisely on words
where those existing channels miss the original target; the new family
is strictly weaker still.

## 10. Scope, resources, and next open interface

The construction jointly pays a transverse, noncore initial anchor,
three positive endpoint reserves, all ordinary physical queries, the
static minimizer, and exact continuation. Its bound excludes ALL its
parameters and partitions on original H_m. It does not assume recovery
of a fixed old past and does not reuse a conditional next-block mass
condition. The new scoped obstruction is not C99 or C100 reapplied:
Section4 exhibits actual violations of both of their stated hypotheses.

The operative restrictions still include orthogonal first/final gradient
directions, a radial middle plateau, only two checkpoint changes, and
homogeneous support planes with no independent affine intercepts. The
large remaining class with other endpoint angles, independent affine
supports, nonradial middle residuals, or many jointly selected checkpoint
transitions is NOT excluded. Neither positive reserves alone nor meeting
an anchor/correlation count supplies those missing old-new constraints.

The accepted finite C59 fact e9a55f57461f253c concerns the ACTUAL
enclosing-H6 word only. The m>=128 comparison above is not a comparison
to C59 and does not transfer that finite witness to later-m subwords.
There is no assertion about a new finite H6 record here.

The direct-proving screen completed the static-law, exact-radius,
all-query, literal-continuation and whole-stated-family-obstruction
subgoals jointly. The desired profile-strength construction failed for
this entire family by (30), so it is not treated as a successful positive
route. The concrete reserve-only continuation claim is refuted in
Section7; no unrestricted continuation claim is inferred from it.

Literature grounding: Matlas search returned the standard Moreau-envelope
smoothness lemma in2303.08785, short-step self-contraction in2003.04201,
and a constrained convex-hull bound in2111.07720; none supplies the
missing long-step law and none is a premise. Web search recovered the
known smooth interpolation reference. The exact local paper
downloads/taylor-hendrickx-glineur-1502.05666.txt, Section2.5, Theorem4,
its proof/construction and Corollary1, was read. Its definitions specialize
to finite Euclidean data, convexity and Lipschitz gradient1. The relevant
interpolation fact is already accepted as1252b8823bb5a290, and the
projection realization here is proved directly. No new external theorem
is used as a black box.

No numerical evaluation, solver, machine algebra, replay, formal verifier,
installation, consult, or subagent was used. Operational file and text
checks do not verify mathematics. One allocated analytical round remains
after this return; no older round or resource is renewed. The main goal
remains OPEN, and this entire joint draft remains UNVERIFIED.

# Two new original-row bases and a reset-threshold obstruction

JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical report, worker max80. Original target OPEN.
This is the single invocation allocated by TASK.md, after baseline79, with
at most two storage mechanisms. No fact_submit, mathematical program,
numerical or arithmetic replay, solver, executed recurrence, subagent,
installation, or consult was used. Every new calculation below belongs to
this ONE proof attempt. None is an accepted intermediate result.

The proposed replacement bases pass the inherited H4 and deep
zero-displacement tests. Their explicit full-space certificates have poor
objective levels. A stronger obstruction then excludes the desired level
for EVERY coefficient choice in a substantially larger boundary-row family,
including arbitrary dependence on the enclosing horizon and ancestors.
The obstruction is a rank-one transcript feasible for that restricted row
family, with every actual step and the actual terminal retained. It violates
an explicitly identified omitted PREFIX interpolation row. It is NOT an
interpolable primal witness, a lower bound on W, or unrestricted dual
infeasibility. The missing remote-row construction remains theorem-level.

## 1. Original problem, accepted inputs, and scope

For every integer n>=0 and every predetermined h in [0,infinity)^n,
W_n(h) is the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over
all finite-dimensional Euclidean spaces, convex differentiable L-smooth
functions with an attained minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star. The actual
updates are x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R.

The ORIGINAL benchmark retains ALL integer splits:

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,
    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,
    C_n=max_(integer 0<=k<n)Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
    B_n=max_(integer 0<=k<n)G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

The original question is whether lim_n B_n inf_h W_n(h)=1. An affirmative
answer must have the original uniform quantifier over every nonnegative
word and every sufficiently large actual horizon. A negative answer needs
a rigorous full-function upper bound beating the benchmark on unbounded
actual horizons. No restricted-function or restricted-row computation is
such an answer.

Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the normalization, the original
interpolation convention, and the full independent-vector certificate test.
In particular its deletion of incoming ACTUAL-terminal rows does not delete
any queried-prefix row. Accepted fact931b9653ce0f7201, read in full, supplies
the all-n envelope B_n<=(12/5)(n+1)^rho and the SAME-m comparison
B_(2^m-1)/D_m<47/50 for all m>=1536. Its final upper-bound statement is
conditional: W_(2^m-1)(H_m)<=17/(16D_m) would imply a 799/800 separation.
That W bound is not accepted and is not proved here. Facts042 and5fd are
not additional proof premises in this report.

Fix the SAME enclosing integer m>=1536 and set

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, d_j=a*c^j,
    Hword_0=(), Hword_(j+1)=(Hword_j,1+d_j,Hword_j),
    n=2^m-1, h=Hword_m, S=sum_(i<n)h_i, D=1+2S,
    b=1+a, Mleaf=1+b, Nleaf=2^(m-1).

These local hierarchy symbols do not overwrite the project glossary.
The literal indices are h_i=1+a*c^level(i), where level(i) is the exponent
of 2 dividing i+1. Thus h_(2k)=b for 0<=k<Nleaf, and the queried odd
indices 2k+1 have h_(2k+1)=1+d_(1+v2(k+1)), 0<=k<Nleaf-1. There are
exactly n physical steps, and index n is the unqueried terminal. Every
child uses these same parameters, without resetting epsilon.

The identities lambda^2=2lambda+1 and 1/lambda=lambda-2 give

    b=sqrt(2)-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)],
    1<b<sqrt(2)<3/2,
    d_1=1-epsilon,
    d_2=lambda(1-epsilon^2)>2,
    2<c<5/2.

For d_2>2 use lambda>12/5 and epsilon<1/8, which give
d_2>(12/5)(63/64)>2. For c<5/2 use lambda<17/7 and
epsilon<=1/6144. Thus steps of levels0,1 lie strictly below2;
every higher-level step lies strictly above3. No step equals2.

Normalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Retain all ORIGINAL independent
vectors x_0,g_0,...,g_n, with x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t. Write

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Actual smooth-convex samples satisfy Q_ij<=0. A nonnegative row array
lambda_ij with finite flow out_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n) has a full certificate

    M_cert(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij.

Its value coefficients cancel. PSD must hold on ALL original vectors.

## 2. Mechanism I: chronological storage with a last-leaf objective interface

This is a completely new original-index array; it does not use inherited
Lambda or Kbase. It starts from original optimum-sector rows and finishes
with a two-gradient objective interface on the last physical leaf.

For each physical i define the scalars and vector expression

    kap80_i=max(1,h_i-1),
    r80_i=2h_i                 if h_i<2,
        2h_i(h_i-1)          if h_i>2,
    s80_i=h_i*abs(h_i-2),
    z80_i=g_i                 if h_i<2,
        x_i-g_i             if h_i>2.

These definitions refer to the actual h_i; no gradient is identified with
another gradient. Let jlast=n-1, Dleaf=1+2b, qleaf=1/Dleaf, and

    Ppre=product_(0<=i<jlast)kap80_i^2,
    w80_i^I=qleaf*product_(i<t<jlast)kap80_t^2  (i<jlast),
    q_I=qleaf*Ppre.

Every nonzero original multiplier of Law I is

    lambda_(i,star)=lambda_(star,i)=w80_i^I*r80_i, i<jlast;
    lambda_(jlast,star)=1;
    lambda_(n,star)=1;
    lambda_(n,jlast)=1.                                  (I.1)

All other rows have coefficient zero. The support has no incoming actual
terminal edge. All signs are positive. Prefix sector pairs are cycles;
the three last rows cancel f_(jlast) and give terminal flow2, so every
finite flow is correct. The full initial point, both endpoint gradients,
and the endpoint value are present in this law.

The original-row identities are

    kap80_i^2||x_i||^2-||x_(i+1)||^2
           +r80_i(Q_(i,star)+Q_(star,i))=s80_i||z80_i||^2,    (I.2)

and, writing X=x_(jlast), u=g_(jlast), v=g_n,

    qleaf||X||^2-2f_n+Q_(jlast,star)+Q_(n,star)+Q_(n,jlast)
      =qleaf||X-Dleaf(u+v)/2||^2
                      +(3-2b)||u-v||^2/4.              (I.3)

For (I.2), Q_(i,star)+Q_(star,i)=||g_i||^2-<x_i,g_i>.
When h_i<2, direct expansion leaves h_i(2-h_i)||g_i||^2.
When h_i>2, it leaves h_i(h_i-2)||x_i-g_i||^2.
For (I.3), expanding the three rows gives

    qleaf||X||^2-<X,u+v>+||u||^2+(2b-1)<u,v>+||v||^2.

Expansion of the displayed two squares has exactly these coefficients.
Since b<3/2 both square coefficients are positive. Multiplying (I.2)
by w80_i^I and adding (I.3) proves the ENTIRE identity

    M_I(q_I)=sum_(i<jlast)w80_i^I*s80_i||z80_i||^2
       +qleaf||x_(jlast)-Dleaf(g_(jlast)+g_n)/2||^2
       +(3-2b)||g_(jlast)-g_n||^2/4.                    (I.4)

There is no omitted displacement or residual. This gives a full-function
upper-certificate draft at q_I, not at the requested q=17/(16D).

At any real original cut r<s<=jlast, the same identity reads

    [product_(r<=i<s)kap80_i^2]||x_r||^2-||x_s||^2
      +sum_(r<=i<s)[product_(i<t<s)kap80_t^2]r80_i
                              (Q_(i,star)+Q_(star,i))
      =sum_(r<=i<s)[product_(i<t<s)kap80_t^2]s80_i||z80_i||^2. (I.5)

Two consecutive blocks compose by multiplying the first identity by the
second block's product and adding, with its ACTUAL common x_s. At a
dyadic parent, the actual one-step bridge is an additional such factor.
The child displacement is always sum h_i g_i and x_s=x_r-sum h_i g_i.
These are context-dependent suffix weights, not fictitious resets.
They solve all-depth positivity for this law analytically, but the
product growth costs too much. Section7 excludes *any* useful coefficient
choice on a much richer support, without assuming scalar-only storage.

## 3. Mechanism II: dense checkpoint circulation and full terminal storage

The second family permits real mixed boundary information and all enclosing
context. Its coefficient support is much larger than Law I. Let

    Leaf={0,2,...,n-1}, Check={1,3,...,n-2}.

There are Nleaf leaves and Nleaf-1 queried checkpoints. Allow these rows:

  (a) both optimum rows for EVERY original finite index, including n;
  (b) EVERY ordered pair of distinct checkpoints;
  (c) EVERY leaf-to-checkpoint row Q_(ell,p), ell in Leaf, p in Check;
  (d) checkpoint-to-leaf rows Q_(p,ell) when |p-ell|=1;
  (e) EVERY outgoing actual-terminal row Q_(n,i), i<n.

Call this support E80. It contains both directions of every adjacent
queried-prefix pair, complete checkpoint coupling, all outgoing leaf
links to all checkpoints regardless of ancestors, and the whole terminal
fan. It permits optimum-to-terminal. It excludes incoming queried-to-
terminal rows and remote checkpoint-to-leaf / leaf-to-leaf rows.

For the support-level obstruction later, EVERY nonnegative coefficient
on E80 is free, with arbitrary dependence on m and full ancestor context.
Storage may be any quadratic form in x_0, actual boundary positions and
boundary gradients, with value terms and all real mixed inner products.
It may use arbitrarily many such boundary coordinates. The only restriction
is that its telescoped original-row certificate uses E80. No reduction
to a scalar metric is presumed.

Here is an explicit FULL feasible coefficient law inside that family, as
a starting point. It proves existence of a genuine changed base and makes
every added cost auditable. Put

    Pfull=product_(i<n)kap80_i^2,
    w80_i=product_(i<t<n)kap80_t^2,
    Kprod=product_(i<n)(1+h_i),
    alpha80=epsilon/[(n+2)^2*Kprod^2],
    eta80=alpha80/[4(S+2)(n+1)^2],
    q_II=Pfull+1.

Start with coefficients lambda_(i,star)=lambda_(star,i)=w80_i*r80_i for i<n,
lambda_(n,star)=2, and every other entry zero. To these add eta80 on
each directed edge of each of the following cycles, counting overlaps:

    A_(ell,p): ell -> p -> star -> ell,
                           for every ell in Leaf and p in Check;
    B_(p,r):   p -> r -> p, for every unordered pair in Check;
    C_(p,ell): p -> ell -> p, for every |p-ell|=1;
    T_i:       n -> i -> star -> n, for every 0<=i<n.      (II.1)

The arrow denotes the ordered Q source and receiver, not a GD update.
All cycles use original indices. Formula (II.1) specifies EVERY entry
without an optimization problem or unknown PSD block. More explicitly,
write rcheck=Nleaf-1. The finite entries are

    lambda_(p,r)=eta80                (distinct p,r in Check),
    lambda_(ell,p)=eta80*(1+1_(|p-ell|=1)),
    lambda_(p,ell)=eta80*1_(|p-ell|=1),
    lambda_(n,i)=eta80                (0<=i<n).

The optimum entries, including EVERY incoming one, are

    lambda_(ell,star)=w80_ell*r80_ell+eta80,
    lambda_(star,ell)=w80_ell*r80_ell+rcheck*eta80,
    lambda_(p,star)=w80_p*r80_p+(Nleaf+1)*eta80,
    lambda_(star,p)=w80_p*r80_p,
    lambda_(n,star)=2,
    lambda_(star,n)=n*eta80.                              (II.2)

All unlisted entries, including diagonals, are zero. Every scalar shown
is strictly positive where its indicator is one. Each cycle has zero
value flow at each vertex, and the initial array has only terminal flow2.
Hence the full original value cancellation holds exactly. In particular
there is no implicit subtraction of an incoming optimum cost.

Let Cyc be the list in (II.1), and write Q_C=sum_(i,j in cycle C)Q_ij.
The exact entire-word composition identity is

    M_II(q_II)=||x_0||^2+sum_(i<n)w80_i*s80_i||z80_i||^2
                  +||x_n-g_n||^2+eta80*sum_(C in Cyc)Q_C. (II.3)

It follows by (I.2) on ALL physical steps and
||x_n||^2-2f_n+2Q_(n,star)=||x_n-g_n||^2.
Every Q_C is kept with its original mixed terms and both original
displacements. Individual cycles are NOT asserted PSD.

For a cut or dyadic composition, apply (I.5) to each physical child and
the actual bridge, multiply by the enclosing suffix product, and then
add the cycles assigned to that parent. One unambiguous assignment is
the smallest dyadic node containing all finite vertices of a cycle;
if that set is not contained in a proper node, assign it to the root.
Rows involving n are assigned to the root. There is no need to discard
crossing cycles: their exact Q_C polynomials form the boundary coupling.
This is a complete additive identity, not a claim of local child PSD.

For completeness, the all-depth PSD expenditure is explicit. Every
w80_i>=1 and s80_i>=epsilon. At level1, s80_i=(2-epsilon)epsilon>=epsilon.
At level0 use b>1 and 2-b>1/2. At higher levels h_i>3, so s80_i>3.
Let z=x_n-g_n and let Vnorm^2=||x_0||^2+sum_(i<=n)||g_i||^2.
The triangular change of coordinates from (x_0,z80_0,...,z80_(n-1),z)
to the original vectors has the following elementary bound:

    Vnorm^2 <= (n+2)^2*Kprod^2
                   [||x_0||^2+sum_(i<n)||z80_i||^2+||z||^2]. (II.4)

Indeed, for h_i<2 one has g_i=z80_i and x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i z80_i;
for h_i>2 one has g_i=x_i-z80_i and
x_(i+1)=(1-h_i)x_i+h_i z80_i. Induction using factors1+h_i bounds
each x_i and each g_i by Kprod times the sum of residual-vector norms
appearing up to that coordinate. The last equality g_n=x_n-z obeys the
same bound including z. Cauchy--Schwarz for n+2 terms, followed by summing
the n+2 original squared norms, proves (II.4).
Thus the first three terms of (II.3) are at least alpha80*Vnorm^2.

After deleting its value terms, every individual Q row has absolute
quadratic value at most (S+2)*Vnorm^2. For two finite indices use
||x_i-x_j||<=S*Vnorm and ||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=Vnorm^2.
For an outgoing optimum row use ||x_i||<=(1+S)Vnorm, and for an incoming
optimum row its quadratic is ||g_i||^2/2. The value terms of each cycle
cancel, so the row bound can be summed inside that cycle.

The number of row occurrences over all cycles is exactly

    Lcyc=3Nleaf(Nleaf-1)+(Nleaf-1)(Nleaf-2)
                          +4(Nleaf-1)+3n
        =4Nleaf^2+4Nleaf-5 < 2(n+1)^2.

Consequently the absolute sum of all added cycle quadratics is strictly
less than alpha80*Vnorm^2/2. Equation (II.3) proves

    M_II(q_II) >= alpha80*Vnorm^2/2.                     (II.5)

This pays ALL cross-cycle and initial costs once, on the full space, for
every m>=1536. It is a full certificate draft at the very poor q_II. It
does not suggest that cycles may be added freely at the desired tiny q.

## 4. Required original-space screens

Use exactly the earlier H4 direction: x_0=0, g_0,...,g_3=1,
g_8,...,g_11=-1, and every other original gradient, including g_n, zero.
The two active blocks have equal physical mass, so their displacement
cancels exactly. For Law I, (I.4) has all nonnegative terms and its
first term is w80_0^I*b(2-b)>0. For Law II, (II.5) is strictly positive
on this nonzero original vector. These conclusions use the FULL forms;
there is no projection to a primitive or constant-gradient subspace.

For the deep wave at every 2<=j<=m-3, let p=2^j-1, Hbridge=1+d_j,
x_0=0, g_0=-Hbridge/b, g_p=1, and all other original gradients zero.
Again b*g_0+Hbridge*g_p=0, exactly. In Law I the first nonnegative
term is w80_0^I*b(2-b)*(Hbridge/b)^2>0. Law II is positive by (II.5).
Both screens therefore PASS for both replacement bases, even if their
initial coefficient q is replaced by17/(16D): x_0=0 on both tests, so
that replacement does not change either value. Passing does not imply
a useful objective level; Section7 supplies a different test.

## 5. Full initial/common-gradient and actual-terminal accounts

For either law, and indeed for any array on E80 with the correct flow,
put K_ij=lambda_ij for finite i!=j, mu_i=lambda_(i,star),
nu_i=lambda_(star,i), and h_n=0 as NOTATION only. The full polynomial is

    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-sum_i mu_i<x_0,g_i>
                                  +sum_(i,j)C_ij<g_i,g_j>,
    C_ii=mu_i+sum_v K_iv-1_(i=n)-h_i*sum_(v>i)K_iv,
    2C_ij=-(K_ij+K_ji)-h_j*sum_(v>j)K_iv
                    +h_i[mu_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv], i<j.    (5.1)

To check the diagonal, half the total incident multiplier mass equals
mu_i+sum_v K_iv-1_(i=n), by flow including nu_i. Forward rows out of i
contribute the additional -h_i sum_(v>i)K_iv. For i<j, squared gradient
differences give the first term, forward rows out of i crossing j give
the second, and rows out of j reaching index<=i or the optimum give
the third. Incoming optimum rows have no initial mixed term. Thus no
nu_i or displacement contribution has been silently omitted.

Let e be the all-ones gradient column and Ppos80_i=sum_(t<i)h_t. Then

    U80=sum_i mu_i=2+sum_i nu_i,
    V80=e^T C e=D+sum_i nu_i(1+Ppos80_i).                 (5.2)

For a direct check, set g_i=1 and f_i=X-Ppos80_i-1/2. All finite Q rows
and outgoing optimum rows vanish, while Q_(star,i)=1-X+Ppos80_i.
Substitution yields qX^2-U80 X+V80, proving (5.2). The initial/common
block is B80=[[q,-U80/2],[-U80/2,V80]]. In particular neither old Huber
contact derivative nor the old D/16 slack may be assumed for these bases.

For any variation v of the gradients orthogonal to e, put
u80=mu^T v and k80=e^T C v. If Delta80=q V80-U80^2/4>0, exact
elimination of the ONE initial/common block charges

    [V80*u80^2/4-U80*u80*k80/2+q*k80^2]/Delta80.       (5.3)

The remaining quadratic is v^T C v minus (5.3). This formula includes
all initial and common cross terms in one global charge. At Delta80=0
one must instead impose the range condition and use the corresponding
singular Schur complement. At the explicit q_I and q_II, the square
identities (I.4) and (II.5) directly establish PSD without assuming away
that condition; (II.5) in particular makes the common block positive
definite. At the desired q, Section7 directly contradicts full PSD.

Law I has outgoing terminal probabilities1/2 at star and jlast. Its
actual terminal square, from expansion of its rows or fact1252(c), is

    M_I(q)=||g_n-Vlast||^2+Rlast,
    Vlast=x_n-(x_(jlast)-g_(jlast))/2,
    Rlast=q||x_0||^2-[f_(jlast)-||g_(jlast)||^2/2]-||Vlast||^2
           +sum_(i!=j in {star,0,...,n-1})lambda_ij Q_ij.  (5.4)

The negative square cost in that prefix remainder is retained by (I.4)
and (5.1); no terminal gradient is fixed by a quotient assumption.

Law II has incoming optimum-terminal mass n*eta80. Define

    Aterm=1+n*eta80,
    Bterm=(1+n*eta80/2)x_n
                       -(eta80/2)sum_(i<n)(x_i-g_i)
         =x_0-sum_(t<n)[h_t(1+eta80(t+1)/2)-eta80/2]g_t,
    delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.

Its exact ACTUAL-terminal completion is

    M_II(q)=Aterm||g_n-Bterm/Aterm||^2+Rprefix,
    Rprefix=q||x_0||^2+sum_(i!=j in {star,0,...,n-1})lambda_ij Q_ij
                       -eta80*sum_(i<n)delta_i-||Bterm||^2/Aterm. (5.5)

Expanding 2Q_(n,star)+eta80 sum_(i<n)Q_(n,i)+n eta80 Q_(star,n)
proves (5.5): the terminal gradient coefficient is1+n eta80, its
linear coefficient is -2Bterm, and every f_n term cancels -2f_n.
The original prefix flow cancels the remaining -eta80 sum f_i.
Both initial and terminal costs are present. The probability formula
for an incoming-terminal-free certificate is deliberately NOT applied
to Law II's different chosen support.

## 6. A complete restricted-row transcript at every physical index

This construction tests ALL coefficient choices on E80, not merely the
two explicit laws. It also shows why full boundary metrics cannot fix
missing rows by themselves. Define

    Rreset=a*c^2/Mleaf=beta*c/Mleaf,
    sbit(k)=number of ones in the binary expansion of integer k>=0,
    v80_k=(-1)^k*Rreset^k*c^(-sbit(k))/Mleaf,
                                      0<=k<Nleaf.                   (6.1)

Here sbit(0)=0. This is a closed formula, never an executed recurrence.
If r=v2(k+1), binary carry gives sbit(k+1)-sbit(k)=1-r. Therefore

    v80_(k+1)/v80_k=-Rreset*c^(r-1)
               =-a*c^(r+1)/Mleaf=-d_(1+r)/Mleaf.        (6.2)

For each leaf index2k prescribe the REAL scalar jets

    x_(2k)=Mleaf*v80_k,  g_(2k)=v80_k,
    f_(2k)=(b+1/2)*v80_k^2.

For each queried checkpoint2k+1<n prescribe

    x_(2k+1)=v80_k, g_(2k+1)=v80_k, f_(2k+1)=v80_k^2/2.     (6.3)

The leaf update gives Mleaf*v80_k-b*v80_k=v80_k. The following bridge update
gives v80_k-(1+d_(1+v2(k+1)))*v80_k=Mleaf*v80_(k+1), by (6.2).
Thus these are the EXACT original physical GD relations. In particular
x_0=Mleaf*v80_0=1; there is no freedom to change the radius in the proof.

After the final physical leaf the actual terminal position is

    t80=x_n=v80_(Nleaf-1)
       =-Rreset^(Nleaf-1)/(Mleaf*c^(m-1)).               (6.4)

The sign is negative because Nleaf-1 is odd. Its binary expansion has
m-1 ones. This gives (6.4) directly from (6.1), including the last leaf.
Complete that terminal with

    u80term=g_n=2t80/(b+2),
    f_n=t80^2/(b+2),
    x_star=g_star=f_star=0.                            (6.5)

There is no assertion of a single function interpolating these jets.
Their scalar Gram matrix is PSD because all vectors are literal scalars.

We now check EVERY row type in E80, including orientations and the
terminal; no numerical check or finite-horizon inference is used.

At a leaf with parameter v,

    Q_(leaf,star)=0, Q_(star,leaf)=-b*v^2.

At a checkpoint with parameter w, x=g=w and f=w^2/2, so both optimum
rows and all ordered checkpoint-checkpoint rows are zero. For EVERY
leaf v and checkpoint w, regardless of order or distance,

    Q_(leaf(v),check(w))=0,
    Q_(check(w),leaf(v))=b*v*(w-v).                    (6.6)

These formulas follow by substituting (6.3). They also hold with w=0
for the optimum. A neighboring checkpoint AFTER a leaf has w=v,
so both adjacent rows vanish. At a checkpoint BEFORE the next leaf,
v=-d*w/Mleaf with d>0; hence

    b*v*(w-v)=-b*(d/Mleaf)*(1+d/Mleaf)*w^2<0.          (6.7)

These are ALL adjacent prefix incidences, so all of them satisfy Q<=0.

Put u=u80term. Equation (6.5) gives

    f_n-u*t80+u^2/2=-b*u^2/4.

For each queried checkpoint, including the optimum, its outgoing
terminal row is therefore -b*u^2/4. For ANY leaf parameter v the
terminal row is exactly

    Q_(n,leaf(v))=-b*(v-u/2)^2<=0.                    (6.8)

Indeed its expression is f_n-u*t80+u^2/2+b*u*v-b*v^2.
The remaining incoming optimum-terminal row is
Q_(star,n)=-f_n+u^2/2=-b*u^2/4<=0.
This checks all row types(a)--(e), every optimum row, every leaf,
every checkpoint, and the actual unqueried terminal.

## 7. All-depth growth, exact lower obstruction for E80, and failed target

This section is entirely symbolic. Since
Mleaf=lambda-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)],

    Rreset-1
      =epsilon*[2/(lambda(1+epsilon))-lambda*epsilon]/Mleaf.

With epsilon<1/8 and lambda<5/2 the bracket is greater than
32/45-5/16=287/720>5/16. Also Mleaf<5/2. Thus

    Rreset>1+epsilon/8=1+1/(32m).                     (7.1)

The integer inequality 2^(m-1)-1>=96m^2 holds for m>=16. At16 it is
32767>=24576. If it holds at m, then
2^m-1=2(2^(m-1)-1)+1>=192m^2+1>=96(m+1)^2,
because 96m^2-192m-95>0 for m>=16. This proves the assertion by induction.
All our m are in that range. Consequently

    Nleaf-1>=96m^2>32m(2m+1).

Bernoulli's binomial inequality gives (1+1/(32m))^(32m)>=2. Together
with (7.1), this yields Rreset^(Nleaf-1)>2^(2m+1). Since Mleaf<4 and
c<4, one has Mleaf*c^(m-1)<2^(2m). Formula (6.4) now gives

    |t80|>2,
    2f_n=2t80^2/(b+2)>8/(b+2)>16/7>2.               (7.2)

The exact expression, stronger than this convenient coarse bound, is

    Qrelax80(m)=2*Rreset^(2Nleaf-2)
                      /[(b+2)*Mleaf^2*c^(2m-2)].       (7.3)

For ANY nonnegative array supported on E80 with the required original
value flow, evaluate its full polynomial on (6.1)--(6.5). Its initial
norm is1 and EVERY selected interpolation row is nonpositive. Therefore

    M_cert(q) <= q-Qrelax80(m).

Full PSD forces

    q>=Qrelax80(m)>2, for EVERY m>=1536.               (7.4)

This is a support-level impossibility proof attempt: arbitrary weights,
both optimum directions, arbitrary context, arbitrary matrix boundary
potentials, complete checkpoint couplings and the full terminal fan do
not escape it if their final row support stays inside E80. A free
nonnegative square added to a storage derivation does not change this
argument: after telescoping, the original-row certificate itself must
still be PSD and is tested by the displayed original vectors and values.

The requested q=17/(16D) is less than1 since S>=b>1 and D>3.
Thus (7.4) excludes it by a large margin for both mechanisms and their
whole specified coefficient family. This is not merely a bad choice of
eta80 or of the context weights. It is also not a contradiction with
the genuine full certificates (I.4), (II.5): their explicit levels are
necessarily at least (7.3), and the same calculation forces that fact.
No eigenvalue computation or automated recurrence was used to reach it.

At epsilon=0, Rreset=1 and (6.4) gives t80=-lambda^(-m). The growth
in (7.2) specifically comes from the small positive SAME-m deformation
repeated across all leaves; it is not an objection to the undeformed
silver schedule's full certificate.

## 8. Exact omitted prefix row and the missing boundary inequality

The transcript is provably NOT a legal primal instance. Let

    pbad=n-2=2(Nleaf-2)+1, ellbad=0,
    vfirst=v80_0=1/Mleaf,
    wbad=v80_(Nleaf-2)=-Mleaf*t80/d_1.

Here pbad is an actual queried checkpoint, and ellbad is the actual
initial leaf. Since t80<-2, d_1=1-epsilon<1 and Mleaf>2,
wbad>2Mleaf>vfirst. Formula (6.6) gives the explicit forbidden row

    Q_(pbad,0)=b*vfirst*(wbad-vfirst)>0.                (8.1)

This is a remote queried-prefix row, neither an incoming-terminal row
nor a duplicate-gradient constraint. There is no way to infer its sign
from E80: the complete transcript just checked is a counterexample to
that inference. The reverse row Q_(0,pbad) is present and equals zero.
This directional distinction is essential.

One economical new support direction is the nonnegative symmetric pair
Q_(pbad,0)+Q_(0,pbad). It has zero value flow and acts positively by
(8.1) on this transcript. But it is indefinite on the original formal
space. For ANY queried u<v its full polynomial is

    Q_uv+Q_vu=||g_u-g_v||^2
                 -<g_u-g_v,sum_(u<=t<v)h_t g_t>.      (8.2)

Setting only g_u nonzero gives (1-h_u)||g_u||^2, which is negative
when h_u>1; setting only g_v nonzero gives ||g_v||^2>0. Thus adding
this row pair is not a free PSD repair and starts a genuinely new
unallocated coefficient mechanism. No such third mechanism is attempted.

The precise unresolved boundary construction is as follows. On the SAME
Hword_m, find nonnegative original multipliers with full flow and genuine
remote checkpoint-to-leaf or other excluded prefix rows such that, with
C and mu formed by (5.1),

    C - (4q)^(-1)mu*mu^T >= 0 on ALL gradient coordinates,
    q=17/(16D),                                           (8.3)

or any other rigorously sufficient original-benchmark-beating level.
Every actual terminal and initial cost must remain in C and mu. Formula
(8.3) is the exact Schur condition for q>0. It is a theorem-level missing
inequality, not a technicality or an already constructed certificate.

At a minimum, any such law must supply enough positive action outside
E80 on (6.1)--(6.5). Writing lambda^out for its excluded rows gives the
explicit necessary boundary payment

    sum_(ij outside E80)lambda^out_ij*Q_ij(transcript)
          >= Qrelax80(m)-q
                       -sum_(ij in E80)lambda_ij*Q_ij(transcript)
          >= Qrelax80(m)-q.                            (8.4)

This payment is necessary, never sufficient: (8.2) shows the costs on
other original directions. A full solution requires one analytic all-depth
law paying (8.3) while meeting (8.4), not unknown feasible SDP blocks or
an unproved cone-invariance assertion. Allowing even all checkpoint
gradients in the boundary state does not establish those missing rows.

## 9. Retrieval, novelty boundaries, and final status

The prior H4 and deep-wave tests were recalled from max79 finding
35290b8fa6113ce5 and the earlier own backward-path report. They were used
as screens, not correctness premises. Neither old base enters this report.
The older repeated-copy template already warned about a deformation
accumulating over leaves; novelty is NOT claimed for that qualitative
warning. Here the closed transcript, every retained row orientation, its
free terminal completion, and the bound for arbitrary arrays on E80 were
derived jointly. In particular this obstruction survives changing the
entire inherited base, rather than only adding nonnegative weights to it.

Query-memory, direct-proving, construct-counterexamples, and
identify-key-failures skills were used. The mathematical portions were
kept as one unverified package because TASK.md explicitly closed formal
calls. Matlas was queried once for long-step boundary/Lyapunov certificates;
the returned bounded-step results do not apply. One web call provided
primary-literature context. The already downloaded primary text
arXiv:2309.16530, Section3, Theorem3.4 and its proof in Sections3.1--3.2,
was read for the gluing architecture. Its Q orientation differs from
ours; its result is for its own undeformed schedule and is NOT imported
as a theorem. No external theorem is used as a proof premise here.

Both analytical mechanisms are exhausted in their stated scopes. Their
literal arrays, signs, flow, all-space identities, ancestor-dependent
products, terminal completions and global mixed costs are explicit.
They pass the inherited screens and fail the target for a new, more
general reason: the restricted rows permit incompatible leaf thresholds.
The original unrestricted minimax problem remains OPEN. No theorem was
submitted, no new fact landed, no full-function competitive bound was
obtained, and no negative minimax resolution is claimed. Return this
bounded invocation; no automatic renewal or third law is authorized.
