# Variable-step dense certificates and a complete alternating-family obstruction

Status: complete joint proof DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is max2 round36,
the second and final analytical round allocated after completed34 by
TASK.md and master_guidance604b16e76c7981e3. Scope update
c3f16cd4617d4d93 and source-scope note60f0c805e0e856b2 leave that
ceiling unchanged. The latter's required ordered-Q translation and
fresh variable-step proof are supplied explicitly below. No numerical evaluation,
solver, machine algebra, replay, formal-verifier call, installation,
consult or subordinate agent was used. The original Goal remains OPEN.

The frozen pairs remain r=5,b=1 and r=6,b=1. Here b in the phrase
"ray b=1" is the integer ray parameter; the alternating step excess
called b below is a different, explicitly local real parameter.

Accepted inputs: fact04219554e41d9750, Sections2,3,10--13, for the
benchmark recurrences, balanced primitive scalar rule, full original
certificate identity, physical joins and substitution; and
fact1252b8823bb5a290 for the original smooth interpolation convention.
No unverified shared result is a premise. All new claims used together
are proved in this single draft. The earlier round35 triangle and
two-tight-cut arguments are not used.

This work changes the attempted certificate mechanism. Both strict
triangular halves of the auxiliary multiplier array are factorized,
with factors depending on the actual variable steps. The whole matrix
remainder is zero, and every ordered multiplier is retained. Two
nonconstant, nonbasic physical words have complete strictly positive
arrays of this kind. They are weak seeds: the ENTIRE alternating
specialization of this declared positive-factor, zero-remainder family
has capacity below the appropriate benchmark. This is an obstruction
to the declared ansatz, not to every full certificate or every seed.

All new local notation in this document has the implicit suffix _ls36.
In particular U_i,D_i below are factor sequences, not the benchmark
U_n^ZJ or any earlier trajectory bound. P_i is prefix capacity, R_i
is a reciprocal product, and p_i is an auxiliary scalar product.

## 1. Original certificate, orientation, and the chosen family

For a word h of length r, let s=sum_(i<r)h_i and A=1+s. Retain all
r+1 independent formal gradients g_0,...,g_r and all formal values f_i.
Set

 x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,
 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
 V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)
       +sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sA||g_r||^2.

The accepted full certificate interface is

 Lambda_ij>=0 (i!=j), Lambda_ii=0,
 e=(1,...,1)^T,
 T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
 L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
 L_flow^T e=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,
 D_prim=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<r, -s(s+2)),
 M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2 >=0 on R^(r+1).       (1)

Accepted04219554e41d9750 proves the original-space identity

 V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>.      (2)

Introduce the differently oriented residual

 Qbar_ij=-2Q_ji
        =2(f_i-f_j)-2<g_j,x_i-x_j>-||g_i-g_j||^2.

We search for an EXACT identity

                       V_h=sum_(i!=j)mu_ij Qbar_ij.          (3)

Then the original array is Lambda_ij=2mu_ji, not 2mu_ij, and its
remainder is M_prim=0 on the whole original space.

The declared family has strictly positive excesses t_i=h_i-1, i<r.
Choose positive U_0,...,U_(r-1) and D_1,...,D_r, set U_-1=0 and
D_(r+1)=0. Set t_r=0 and h_r=1 solely as auxiliary placeholders in
expressions multiplied by D_(r+1) or an empty sum. They are not an
extra physical step. For every 0<=i<j<=r define

 mu_ij=(U_i-U_(i-1))(D_j+t_j D_(j+1)),
mu_ji=(U_(i-1)+t_i U_i)(D_j-D_(j+1)).                      (4)

Equivalently, the original ordered entries are, for every i<j,

 Lambda_ij=2(U_(i-1)+t_i U_i)(D_j-D_(j+1)),
 Lambda_ji=2(U_i-U_(i-1))(D_j+t_j D_(j+1)).                 (4a)

These formulas define EVERY off-diagonal entry, and mu_ii=0. The
family to be excluded below consists of all choices of these positive
factors for which all entries are nonnegative and identity(3) holds.
It is not assumed that every original certificate admits this form.

Nonnegative entries in(4) force U_i>=U_(i-1) and D_j>=D_(j+1):
use j=r in the first formula, and i=0 in the second, respectively.
Conversely these two monotonicities make every entry nonnegative.

## 2. Why the variable-step factorization cancels all mixed terms

For i<j the two telescoping sums are

 sum_(a<=i)mu_(a,j)=U_i(D_j+t_j D_(j+1)),
 sum_(a>j)mu_(a,i)=(U_(i-1)+t_i U_i)D_(j+1).

The coefficient of <g_i,g_j> in sum mu Qbar is exactly

 2(mu_ij+mu_ji)
 -2h_i sum_(a<=i)mu_(a,j)+2h_j sum_(a>j)mu_(a,i).           (5)

Expansion of(4) gives

 mu_ij+mu_ji
 =h_i U_i(D_j+t_j D_(j+1))
       -h_j(U_(i-1)+t_i U_i)D_(j+1),

so(5) is zero for every pair. This calculation allows unequal steps;
it is not an appeal to the constant-step source theorem.

Let d_i=sum_(j!=i)(mu_ij-mu_ji), so that the coefficient of f_i is
2d_i. For i<r put p_(i+1)=U_i D_(i+1), with p_0=0. The weights
entering i from smaller and larger indices are, respectively,

I_i=U_(i-1)(D_i+t_i D_(i+1)) for 1<=i<r,
 J_i=(U_(i-1)+t_i U_i)D_(i+1),

with I_0=0. Therefore

              t_i J_i-I_i=t_i^2 p_(i+1)-p_i.                (6)

If d_i=h_i, the coefficient of ||g_i||^2 is

 -d_i-2I_i-2J_i+2h_i J_i
                   =-h_i+2(t_i^2 p_(i+1)-p_i).             (7)

At the terminal, d_r=-s, the incoming weight is p_r, and its squared
gradient coefficient is s-2p_r. These formulas also directly check the
terminal orientation of the original array.

## 3. Complete variable-step construction with explicit sign tests

For given t_i>0 define

 P_k=1+sum_(i<k)(1+t_i),
 R_k=product_(i<k)t_i^(-1),
 p_k=(R_k^2-1)/2,       0<=k<=r.

Thus P_0=R_0=1 and P_r=A. Assume

 R_r=P_r,
 R_k>1 and P_k>R_k for 1<=k<r.                             (8)

Set U_0=1, and for 1<=k<r set

 U_k/U_(k-1)
   = (P_k+R_k)(1+R_(k+1))/((1+R_k)(P_k-R_k)),
 D_k=p_k/U_(k-1) for 1<=k<=r.                              (9)

All these factors are positive. Their successive D ratios are

 D_(k+1)/D_k
   = (P_k-R_k)(R_(k+1)-1)/((R_k-1)(P_k+R_k)).              (10)

For the exact sign check, U_k>=U_(k-1) and D_k>=D_(k+1) are equivalent
to the following two scalar inequalities for each 1<=k<r:

 -R_k(2+R_k+R_(k+1))
       <=P_k(R_(k+1)-R_k)
       <=R_k(R_(k+1)+R_k-2).                               (11)

No matrix PSD test is being replaced by these inequalities: the FULL
matrix will be proved identically zero. Inequalities(11) concern all
of the actual coefficient signs of this particular factorization.

Here is the complete value-flow verification. Define

 u_k=(sum_(i<k)h_i U_i)/U_k,
 v_k=(sum_(j=k+2)^r h_(j-1)D_j)/D_(k+1).

The exact identities are

 u_k=(P_k-R_k)/(1+R_(k+1)),
 v_k=(P_(k+1)-R_(k+1))/(R_(k+1)-1).                        (12)

For u, start from u_0=0 and use
u_k=(U_(k-1)/U_k)(u_(k-1)+h_(k-1)). Inserting the preceding
formula for u_(k-1) gives
u_(k-1)+h_(k-1)=(P_k+R_k)/(1+R_k), and then(9) proves(12).
For v, start from v_(r-1)=0, which agrees with R_r=P_r, and use
v_(k-1)=(D_(k+1)/D_k)(h_k+v_k). The factor h_k+v_k equals
(P_k+R_k)/(R_(k+1)-1). Substitution in(10) proves the asserted
formula for v_(k-1). Thus both identities hold, including the endpoints.

The sum of the flows over indices 0,...,k telescopes across that cut:

 sum_(i<=k)d_i
   =p_(k+1)(1-t_k+v_k-u_k)=P_(k+1)-1.                     (13)

To check the last equality explicitly, write R=R_(k+1), P=P_k,
t=t_k, so that R_k=tR and P_(k+1)=P+1+t. Then

 p_(k+1)(v_k-u_k)
   =[(R+1)(P+1+t-R)-(R-1)(P-tR)]/2
   =P+t+(t-1)(R^2-1)/2.

Adding p_(k+1)(1-t) leaves P+t=P_(k+1)-1, as claimed.
Differences of(13) give d_i=h_i for i<r, and total flow zero gives
d_r=-s. The function coefficients are now all correct.

The definition of R gives

        t_i^2 p_(i+1)-p_i=(1-t_i^2)/2.

Equation(7) becomes -h_i+1-t_i^2=-h_i t_i, the required coefficient
in V_h. At the terminal, R_r=A gives p_r=(A^2-1)/2, so
s-2p_r=-sA. Section2 cancels all mixed products. This proves(3) as
a polynomial identity in ALL values and ALL original gradients.

Consequently(8)--(11), together with(4) and(9), supply a full original
certificate with Lambda=2mu^T and M_prim=0, with no equality or
identification of formal coordinates. Strict inequalities in the two
factor monotonicities give strictly positive entries on all r(r+1)
ordered off-diagonal pairs.

## 4. Necessary restrictions on the ENTIRE declared factorized family

This section does not assume the special construction(8)--(9). Start
with ANY positive factors in(4) for which all entries are nonnegative
and the original flow and zero-remainder identity hold.

The value coefficients give d_i=h_i for i<r and d_r=-s. Comparing(7)
with the desired -h_i t_i yields

             t_i^2(2p_(i+1)+1)=2p_i+1,   p_0=0.           (14)

At the terminal, s-2p_r=-sA gives 2p_r+1=A^2. Multiplying(14)
therefore proves the necessary exact balance

                         A product_(i<r)t_i=1.             (15)

The positive factors make p_1=U_0D_1>0, so(14) at i=0 forces
0<t_0<1. When r>=2 define

 z=(sum_(j=2)^r h_(j-1)D_j)/D_1>0.

The first value flow is h_0=p_1(1-t_0+z). Substitution of
p_1=(t_0^(-2)-1)/2 gives

                z=(t_0^2+2t_0-1)/(1-t_0)>0.

It follows that EVERY member of this declared family with r>=2 obeys

                         sqrt(2)-1<t_0<1.                  (16)

This is a necessary condition on this positive-factor representation,
not a claimed necessary condition on all primitive schedules.

As a concrete failed high-mass migration, put t=(1/3,b,1/3,b,1/3),
where b is the unique positive root of 2b^3+7b^2-27=0. The polynomial
is increasing on b>0 and has values -9/2 at 3/2 and 46/27 at 5/3.
Thus 3/2<b<5/3, A=7+2b>10, and(15) holds exactly. Nevertheless the
required z above would be -1/3. It cannot equal a strictly positive
weighted sum. Unit-quadratic balance and a large capacity alone do
not extend the source factorization. This diagnostic is NOT asserted
to pass every other primitive test or to be a full-cone survivor.

## 5. Scope-complete obstruction at five alternating steps

Consider every member of the declared factorized family whose actual
physical word is

                  h=(1+a,1+b,1+a,1+b,1+a),  a,b>0.

Here A=6+3a+2b. Equations(15)--(16) force

         A a^3 b^2=1,       sqrt(2)-1<a<1.                 (17)

We prove the strict cap

                              A<29/3.                      (18)

Suppose instead A>=29/3 and write b=(A-6-3a)/2. For fixed
a in [sqrt(2)-1,1], the expression

                    F(A,a)=A a^3(A-6-3a)^2/4

is strictly increasing in A on A>=29/3. All factors are positive
there. At A=29/3, the derivative of a^3(11/3-3a)^2 has sign
11/3-5a on this interval: its exact derivative is
3a^2(11/3-3a)(11/3-5a). Thus its minimum is attained at one of
the two endpoints. At a=1, F(29/3,1)=29/27>1. At a=sqrt(2)-1,
direct multiplication gives

 F(29/3,sqrt(2)-1)=(77285sqrt(2)-109243)/54>1,

because

              2*77285^2-109297^2=108241>0.

Both quantities being compared before squaring are positive. Hence
F(A,a)>1 throughout the assumed region, contradicting(17). This
proves(18) for the ENTIRE declared five-step alternating family,
with arbitrary positive factors and all of their allowed boundary
zero multipliers. No recurrence(9) or extra symmetry of the array is
needed for this obstruction.

## 6. Scope-complete obstruction at six alternating steps

Now consider every member of the same declared family whose word is

               h=(1+a,1+b,1+a,1+b,1+a,1+b),  a,b>0.

Here A=7+3a+3b, and(15)--(16) give

            A(ab)^3=1,       sqrt(2)-1<a<1.                (19)

We prove

                              A<23/2.                      (20)

If A>=23/2, write b=(A-7)/3-a. For each fixed admissible a,
A[a((A-7)/3-a)]^3 is strictly increasing in A in this region.
At A=23/2 the product a(3/2-a) is a positive concave quadratic
in a, so its minimum on [sqrt(2)-1,1] occurs at an endpoint.
The endpoint a=1 gives A(ab)^3=23/16>1. The other gives

 (23/2)[(sqrt(2)-1)(5/2-sqrt(2))]^3
                    =(54901sqrt(2)-77625)/16>1,

since

                2*54901^2-77641^2=114721>0.

Again all compared sides are positive. This contradicts(19), proving
(20) for the entire declared six-step alternating family.

## 7. Two explicit nonbasic certificates; all 30/42 signs and full PSD

The exclusions above are not the consequence of an empty ansatz. We
now give one complete nonconstant algebraic certificate at each length.
Formulas(4),(8)--(9) are finite explicit formulas for EVERY multiplier;
no linear system, optimizer or uncomputed PSD condition remains.

Five steps: set a=2/3 and let b be the unique positive root of

                         16b^3+64b^2-27=0.

The polynomial is strictly increasing on b>0. Its values at 3/5 and
5/8 are -63/125 and 61/32, so

       3/5<b<5/8,
       h=(5/3,1+b,5/3,1+b,5/3),
       A=8+2b,       46/5<A<37/4.                          (21)

The polynomial equation is exactly A(2/3)^3 b^2=1, hence R_5=A.
Define the factors by(9) and all 30 original multipliers by(4) and
Lambda_ij=2mu_ji.

Six steps: set a=2/3 and let b be the unique positive root of

                          8b^4+24b^3-9=0.

Its derivative is positive on b>0. At b=2/3 the value is -25/81;
at b=7/10 it is 1441/1250. Therefore

       2/3<b<7/10,
       h=(5/3,1+b,5/3,1+b,5/3,1+b),
       A=9+3b,       11<A<111/10.                          (22)

Its defining equation is exactly A[(2/3)b]^3=1, hence R_6=A.
Use(9),(4) to define all 42 original multipliers.

We check EVERY hypothesis in Section3 with exact bounds. In both
examples all t_i lie in (or at the interior rational point of)
[3/5,7/10], and R_k>1 because t_i<1. The strict prefix inequalities
P_k>R_k in the five-step case follow from

 P_1=8/3>3/2=R_1,
 P_2>64/15>5/2>R_2,
 P_3>89/15>15/4>R_3,
 P_4-R_4=(A-5)/3>0.

In the six-step case they follow from

 P_1=8/3>3/2=R_1,
 P_2>13/3>9/4>R_2,
 P_3>6>27/8>R_3,
 P_4>23/3>81/16>R_4,
 P_5-R_5=(1-b)A-(1+b)>8/5.

The last inequalities use terminal balance, not an approximate root.
Thus every denominator in(9)--(10) is strictly positive.

Because R_(k+1)>R_k, the first inequality in(11) is strict. For
the second inequality define

 E_k=(1+t_k)R_k-(1-t_k)P_k-2t_k.

It is exactly the second sign inequality after multiplication by
positive factors. At k=1, direct substitution in both examples gives

                       E_1=(13b-7)/6>2/15.

For k>=2 the common rational interval yields

 E_k >= H_k:=(8/5)(10/7)^k-9/5-17k/25.

Here R_k>=(10/7)^k, P_k<=1+17k/10, 1+t_k>=8/5,
1-t_k<=2/5 and 2t_k<=7/5. The lower bound is strictly positive:

 H_2=129/1225>0,
 H_(k+1)-H_k=(24/35)(10/7)^k-17/25>=1/175>0.

Consequently U_0>0, all successive U differences are positive,
all successive D differences are positive, and D_r>0=D_(r+1).
Every one of the 30 or 42 original off-diagonal entries is STRICTLY
positive. Section3 proves the exact flows and identity(3), so in the
notation of(1)

                         M_prim=0 on R^6 or R^7.            (23)

This is a full-space PSD statement, not a compressed Hessian test.
There are no zero physical steps in these examples and no coordinates
were deleted.

These words are genuinely nonbasic in the exact ConPP grammar of
accepted04219554e41d9750. To see this without a numerical comparison,
at any index j set P=1+sum_(i<j)h_i and D=1+sum_(i>j)h_i. Then
PD>=P+D-1=A-h_j. In the five-step example h_j<=5/3 and A>46/5,
and in the six-step example h_j<17/10 and A>11. In both cases

                  PD-A(h_j-1)>=A(2-h_j)-h_j>0.              (24)

But a nonempty word obtained by an exact ConPP join at its root has
some bridge h_j=1+d with dA=PD, by the defining identity in the
accepted join. Equation(24) rules out every such root. The examples
are also nonconstant since b differs from 2/3 by its isolating interval.
They are therefore actual dense nonbasic seeds, with a proved capacity
deficit rather than a claimed asymptotic gain.

## 8. Correct fixed-ray comparison and a finite uniform deficit

Let lambda=1+sqrt(2). The accepted scalar rules give

 C_2=Omega(1,lambda), C_5=lambda C_2,
 C_6=Omega(C_2,lambda^2).

The positive root C_2 satisfies X^2-(1+lambda)X-lambda=0.
We have lambda>239/99 because 2*99^2>140^2. At X=803/200,
the strict negativity of that polynomial follows from

                  239*200600-99*484209=6709>0.

Thus C_2>803/200. Also lambda>241/100, and hence

       C_5>193523/20000>29/3,
       C_5/(29/3)>580569/580000=1+569/580000.               (25)

For the six-step comparison, C_2>4 and lambda^2>29/5. The
polynomial for Omega(4,29/5) has value -23/80 at X=47/4,
so its positive root exceeds 47/4. Monotonicity of Omega yields

              C_6>47/4>23/2,
              C_6/(23/2)>47/46=1+1/46.                   (26)

The ray lower comparison needed here is

                         Z_m>=C_(m-1) Z_1                 (27)

for every positive integer m. Here is a direct proof from the original
recurrences, with no continuous phase limit. Expand a maximizing B_n
tree. Replace EACH primitive empty leaf by an original maximizing
primitive tree of length m-1 and its ONE dominant empty leaf by a
maximizing dominant tree of that same length. The resulting original
tree has m(n+1)-1 internal nodes. The primitive masses are C_(m-1)
and the terminal dominant mass is B_(m-1)>=C_(m-1). Homogeneity and
monotonicity at each join give

                    B_(m(n+1)-1)>=C_(m-1) B_n.

Take n=2^j-1, divide by lambda^j, and use the accepted integer-ray
limits. This proves(27). Thus every actual seed in the declared
alternating families satisfies the STRICT WRONG-WAY comparisons

 r=5: Z_6/(A Z_1)>1+569/580000,
 r=6: Z_7/(A Z_1)>1+1/46.                                 (28)

In particular neither family can satisfy the required A>Z_6/Z_1 or
A>Z_7/Z_1 on the frozen b=1 rays. These comparisons do not transfer
the four-step193/25 threshold or assume any unproved phase optimum.

There is a stronger finite physical statement. Let h be ANY actual
certificate from one of these families. Apply the accepted physical
substitution to a fixed maximizing B_n tree, using this seed of
capacity A. Keep the ONE dominant empty leaf untouched. The actual
word H_A has length mn, with m=r+1, and exact all-function value

                          W_(mn)(H_A)=1/D_A.

Let C=C_r. Repeat the SAME substitution using an original maximizing
basic primitive seed of length r and capacity C. This produces an
original basic dominant tree of length mn and mass D_C, so
B_(mn)>=D_C. The accepted original-coordinate joins and final square
remain in both constructions. Since C>A, monotonicity at the joins
gives D_C>D_A for every n>=1. Therefore

                W_(mn)(H_A)>1/B_(mn),  EVERY n>=1.          (29)

The square-root defect bound also gives, for n>=1,

 B_(mn)W_(mn)(H_A)
   >=[(sqrt(C B_n)-(sqrt(C)-1))^2]/(A B_n)
   >=(C/A)(1-B_n^(-1/2))^2.                               (30)

This pays for the unchanged dominant leaf explicitly. Both(25) and
(26) imply C/A>1+1/2000. On n=2^j-1, the accepted elementary
bound B_n>=2n+1 and 2^30>10^9 imply, for every j>=30,
B_n>400000000, hence B_n^(-1/2)<1/20000. Inserting this in(30)
and using

 (1+1/2000)(1-1/20000)^2
   >(1+1/2000)(1-1/10000)>1+1/4000

proves the uniform finite statement

 W_(m(2^j-1))(H_A)
       >(1+1/4000) U_(m(2^j-1))^ZJ,  EVERY integer j>=30,
       m=6 or m=7 respectively.                            (31)

The actual horizons are 6*2^j-6 and 7*2^j-7. This is a deficit of
this explicit seed-substitution mechanism, not a lower bound against
arbitrary schedules and not a solution of the original Goal.

## 9. What is closed and what remains

The source-inspired ansatz has been fully attempted, not left as a
generic feasibility problem. It produces explicit nonconstant positive
physical words, all multipliers, exact flow and full zero PSD remainder.
It also has an exact scope-complete obstruction on both declared
alternating word families. Every member of those families loses even
to a same-length basic capacity, and its accepted physical lift is
uniformly weaker than the original benchmark at the stated true
horizons. No numerical non-discovery is being used as evidence.

The ansatz restrictions matter: positive triangular factors, the exact
zero remainder, strictly positive excesses, and one of the two stated
alternating word patterns. A general full PSD remainder, a different
word pattern within the factorized class, different triangular ranks,
or a signed component decomposition with nonnegative final entries is
outside this exclusion. None of those alternatives is asserted to
work. There is no lossless reduction from the unrestricted full cone
to the family studied here.

No original-scope improving seed or full asymptotic separation was
obtained in the two-round allocation. The whole assignment has reached
its analytical ceiling36 (attempted ceiling37 including the historical
interruption). This document does not renew an allocation, relaunch a
worker, claim an inspected runtime exit, or request an unauthorized
formal submission. Its mathematics remains an unverified joint draft.

## 10. Primary source and retrieval record

Source of the factorization IDEA only: Salah R. Chikhi,
"Last-Iterate Performance of Gradient Descent and Relaxed Proximal
Point via s-Composability", arXiv:2609.13765v2 (20 September2026),
https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13765v2, Section4, especially formulas(10)--(14).
The PDF was downloaded into this worker's downloads directory; all15
pages and the full constant-step certificate proof were read.

The paper's theorem concerns a CONSTANT h=1+t satisfying
(1+rh)t^r=1. Its residual Qbar_ij equals -2 times the project's Q_ji.
Its s-composability potential has additional terminal/optimum terms;
the history inequality in its Section4 is the intrinsic V_h used here.
Neither its constant-step result nor its proximal objective result is
silently used for a variable word or for the project's last iterate.
Sections2--7 above supply the complete variable-step proof instead.
Thus no additional external theorem is used as a black box or treated
as a fact-graph predecessor. If formal verification is later selected,
the source can be recorded as proof-pattern attribution, with the full
joint proof and accepted predecessors retained.

Matlas theorem searches returned unrelated statements twice; the
matched source was located through web search and opened on arXiv.
The first relative shared-store read failed with "No such file or
directory"; the absolute project shared store was then read.
The PDF text extraction command failed with "pdftotext: command not
found" and the consequent text search found no file. The already
present local pypdf reader then extracted all15 pages successfully.
No installation or mathematical computation was performed.

# C51: a full exact H3 certificate and explicit Huber certificate coordinates

Status: complete candidate proof, **not verifier-accepted**. No new verifier
allocation and no additional optimization call. The only mathematical
predecessor is accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290`. The numerical solve guided the
choice of rational coefficients; its values, eigenvalues and inferred
multipliers are not proof premises.

This supplies a concrete full-objective certificate for the actual m=3
schedule. It does not prove a growing-horizon certificate or resolve the
original exact-profile problem.

## 1. A lossless parameterization at the Huber value

Fix n>=1 and any nonnegative schedule h=(h0,...,h_(n-1)) with sum S>0.
Set P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t for 0<=i<=n, h_n=0, and tau=1/(2S+1).
Use the independent formal vectors (x0,g0,...,gn), the GD identities
xi=x0-sum_(t<i)h_t gt, and all ordered interpolation residuals

    Qij=fi-fj+<gi,xj-xi>+||gi-gj||^2/2,

with the optimum jet equal to zero. Every legal trajectory satisfies Qij<=0
by fact1252b8823bb5a290.

Choose an arbitrary array r_ij>=0 for 0<=i<n and 0<=j<=n, j!=i, and set
r_ii=0. These are all finite multipliers whose source is a queried prefix
sample; there is no support restriction among them. Define

    mu_i = [2h_i+sum_(j=0)^n r_ij(P_i-P_j)]/(2S-P_i),  i<n,             (1)
    mu_n = 2-sum_(i<n)mu_i,
    beta_i=mu_i+sum_(j=0)^n r_ij-sum_(k<n)r_ki,          i<n.            (2)

The denominators in (1) are positive, since 2S-P_i>=S>0. Define the
complete multiplier array by

    lambda_(star,i)=0,
    lambda_(i,star)=mu_i,                 0<=i<=n,
    lambda_ij=r_ij,                      0<=i<n, j finite, j!=i,
    lambda_(n,i)=beta_i,                 0<=i<n.                       (3)

For the moment mu and beta may have either sign. Their eventual
nonnegativity will be checked explicitly.

Let e_i be the standard basis of the real space of dimension n+1, indexed 0,...,n. Put

    p_i=sum_(t<i)h_t e_t,
    v_ij=p_i-p_j,
    A_i*= e_i e_i^T/2+(e_i p_i^T+p_i e_i^T)/2,
    A_ij=(e_i v_ij^T+v_ij e_i^T)/2
                           +(e_i-e_j)(e_i-e_j)^T/2,
    A=sum_i mu_i A_i*+sum_(i!=j finite)lambda_ij A_ij,
    M=[[tau,-mu^T/2],[-mu/2,A]],
    Z_hub=A-mu mu^T/(4tau),
    K_hub=Z_hub restricted to rows/columns 0,...,n-1.                          (4)

All matrices in (4) are full formal coefficient matrices, not restrictions
to a recovered primal trajectory. The exact identity is

    tau||x0||^2-2fn+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Qij
                       =(x0,g0,...,gn)^T M (x0,g0,...,gn).             (5)

Here vector-valued quadratic notation means the corresponding sum of
inner products in any finite-dimensional real Euclidean space.

The sufficient and necessary certificate tests in these coordinates are

         r_ij>=0,  mu_i>=0,  beta_i>=0,  K_hub>=0.                        (6)

More precisely, (6) is equivalent to existence of this full certificate,
and every full certificate at level tau is represented by some r.

### Proof of the identity and the nullspace condition

At a prefix finite node i<n, equation (2) makes outgoing minus incoming
flow zero. At n it is

    mu_n+sum_i beta_i-sum_i r_(i,n)
      =sum_i mu_i=2.

Thus every fi coefficient in (5) cancels. Expanding each Q gives exactly
the matrices in (4), including the initial-vector cross coefficient
-mu_i/2. This proves (5).

Let 1 denote the all-ones vector of length n+1, and define the finite drift
d_i=sum_(j finite)lambda_ij(P_i-P_j). Directly from (4) and the value flow,

              2(A1)_i=2h_i+(1+P_i)mu_i+d_i.                          (7)

For a detailed derivation, the term e_i v_ij^T+v_ij e_i^T contributes
lambda_ij(P_i-P_j) to coordinate i and
h_k lambda_ij(1_(k<i)-1_(k<j)) to coordinate k. The difference-square
term annihilates 1. For k<n, summing the latter cut term over all finite
edges gives h_k[2-sum_(i>k)mu_i] by finite value flow. The star terms
contribute (1+P_k)mu_k+h_k sum_(i>k)mu_i. The two tail sums cancel.
For k=n both cut terms vanish and h_n=0. This establishes (7) for all k.

For i<n, equations (1) and (7) imply

              (A1)_i=mu_i/(2tau).                                    (8)

Also sum_i mu_i=2, so the x0 row of M annihilates
v_H=(1,tau,...,tau).

To establish the last gradient row without assuming it, use the exact
Huber jets

    x0=1, xi=1-tau P_i, gi=tau,
    fi=tau xi-tau^2/2, f_star=g_star=x_star=0.                         (9)

They are the GD trajectory of the fixed scalar function that is x^2/2
on |x|<=tau and tau|x|-tau^2/2 elsewhere. Since P_i<=S, every finite
xi>=tau. Every finite-source Qij in (3) is zero, and 2fn=tau.
Evaluate the polynomial identity (5) at (9): v_H^T M v_H=0, without any
positivity assumption on the coefficients. All rows except possibly the
last already annihilate v_H, and its last coordinate tau is positive.
Consequently the last row also annihilates it. We have

              M v_H=0,  A1=mu/(2tau),  Z_hub 1=0.                    (10)

Completing the x0 square in (5) gives a positive initial square plus the
gradient form with matrix Z_hub. Since Z_hub 1=0, for every scalar coordinate vector
z=(z0,...,zn),

    z^T Z_hub z=(z0-zn,...,z_(n-1)-zn)^T K_hub
                                      (z0-zn,...,z_(n-1)-zn).         (11)

Hence M>=0 iff K_hub>=0. If the signs in (6) hold, (5) proves
2fn<=tau||x0||^2 on every legal trajectory in every dimension. The exact
Huber function (9) attains equality. Normalization from the predecessor
then gives W_n(h)=tau.

### Why these coordinates cover every tight full certificate

Let any nonnegative full certificate at level tau be given. Evaluate it
on (9). The only strict interpolation residuals are those leaving star:

    Q_(star,i)=-tau[1-tau(P_i+1)]<0,

because P_i<=S and 1-tau(S+1)=S/(2S+1)>0. The objective gap is zero.
Full PSD and nonnegative multipliers therefore force lambda_(star,i)=0
and Mv_H=0. The value flow implies sum mu_i=2. Applying (7) to the
prefix rows gives (1), and the flow at each prefix node gives (2).
Take r to be the actual finite prefix-source multipliers of the original
certificate. This reconstructs every coefficient, proving the asserted
losslessness. This argument does not delete incoming terminal edges.

## 2. The actual seven-step word and rational coefficients

Write t=sqrt(2), a=(1+t)/2 and b=1+8(1+t)/9. For the assigned m=3
parameters c_3=(4/3)(1+t), a_3=1/[2(1+t)], the actual word is exactly

    h=(a,5/3,a,b,a,5/3,a),
    S=(65+26t)/9,  tau=9/(139+52t).                                  (12)

No steps have been contracted or replaced. Use r=R_int/10^6, where R_int has
rows i=0,...,6 and columns j=0,...,7:

       0  12935   7196    7116   3081  2983  2929  3123
   54548      0   9076   29849  11139  7899  5596  5450
   30421  27844      0  227407   8150  7203  5823  5265
   20167 182039 356887       0   3112  3600  4498  4434
    9541  13214  13036   70218      0 16219  9699  7434
   11466  13010  10230  174850  98657     0  9221  9067
    6981   9672  10333  207590 167381 32994     0 13402

Equations (1)--(4) specify every remaining multiplier and every entry of
the exact 9x9 residual in Q(sqrt(2)). All finite off-diagonal r are
strictly positive. The following rational enclosures verify the other
signs; all displayed integer intervals have common denominator 10^6:

    mu_0 [98895,98896]       beta_0 [5134,5135]
    mu_1 [143370,143371]     beta_1 [8213,8214]
    mu_2 [106957,106958]     beta_2 [12312,12313]
    mu_3 [390815,390816]     beta_3 [248522,248523]
    mu_4 [179520,179521]     beta_4 [27361,27362]
    mu_5 [311655,311656]     beta_5 [567258,567259]
    mu_6 [352772,352773]     beta_6 [763359,763360]
    mu_7 [416014,416015]

The zero star-source multipliers here are an explicit algebraic choice
required by exact Huber complementarity. They are not a silent
truncation of the solver's small positive coefficients.

## 3. Full PSD, certified with a fixed rational interval calculation

Here is the precise arithmetic specification behind all the enclosures,
so no floating eigenvalue or optimization output is a premise. Take

    D=10^18,
    l=1414213562373095048, u=l+1.

The integer inequalities l^2<2D^2<u^2 put t in [l/D,u/D]. Represent
each intermediate interval by two integers [L,U] denoting [L/D,U/D].
Addition and negation use their exact endpoint rules. For multiplication,
take the minimum and maximum of the four endpoint integer products,
divide by D, and round the lower endpoint down and upper endpoint up.
For division by an interval not containing zero, take the minimum and
maximum of the four rational quotients (numerator endpoint times D,
divided by denominator endpoint) and round outward to integers.
All input rationals are enclosed by the same outward rule. These rules
preserve inclusion by monotonicity and the interval endpoint extrema.

Evaluate (1)--(4) with these rules, then apply the following explicitly
ordered L_ldl D_ldl L_ldl^T recurrence to the leading 7x7 K_hub:

    (L_ldl)_ii=1, (L_ldl)_ij=0 when j>i,
    d_k=(K_hub)_kk-sum_(j<k)(L_ldl)_kj^2 d_j,
    (L_ldl)_ik=[(K_hub)_ik-sum_(j<k)(L_ldl)_ij (L_ldl)_kj d_j]/d_k,
                                      i>k, k=0,...,6.                (13)

Here D_ldl denotes the diagonal matrix with entries d_0,...,d_6.

The resulting pivot enclosures, again with denominator 10^6, are

    d_0 [33001,33002]
    d_1 [18414,18415]
    d_2 [32189,32190]
    d_3 [6594,6595]
    d_4 [55347,55348]
    d_5 [17763,17764]
    d_6 [13887,13888].                                                (14)

For additional intermediate audit data, the strictly lower triangular
entries of L_ldl are enclosed by the following integer intervals /10^6:

    row1: [-606615,-606614]
    row2: [-301481,-301480], [-733972,-733971]
    row3: [-388850,-388849], [126723,126724], [-290764,-290763]
    row4: [-74869,-74868], [-461577,-461576], [-373558,-373557],
          [-1923862,-1923861]
    row5: [23910,23911], [-155759,-155758], [-235331,-235330],
          [389447,389448], [-488882,-488881]
    row6: [130893,130894], [51682,51683], [-87399,-87398],
          [296037,296038], [-143294,-143293], [-1378617,-1378616].

All denominators in (13) are positive by the sequentially established
lower endpoints in (14). Thus the exact K_hub has an L_ldl D_ldl L_ldl^T factorization
with all seven diagonal pivots positive. This proves K_hub positive definite.
Equations (10)--(11) prove full 9x9 PSD and an exactly one-dimensional
kernel generated by (1,tau,...,tau). In particular this is not PSD checked
only on the returned primal span.

The tiny reference calculation `c51_exact_h3_audit.py` implements exactly
these rational interval rules once on this fixed 7x7 matrix; it runs no
solver, search or numerical sweep. Its JSON gives every full M and Z_hub
entry as an outward rational enclosure, as well as the finer pivot bounds.
The proof is the explicit coefficient formulas, the rational inequalities
above and the LDL recurrence, rather than any repaired numerical spectrum.

## 4. Consequences and limits

Together (3), (5), (6), (12) and (14) constitute a full exact candidate
certificate with objective level 9/(139+52sqrt(2)). The fixed Huber
function (9) attains it with initial distance exactly one. Every ordered
interpolation inequality and every formal gradient, including terminal,
belongs to the model. Returning to arbitrary L and initial distance uses
the normalization in accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290.

The strict sign and pivot margins imply, by continuity of the explicit
formulas, that the SAME fixed rational r gives a Huber-tight certificate
for every h in some open neighborhood of this seven-step word. This is
a local finite-horizon statement, not a claim that Huber tightness or the
pivot margins persist as m grows.

The numerical benchmark comparison is tau/U_7^ZJ approximately1.296454,
with the exact benchmark still defined by the full two-sequence DP.
Consequently this finite word is not evidence of improvement over ZJ.
The operationally useful output for the growing-horizon upper attempt is
an explicit lossless coordinate system in which the exact remaining tasks
are mu,beta nonnegativity and the full K_hub PSD. No recurring n-dependent
choice of r, or uniform positive pivot bound, has been proved here.

All mathematical conclusions in this file remain candidate statements
until accepted by the verifier; this file is not a fact-graph node.

# Unequal scalar clipping: an exact profile on a uniform ratio band

**ONE JOINT UNVERIFIED ANALYTICAL REPORT.** Project
`gd-exact-profile-20260926`, worker `max3`, invocation75 after ended74,
completed ceiling75 / attempted ceiling76, controller cap1. No statement
new to this report is a verifier-accepted fact. No formal gate is authorized.

The principal scoped result is the following candidate theorem. Fix the
SAME-enclosing-m hierarchy in Section2, with every integer m>=1536. Let
the two core curvatures be1, and let the two positive clipping levels be
u and v. If

    max(u,v)/min(u,v) <= 1/(c-2),                       (T)

then the exact supremum over EVERY nonzero real initial state of the
actual full-word objective score is

    sup_s 2 f(Psi_m(s))/s^2 = 1/D_m.                  (P)

Both clipped rays attain this value, at s=D_m*u and s=-D_m*v.
All starts in the respective interior capacity intervals have score
at most 1/d_m^2 < 1/D_m. This includes arbitrary m-dependent parameters,
both initial signs, threshold equalities, and all intervening returns.
It is not restricted to small perturbations of equal clipping: (T)
permits a ratio close to the silver ratio.

The new proof retains the **exact two signed endpoints** of every
interval image. It proves coordinatewise inequalities for those
endpoints; it never asserts that an asymmetric image is symmetric or
that two separately maximizing child histories can be combined.

This does NOT settle unrestricted positive clipping ratios. The
weighted-core absorption used in the proof has an exact failure
threshold, and Section8 gives a legal full-word escaping trajectory.
That trajectory is not an improving witness: its entire root-crossing
subfamily has optimized score strictly below 3/(4D_m). The remaining
general-ratio inequality is written explicitly in Section9.
The second substantive mechanism, involving two independent positive
core curvatures, is therefore not activated. The static general family,
its minimum sets and interpolation rows are defined in Section3; its
flat-curvature boundary is evaluated in Section10 as required boundary
accounting. The original unrestricted minimax question remains OPEN.

## 1. Scope, normalization and provenance

For any finite predetermined nonnegative word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), the
original problem uses x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L) grad F(x_i) and

    W_n(h)=sup 2(F(x_n)-F(x_star))/(L ||x_0-x_star||^2),
    W_n^star=inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n) W_n(h), W=2R.

The supremum is over every finite dimension, every convex differentiable
globally L-smooth F with an attained minimum, and every permissible
initial point and minimizer. The original target is
W_n^star/U_n^ZJ ->1, or a rigorous negative resolution. Its original
every-n/every-h quantifiers, including zero and arbitrarily large
steps, are unchanged. A family upper bound below is not an upper bound
on this full W. The original PROBLEM.md is unchanged.

The sole accepted input used for the benchmark comparison is
fact_id `931b9653ce0f7201`, read in full. With the original all-integer-
split benchmark B_n=1/U_n^ZJ, it proves B_(2^m-1)/D_m<47/50 on exactly
this hierarchy. Its full-W bound 17/(16D_m) is only an UNPROVED
hypothesis. Nothing in this report establishes that hypothesis.

The prior `bac703f383b0fbee` symmetric-Huber report was read in full as
awareness. Its interval and profile claims are rederived jointly here.
The earlier fixed two-curvature quadratic family `bf10ce01c54882c8`
and disjoint-inward-interval family `13c7d6e297580087` are not assumed
as premises and are not reductions of the present family.

Assignment guidance `1392963f4209aab6` and latest-record guidance
`ad191c13674b2437` were read; the latter leaves this worker's cap and
assignment unchanged. The shared plan is `1dfce6b448cb7e53`.

## 2. Physical word and elementary parameter inequalities

Fix an arbitrary integer m>=1536. All notation in this section is
local to this report, including hierarchy a,c,H_j,A_j. Put

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, d_j=a*c^j (0<=j<=m),
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j) (0<=j<m),
    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, D_m=1+2S_m=2A_m-1.

The length of H_j is 2^j-1 by the displayed concatenation. Every child
uses the same m and the same epsilon,beta,c,a. The largest physical
bridge in H_m is1+d_(m-1); d_m is only an auxiliary amplitude ruler.
No extra update or truncation is introduced.

Here epsilon<=1/6144<1/100 and beta>99/100. Since sqrt(2)<29/20,

    2<lambda<c<(49/20)(101/100)<5/2,
    1/3<99/250<a<1/lambda<1/2.

The identity lambda(lambda-2)=1 and the strict increase of x(x-2)
for x>2 give c(c-2)>1. Let

    eta=a/(c-2)=beta/[c(c-2)],   0<eta<1.

Directly summing the word gives A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j and hence

    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j,
    d_j/A_j <= c-2 <1/2,                              (2.1)
    A_j<c^j for j>=1.

For the closed form, its initial value is1 and its update is correct
because 2eta+a=c*eta. Define two further local constants

    T=1/(c-2),   b=a*beta.

These are a clipping-ratio allowance and a plateau factor, respectively;
b is NOT either clipping level or the original benchmark. They satisfy

    0<b<1/2, c*b=beta^2<1, T*b=beta^2/[c(c-2)]<1.      (2.2)

For later plateau placement we need d_j>A_(j-1), for 2<=j<=m.
At j=2,

    c(d_2-A_1)=beta*c^2-2c-beta
               =2epsilon-lambda^2*epsilon^2*(1+epsilon)>0.

The last inequality follows from lambda^2=2lambda+1<6 and
6epsilon(1+epsilon)<2. If d_j>A_(j-1), then

    d_(j+1)-A_j=(c-1/c)d_j-2A_(j-1)>0,

because c-1/c>lambda-1/lambda=2. Also

    d_j-A_(j-1)-d_(j-1)
       =(c-1)d_(j-1)-A_(j-1)<0,                      (2.3)

using d_(j-1)/A_(j-1)<=c-2 and
(c-1)(c-2)<(3/2)(1/2)<1. Every sign needed below therefore holds
uniformly for the advertised m, with no asymptotic replacement of c.

## 3. One static function, every query and the full minimum set

For fixed positive b_plus,b_minus and fixed kappa_plus,kappa_minus in
[0,1], define ONE scalar derivative on the whole real line by

    g(x)=min(kappa_plus*x,b_plus) for x>=0,
    g(x)=max(kappa_minus*x,-b_minus) for x<=0,
    f(x)=integral from0 to x of g(t) dt.

If kappa_sigma>0, write t_sigma=b_sigma/kappa_sigma. On that ray,
with r=|x|,

    f(x)=kappa_sigma*r^2/2             for 0<=r<=t_sigma,
    f(x)=b_sigma*r-b_sigma^2/(2kappa_sigma) for r>=t_sigma.

If kappa_sigma=0, f and g vanish on that entire ray; there is no finite
clipping threshold on it. These definitions agree at0 and at every
finite threshold. g is continuous and nondecreasing. On each smooth
piece its slope lies in[0,1]; partitioning any interval at its finitely
many breakpoints proves |g(x)-g(y)|<=|x-y|. Thus f is finite, convex,
differentiable and globally1-smooth. For general advertised L>0 use
F=L*f; the physical updates h_i/L reproduce x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g(x_i).

The complete attained minimum is0 and the complete minimum set C is:

* {0} if both curvatures are positive;
* (-infinity,0] if kappa_minus=0<kappa_plus;
* [0,infinity) if kappa_plus=0<kappa_minus;
* the whole real line if both curvatures are0.

For a positive closest initial distance, the normalization uses exactly
dist(s,C), never a chosen larger radius. In the first case this is |s|.
In either one-flat-ray case an active-side start has distance |s|;
starts in C are stationary with zero gap. If C is the whole line every
original admissible pair x_0!=x_star has zero gap. The closest-distance
quotient itself is only used when its denominator is positive.

For ANY fixed parameters and start s, all physical samples, including
the terminal, are defined by

    x_0=s, x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g(x_i),
    g_i=g(x_i), f_i=f(x_i), 0<=i<=n.

Every ordered smooth-convex interpolation row holds directly. If x<y
and Delta=g(y)-g(x)>=0, monotonicity and the Lipschitz inequality imply

    g(t)-g(x) >= max(0,Delta-(y-t)),
    g(y)-g(t) >= max(0,Delta-(t-x)), x<=t<=y.

Here Delta<=y-x. Integrating gives at least Delta^2/2 in each case.
Consequently, in BOTH orders, including equal sample locations,

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2 <=0.      (3.1)

This proves the original rows for all remote pairs, repeated visits,
the unqueried terminal and any point of C, where g=f=0. The full Gram
matrix of the initial displacement and all scalar gradients is the
ordinary Gram of these real numbers and is positive semidefinite:
every quadratic form is the square of their linear combination. No
dual certificate, omitted row or restricted PSD space is used.

For the unit-core family set u=b_plus,v=b_minus. Then
g(x)=clip(x,[-v,u]), f(x)=x*g(x)-g(x)^2/2. With y_i=x_i-g_i,
the original residual has the additional exact expression

    Q_ij=y_j*(g_i-g_j)<=0.                            (3.2)

If y_j>0, g_j=u and g_i<=u; if y_j<0, g_j=-v and g_i>=-v;
if y_j=0 the row is equality. In particular Q_(i,star)=0 and
Q_(star,i)=-y_i*g_i<=0 at star0.

Scaling x=q*z and both clipping levels by q>0 multiplies f by q^2
and leaves the curvatures and physical word unchanged. Thus a spatial
scale can be removed, but the clipping ratio remains. Reflection
x->-x swaps the two clipping levels AND the two curvatures, and swaps
initial signs. It preserves the score. Rescaling a core curvature to1
would change the effective word, so no such reduction is made. In the
unit-core proof only, reflection lets us write u=U>=v>0; the ratio
t=U/v is retained throughout.

## 4. Exact signed interval interface

For unit cores let T_(1+d)(x)=x-(1+d)*clip(x,[-v,u]), where d>0.
It is continuous, fixes0, and has slopes1,-d,1 on its three pieces.
For p,q>=0, its image of [-p,q] is EXACTLY [-p',q'], where

    p'=max(d*min(q,u), p-(1+d)*v),
    q'=max(d*min(p,v), q-(1+d)*u).                     (4.1)

Both first terms are nonnegative, so a separate zero term is unnecessary.
On [0,q], the most negative value is -d*min(q,u), while the only
possible positive extremum beyond0 is q-(1+d)u. On [-p,0], the most
positive value is d*min(p,v), while the only possible negative extremum
beyond0 is -p+(1+d)v. The extrema occur at endpoints or included
thresholds. Continuity makes the entire interval between them attained.
This also proves the formula when an endpoint equals either threshold.

Let Psi_j be the chronological full map for H_j. Let

    Phi_j(p,q)=(p_j(p,q),q_j(p,q))

mean that Psi_j([-p,q])=[-p_j(p,q),q_j(p,q)] EXACTLY. Its two
coordinates are nonnegative and nondecreasing in both input endpoints,
by interval inclusion. Phi_0 is the identity. Writing E_d for (4.1),

    Phi_(j+1)=Phi_j composed with E_(d_j) composed with Phi_j. (4.2)

This identity concerns the image of ONE continuous map on ONE interval.
All values entering the second child are actually attained after the
first child and bridge. It does not select independent maximizing
histories. Both endpoints are retained even where their upper bounds
happen to agree. The proof below uses (4.2) symbolically; it was not
executed as a recurrence or program.

## 5. Weighted-core absorption in the ratio band

For all parameters, even outside the band, a start x>=A_j*u stays
on the positive saturated ray throughout H_j and
Psi_j(x)=x-S_j*u. Indeed each prefix has mass at most S_j and the
final state is at least u. Likewise for x<=-A_j*v,
Psi_j(x)=x+S_j*v. This includes equality and every original update.

Now assume max(u,v)/min(u,v)<=T. For every j<=m we claim

    Psi_j([-A_j*v,A_j*u])=[-v,u].                     (5.1)

At j=0 this is the identity. Assume it at j. Write d=d_j and A=A_j.
Since A_(j+1)=2A+d, the first child sends the two endpoints of the
larger interval to -v*(A+1+d) and u*(A+1+d). The interior part maps
to [-v,u] by induction, and each exterior part has the saturated
formula. Thus these are the exact first-child extrema. Formula (4.1)
then gives bridge extrema

    -max(d*u,A*v),   max(d*v,A*u).

From d/A<=c-2 and the ratio bound, d*u<=A*v and d*v<=A*u.
So the bridge image is exactly [-A*v,A*u]. The second child sends
it to [-v,u] by induction. This proves (5.1) with both endpoint
signs and all intervening states retained.

## 6. A two-endpoint plateau and its geometric propagation

By reflection suppose u=U>=v, with t=U/v<=T. For every 1<=j<=m,
both coordinates satisfy

    p_j(d_j*U,d_j*U)<=b*U,
    q_j(d_j*U,d_j*U)<=b*U.                            (6.1)

Base j=1: d_1=beta and d_0=a. Since beta*U<U, (4.1) gives

    p_1=max(b*U, beta*U-(1+a)*v),
    q_1=a*min(beta*U,v)<=b*U.

The first coordinate is at most bU because
beta*(1-a)*t<=1+a. To prove this at t<=T, multiply by c-2>0:

    (1+a)(c-2)-beta(1-a)
      =c-2-a*(2-beta)
      =[c(c-2)-beta*(2-beta)]/c>0,

as c(c-2)>1 and beta*(2-beta)=1-epsilon^2<1.

For j>=2, put A=A_(j-1), d=d_(j-1), S=A-1. The input radius
d_j U exceeds both A*u and A*v because d_j>A and U>=v. From
Section5, the first child has the exact signed image endpoints

    p=d_j*U-S*v,    q=U*(d_j-S).

They exceed v and U respectively. The bridge therefore gives

    p'=max(d*U, d_j*U-(A+d)*v),
    q'=max(d*v, U*(d_j-A-d)).

The second term in q' is negative by (2.3), so q'=d*v. For p',
A/d>=T and t<=T imply

    t*(c-1)<=T*(c-1)=T+1<=A/d+1.

Equivalently (d_j-d)U<=(A+d)v, and hence p'=d*U. Thus the
EXACT bridge image is [-d*U,d*v], generally asymmetric. Its second
child endpoints are each at most the corresponding endpoints of
Phi_(j-1)(d*U,d*U), by monotonicity. The induction hypothesis bounds
both by bU. This proves (6.1). No equality with a symmetric image
is substituted for the actual asymmetric image.

In fact, for every integer r>=0 and every 1<=j<=m,

    p_j(d_j*U*b^r,d_j*U*b^r)<=U*b^(r+1),
    q_j(d_j*U*b^r,d_j*U*b^r)<=U*b^(r+1).              (6.2)

This is simultaneous induction in j with ALL integers r retained.
At j=1, r=0 is (6.1). If r>=1, the input radius beta*U*b^r is
less than v, because t*b<1 and beta<1. The leaf is the linear map
-a*x on this entire interval, giving exactly U*b^(r+1) for both
absolute endpoints.

For the next j, r=0 is (6.1). If r>=1, then

    d_j*U*b^r=(c*b)*d_(j-1)*U*b^(r-1)
                 <=d_(j-1)*U*b^(r-1).

Monotonicity and the first-child induction hypothesis give ACTUAL
endpoints p,q<=U*b^r. This is inside the common core since U*b^r<v
by t*b<1. The bridge is therefore exactly linear, with actual signed
endpoint pair (d_(j-1)*q,d_(j-1)*p). Both coordinates are at most
d_(j-1)*U*b^r. The second-child induction hypothesis, again using
coordinatewise monotonicity, bounds both outputs by U*b^(r+1).
This proves (6.2). The actual pair is neither discarded nor asserted
to have equal coordinates at any stage.

Take ANY real radius 0<R<=d_j U. Since0<b<1, some integer r>=0
satisfies d_j U b^(r+1)<R<=d_j U b^r. At an exact geometric
endpoint, use it as the upper endpoint of this inequality. By (6.2)
and monotonicity, both endpoints of Phi_j(R,R) are at most
U*b^(r+1)<R/d_j. Thus

    |Psi_j(s)|<=|s|/d_j for 0<|s|<=d_j U.             (6.3)

For any start in [-A_j v,A_j U] with |s|>d_j U, (5.1) gives
|Psi_j(s)|<=U<|s|/d_j. Together these prove the uniform interior
radial bound

    |Psi_j(s)|<=|s|/d_j for s in [-A_j v,A_j U].       (6.4)

The value0 maps to0. The geometric proof covers every real amplitude
between thresholds, not a finite mesh. Arbitrarily many sign changes
and returns are already present in the complete Phi_j composition.

For additional origin accounting, if |s|<=min(u,v), every physical
iterate stays in this common core and the absolute full-word multiplier
is P_j=beta^(2^j-1)/c^j. Joint induction proves this: after the first
child its magnitude is P_j|s|, the bridge gives
d_j P_j|s|=a beta^(2^j-1)|s|<=|s|, and the second child remains in
the core by the same induction. The new multiplier is d_j P_j^2.
This gives the stated expression at j+1, with P_0=1. Thus the
origin limit of the score at each fixed m is P_m^2. No interchange
between m and an amplitude supremum is made.

## 7. Exact all-amplitude score in the band; symmetric recovery

For unit cores, f(x)<=x^2/2 everywhere. This follows directly from
the quadratic-core expression and
x^2/2-(b_sigma*|x|-b_sigma^2/2)=(|x|-b_sigma)^2/2 on either
saturated ray. Therefore (6.4) gives, for every nonzero interior start,

    2 f(Psi_m(s))/s^2 <=1/d_m^2.                      (7.1)

This does not assume that the terminal lies in the common symmetric
core; it uses the global displayed objective inequality.
From a>1/3, A_m<c^m and m>=1536>=5,

    d_m^2>c^(2m)/9>2c^m>D_m,

where c^m>2^m>=32>18 supplies the middle strict inequality.
Thus the entire interior score is strictly below 1/D_m.

On the positive exterior s>=A_m*u, every query is on its original
saturated ray and the terminal is s-S_m*u>=u. Set z=s/u. The score
is exactly

    (2z-D_m)/z^2 <=1/D_m,

because z^2-D_m*(2z-D_m)=(z-D_m)^2>=0. Equality occurs exactly
at z=D_m, which is admissible since D_m>=A_m. The negative exterior
is identical with z=|s|/v. At the two maximizers, the complete actual
trajectory is

    x_i=u*(D_m-prefix_mass_i), or
    x_i=-v*(D_m-prefix_mass_i), 0<=i<=n_m,

where prefix_mass_i is the sum of exactly the first i physical steps.
The terminals are u*A_m and -v*A_m, the true radii are u*D_m and
v*D_m, and the objective gaps are u^2*D_m/2 and v^2*D_m/2.
This proves (P), with equality precisely at those two starts since
the interior bound is strict. Every row is already proved in Section3.

When u=v, reflection symmetry makes every actual image of a symmetric
interval symmetric. Formula (4.1) reduces to the scalar envelope

    E_d(R)=dR for R<=1,
           d for 1<=R<=1+2d,
           R-1-d for R>=1+2d

after dividing all positions by u. The absorption, plateau and
geometric proof above specialize to the prior symmetric-Huber proof,
but none was assumed as an accepted premise. In particular the exact
all-amplitude symmetric value1/D_m is jointly recovered.

By accepted fact_id931b9653ce0f7201, the family optimum in (P) obeys
B_(n_m)/D_m<47/50 for every advertised m. This is a benchmark
deficit for the specified unit-curvature ratio band only. It is not
an unrestricted W upper bound or a resolution of the original target.

## 8. Exact failure of weighted absorption; a paid physical escape

The ratio condition needed for weighted absorption can itself be
located exactly, without computation. Define

    T_j=A_j/d_j=T+[(1-eta)/a]*(2/c)^j.

It strictly decreases to T as j increases. Repeating the induction
in Section5 shows that weighted absorption at EVERY level through m
holds whenever max(u,v)/min(u,v)<=T_(m-1), because this implies
the needed ratio bound A_j/d_j at every physical bridge j<m.

Conversely, suppose u/v=t>T_(m-1). Put
A=A_(m-1), d=d_(m-1), S=A-1, and start the SAME full word H_m at
s=A*u. The first H_(m-1) is entirely positive saturated, with
queries x=u*(A-p) at prefix mass p in[0,S]; it ends at u. The root
bridge1+d sends u to -d*u. Since d*u>A*v, the ENTIRE second child
is negative saturated, with queries

    x=-d*u+v*q, 0<=q<=S,

where q is the actual suffix prefix mass. The true terminal is

    x_n=-d*u+S*v < -v.                               (8.1)

The initial state lies in [-A_m*v,A_m*u], but the terminal is outside
[-v,u]. Thus weighted absorption on the whole word fails. At
t=T_(m-1), the same formulas give terminal exactly -v, so equality
is not discarded. Reflection treats the reversed clipping ordering.
This proves the claimed exact threshold for the simultaneous
weighted-absorption property through level m.

The escape is a genuine one-dimensional static instance, with all
queries, rows, true minimum{0}, radius A*u and terminal explicitly
given. Its objective score, for every t>=A/d, is exactly

    E_root(t)=[2d*t-(2A-1)]/(A^2*t^2).                (8.2)

With D_child=2A-1, the identity

    d^2*t^2-D_child*(2d*t-D_child)=(d*t-D_child)^2

proves

    sup_(t>=A/d) E_root(t)=d^2/[A^2*(2A-1)],          (8.3)

attained at t=(2A-1)/d. This is feasible because A>1. Since
D_m=4A+2d-1, A>2, and d/A<c-2<1/2,

    D_m*sup E_root
      =(d/A)^2*(4A+2d-1)/(2A-1)<3/4.                (8.4)

Indeed 4A+2d-1<5A-1<=3(2A-1) for A>=2. Thus the escape refutes
an extrapolation of the absorption lemma, not the conjectured
all-ratio profile. This is diagnostic analysis of a concrete missing
step; it is not a new disjoint-inward-block construction proposal.

Two direct proof extensions have therefore been screened. First,
extending the weighted-core induction fails at the actual root above.
Second, extending the simultaneous endpoint plateau globally also
fails: for fixed m, take v=1 and u=U so large that

    U>max(A_m/d_m, S_m/[d_m*(1-b)], T).

Then the negative input -d_m U is saturated throughout H_m and
Psi_m(-d_m U)=-d_m U+S_m. Its magnitude exceeds bU, since the
displayed stronger condition gives d_m U-S_m>d_m bU>bU
(d_m>1). Consequently the two-endpoint bound (6.1) at j=m is
false for sufficiently large clipping ratio. These are literal
physical trajectories, not a failure of a chosen algebraic certificate.
The corresponding exterior objective remains covered by the
all-saturated formula, so this does not refute a score bound either.

## 9. What remains, precisely

The full unit-core family would have profile1/D_m if the following
two signed inequalities held for every positive u,v and every R>0:

    2 f(-p_m(R,R)) <= R^2/D_m,
    2 f( q_m(R,R)) <= R^2/D_m.                        (9.1)

Here Phi_m is the exact actual interval map of Section4, with u,v
fixed for the entire word. These are necessary as well as sufficient
for the corresponding bound on all individual starts: every endpoint
is attained by some |s|<=R, and a bound using its actual |s| is at
least as strong as the displayed R-bound; conversely set R=|s| and
use that f is nonincreasing on the negative ray and nondecreasing
on the positive ray. An endpoint achieved at s=0 is0 and causes no
division issue. Lower attainment1/D_m is always available on either
saturated ray, regardless of ratio.

Equation (9.1) is an unresolved interface, not a theorem or a closed
profile claimed for the unrestricted family. Section7 evaluates it
uniformly on the entire ratio band(T); Section8 evaluates a concrete
escaping subfamily outside that band. The remaining difficulty is to
bound endpoint objective energy after a large-side-to-small-side
overshoot and possible later returns, when the negative and positive
endpoints occupy different affine/quadratic pieces. Bounding their
absolute radii by a common plateau loses the small-side objective
weight, and the literal common-plateau assertion is false. The signed
objective terms in (9.1) retain that weight.

For completeness, the exact general-curvature one-step interval
interface follows directly from the static function in Section3.
Let kappa_+=kappa_plus, kappa_-=kappa_minus and h>=0. Define

    C_+(q)=(h*kappa_+-1)_+ * min(q,b_plus/kappa_+),
    C_-(p)=(h*kappa_--1)_+ * min(p,b_minus/kappa_-)

when the relevant curvature is positive, and set the corresponding
C to0 when it is0. Then the exact signed endpoints are

    p'=max(C_+(q), p-h*min(kappa_-*p,b_minus), 0),
    q'=max(C_-(p), q-h*min(kappa_+*q,b_plus), 0).       (9.2)

To verify it, on each half-line the map is continuous piecewise
linear, with inner slope1-h*kappa_sigma and outer slope1. A
negative inner slope has its opposite-sign extremum at the included
clipping threshold or interval endpoint; a nonnegative inner slope
has no opposite-sign output. The remaining same-sign extremum is
at the outer endpoint or0. Formula(9.2) includes h*kappa=1,
endpoint=threshold and kappa=0. Composing these actual interval
images in the physical order preserves all sign changes and returns.
It is only an exact setup for the unattempted second mechanism,
not a claimed quantitative evaluation over independent positive
curvatures. No effective-word rescaling is used.

## 10. Flat-curvature boundary, including zero and huge steps

This elementary boundary accounting does not open the second search
mechanism. Suppose one ray is flat and the other has curvature
kappa in(0,1] and positive clipping level b0. By reflection put the
active ray on x>0. Its complete minimum set is(-infinity,0]. Let h
be ANY finite nonnegative word and S=sum h, retaining zero and
arbitrarily large steps. For every active start s>0 the exact family
profile is

    sup_s 2 f(x_n)/s^2 = kappa/(1+2*kappa*S).          (10.1)

If a query ever reaches the minimum half-line, it stays there and
the terminal gap is0. Otherwise all iterates are positive and
nonincreasing. If the terminal is saturated, every earlier query
is saturated too. Writing z=s/b0, its exact score is

    [2z-(2S+1/kappa)]/z^2 <=1/(2S+1/kappa),

by the square identity used in Section7. Equality is attained by
s=b0*(2S+1/kappa), with actual terminal b0*(S+1/kappa), and all
queries on the saturated active ray. The own radius is exactly s.

If the terminal lies in the active core, g_n=kappa*x_n, and
monotonicity of the positive iterates gives g_i>=g_n for all i<n.
The exact sum of updates yields

    s=x_n+sum_i h_i*g_i >=x_n*(1+kappa*S).

Hence its score is at most kappa/(1+kappa*S)^2, which is at most
kappa/(1+2*kappa*S). This proves the upper bound for every start,
including the threshold equality. It proves (10.1) without assuming
that each update is short or stays positive. At S=0 the word is
stationary and the expression is kappa, as it should be.

On H_m, this fixed-kappa value is at most1/D_m because
kappa/(1+2kappa*S_m)<=1/(1+2S_m) is equivalent to kappa<=1.
Allowing all kappa in(0,1] gives the exact boundary-family profile
1/D_m, attained at kappa1. Both-flat and minimum-set starts have
zero gap as described in Section3. No formula divides by a zero
curvature or a zero closest radius. This boundary result says
nothing about two independent positive core curvatures.

## 11. Retrieval, audit and bounded handoff

Skills used: query-memory, search-math-results, direct-proving and
construct-counterexamples. The theorem search was conditioned on
the live asymmetric-clipping interval problem, program_stage
active_program, search_mode repair. Matlas returned three unrelated
or inapplicable statements (circle packing robustness, a conditional
gradient-norm averaging theorem, and Burer--Monteiro ascent). None
was imported. Web fallback supplied silver-stepsize/Huber background
but no theorem used in the proof. The exact known local paper
`downloads/taylor-hendrickx-glineur-1502.05666.txt` was read at
Section2.4 and Lemma2, including both directions of its proof.
Its conjugacy definitions provided context only; Section3 proves
every required interpolation row directly. No external result is
used as a mathematical premise.

Some initial broad tool output was truncated. The relevant current
guidance and the accepted931 fact were subsequently read directly;
the symmetric report was recovered across its introductory section
and complete mathematical body. Empty administrative command
outputs were only successful mkdir/append outputs, not mathematical
evidence. No verification success is claimed.

All theorem-like statements in this report belong to ONE JOINT
UNVERIFIED draft. In particular (P), the absorption threshold and
the boundary evaluation have not been promoted to facts. The new
proof mechanism is the exact signed interval image, weighted
absorption and two-endpoint plateau. The root-escape calculation
tests the same mechanism's scope. The independent-positive-curvature
mechanism remains unattempted because its prerequisite, a decisive
all-ratio unit-core result, has not been obtained.

Resource account: mathematical/numerical programs0; arithmetic,
trajectory and recurrence replays0; solvers/optimizers/benchmarkDP0;
formal calls/fact_submit0; new accepted facts0; installations0;
paid consults0; subagents0. Only text reasoning, retrieval, local
artifact writing and administrative integrity checks were used.
All older grants remain closed. No outward publication, active-task
overwrite, force stop, finalization, revocation, shutdown, partial
Goal completion or automatic renewal is performed.

At the authorized cap the deliverable is this joint draft and a
self-contained handoff. Original target OPEN; unrestricted asymmetric
clipping OPEN; independent positive core curvatures OPEN.
