# The matched equality locus: exact branches, boundary exclusions, and an old-test identity

Status: complete joint proof DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is max4 round33,
the first of the two analytical rounds allocated after completed32 by
TASK.md and master_guidance77175b3daa836eea. No numerical evaluation,
machine algebra, solver, replay, formal verifier, consult, installation,
or subordinate agent is used. No new fact is asserted.

The requested GLOBAL threshold decision is NOT complete. In particular,
this draft does not claim that the remaining simultaneous locus is empty.
Its new results are exact branch eliminations and exclusions, a reduction
of the remaining actual-array signs to two explicit signs at threshold,
and an exact redundancy comparison for a proposed positive-remainder
bound. The new bound is the old unit-quadratic test in different algebraic
coordinates; it must not be counted as an independent obstruction.

The accepted mathematical input is04219554e41d9750, Section10. Appendix A
reproduces the full original-row derivation, costs, matching, and matrix
check needed from the earlier UNVERIFIED draftf2aab392648bc228. That draft
is awareness only; the reproduced argument and the new argument below
form ONE candidate for joint checking. There is no appeal to an
unverified intermediate assertion as a fact.

## 1. The exact interface, with no omitted coefficient

Use the full primitive interface in Appendix A. In particular

 h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,v), a,b,c>0, bc<=1, v>=0,
 u=1+a, beta=1+b, w=1+c, A=2+a, N=A+beta,
 Hpre=A+b, s=sum h, C=1+s=N+c+1+v,
 t=c/(1+v), mu=1+t, ell=a^2*b*(1-b*t),
 R=ell*N^2+mu-C^2*a^2*b*beta*t^2, D=b^2*N^2-A^2.

The original array has twenty nonnegative off-diagonal entries, zero
diagonal, its exact original value flow, and full PSD matrix M on R^5.
No formal gradient is identified, even at a zero step.

For 0<v<2 let Hbar be the positive root

 (1-bt)*Hbar^2+(beta-2*b*w)*Hbar-b*w^2/mu=0,
 k=Hbar/(a^2*mu), x=b*(w+Hbar*mu)/(1+Hbar), rho=x/beta,
 eps=k*R-D.

Appendix A derives an exact sum of a strictly positive definite
two-vector Gram expression and nonnegative ORIGINAL coefficient costs
for eps. Its strictly positive costs imply that eps=0,R>0 forces

 a=sqrt(2)-1, A=1+sqrt(2), a*A=1,
 Lambda01=2, Lambda10=2A,
 Lambda02=Lambda03=Lambda04=Lambda14=Lambda20=Lambda23=0,
 Lambda30=Lambda31=Lambda40=Lambda41=0.

For the remainder of the positive-energy equality analysis A and a have
these fixed values. Define

 chi=c*v/(w+v), gamma=1+chi=w/mu, Ecap=1+v, Wtail=c+Ecap=w+v,
 Tstar=R/(ell*mu^2+mu*a^2*(x-b*mu)^2), q13=2*rho*Tstar,
 Ppre=N+c, s=Ppre+v.

The other original entries, without any choice left, are

 Lambda13=q13,
 Lambda12=[2*A*Hpre-(beta+w)*q13]/beta,
 Lambda21=2*Hpre+Lambda12+q13,
 Lambda24=[2*w*N+beta*Lambda21]/Wtail,
 Lambda42=Lambda24/c,
 Lambda32=2*Ppre+q13+(c-1)*Lambda42,
 Lambda43=[2*s*C-Wtail*Lambda42]/v,
 Lambda34=(1-c)*Lambda42+Lambda43-2*s.

Put nstar=Lambda42+Lambda43-s*(s+2) and

 vcol=(0,-rho,rho-1,1,0)^T, dcol=(0,0,0,-1,1)^T.

The whole matrix, not a compression with a free covariance, is

                  M=Tstar*vcol*vcol^T+nstar*dcol*dcol^T.       (1)

Appendix A checks every flow and moment and proves (1). Consequently,
before the further exclusions below, exact feasibility on this locus is
equivalent to

 Lambda12,Lambda32,Lambda34,Lambda43>=0, nstar>=0.              (2)

Lambda21,Lambda24,Lambda42 are then strictly positive. These are the
remaining signs in the ENTIRE twenty-entry array, not a selected support
being proposed for the unrestricted cone.

## 2. A simpler root equation, with both roots accounted for

The Hbar polynomial is exactly

              beta*Hbar*(Hbar+1)=b*mu*(Hbar+gamma)^2.

Indeed 1-bt=beta-b*mu, and w=gamma*mu; expanding both sides gives
the displayed polynomial in Section1. Therefore

 rho=(b*mu/beta)*(Hbar+gamma)/(1+Hbar)
     =Hbar/(Hbar+gamma),
 Hbar=gamma*rho/(1-rho),
                    chi*rho^2+rho=b*w/beta.                 (3)

In particular 0<rho<1. The two real roots of (3) are

 rho_plus =[-1+sqrt(1+4*chi*b*w/beta)]/(2*chi)>0,
 rho_minus=[-1-sqrt(1+4*chi*b*w/beta)]/(2*chi)<0.

They are distinct since chi>0. The negative root cannot describe this
matched locus: Tstar>0 and Lambda13=2*rho*Tstar would give an original
negative multiplier. Thus discarding it is justified by an actual sign.
The original Hbar polynomial itself has one positive and one negative
root because its leading coefficient and b*w^2/mu are positive.

Useful consequences of (3), all exact, are

 b*mu < x < b*w,
 xi:=b*mu/x=(1+chi*rho)/(1+chi) in (0,1),
 D=beta^2*rho^2*(1+2*chi*rho)*Tstar>0.                       (4)

For the last identity, Appendix A gives
k*(ell*mu^2+mu*a^2*(x-b*mu)^2)=2*b*w*x-x^2.
Use b*w=beta*rho*(1+chi*rho) and eps=0.

Let b0 be the unique positive solution of

 b0*(A+1+b0)=A, N0=A+1+b0.

The quadratic b^2+(A+1)b-A has negative constant term, hence its
other root is negative. Its derivative is positive for b>=0.
Since D>0, (4) implies b>b0; the negative b-root is not admissible.

## 3. A full quadratic identity and the terminal endpoint exclusion

The original full unit-quadratic column is

 pquad=(1,-a,ab,-abc,abc*(v-1))^T,
 Q=pquad^T M pquad=1-C^2*a^2*b^2*c^2*(v-1)^2.

Its identity follows from T*pquad=e and the telescoping of
h_i(2-h_i)*pquad_i^2; Appendix A gives the derivation.
Under a*A=1, direct expansion of R gives

 R/a^2=b*beta*(N^2-C^2*t^2)-mu*D.

Use eps=0, k=Hbar/(a^2*mu), and

 mu*(1+Hbar)/(Hbar*b*beta)=mu^2/x^2

(which follows from (3)). We obtain

 C^2*c^2=N^2*Ecap^2-Wtail^2*D/x^2,
 b^2*C^2*c^2=Ecap^2*[A^2+(1-xi^2)*D].                       (5)

Every factor in (1-xi^2)*D is strictly positive. Thus

                         C*b*c>A*(1+v).                    (6)

Since M is PSD, Q>=0. Equations (5)--(6) imply

 Q=v^2*(2-v^2)-(v^2-1)^2*(1-xi^2)*D/A^2.

If v>=sqrt(2), the first term is nonpositive and the second term
strictly negative. Hence EVERY actual positive-energy equality member
satisfies

                            0<v<sqrt(2).                    (7)

This is a statement about the matched original matrix. It is not an
inference from a freely assigned terminal covariance. Indeed (1) gives
the still stronger exact identity

 Q=a^2*(b*w-x)^2*Tstar+(a*b*c*v)^2*nstar.                    (8)

Both coefficients of Tstar and nstar in (8) are retained. In particular,
Q=0 is impossible here: b*w-x>0 and Tstar>0.

## 4. The original12 sign forces b below a fixed algebraic cap

Use Lambda12>=0, (4), and q13=2*rho*Tstar. They give

 rho*Tstar<=A*Hpre/(beta+w),
 D<=A*Hpre*beta*(2*b*w-x)/(beta+w)
   <A*Hpre*beta*b*(2*w-mu)/(beta+w).                       (9)

Since Ecap<A by (7), mu>1+c/A. Thus
2*w-mu<1+(2-a)*c. For fixed b>0 the function

              [1+(2-a)*c]/(beta+1+c)

has positive derivative: its derivative numerator is
(2-a)*(beta+1)-1>0, using a=sqrt(2)-1<1 and beta>1.
The condition bc<=1 therefore yields

                   D<A*Hpre*b*(b+2-a)/beta.                (10)

This does not drop the original12 sign; it is a consequence of that
sign, the SAME rho and Tstar, and the terminal PSD restriction.

Using A^2=2A+1, Hpre=A+b, and N=A+1+b, the left side minus the
right side of (10), after multiplying by beta, is

 Fcap(b):=beta*D-A*(A+b)*b*(b+2-a)
        =b^5+(2A+3)*b^4+(5A+4)*b^3+2b^2
                                      -(5A+3)*b-(2A+1).       (11)

Thus Fcap(b)<0. There is exactly one positive root bcap of Fcap:
Fcap(0)=-(2A+1)<0,

 Fcap''(b)=20b^3+12(2A+3)b^2+6(5A+4)b+4>0 for b>=0,

Fcap'(0)<0, and Fcap tends to positive infinity. The derivative
therefore crosses zero once; the function first decreases while
negative and then increases to infinity, crossing zero once.
Moreover Fcap(1)=6>0. At b0, D=0 and the unexpanded expression
in (11) is strictly negative. Hence

                        b0<b<bcap<1.                      (12)

In particular the physical second step is strictly less than2.
Neither equality b=1 nor the limiting algebraic root b=bcap is allowed.

## 5. Threshold consequences and the two remaining signs

Let Cstar=193/25. On the matched locus with C>=Cstar, (7),(12),bc<=1
give the further strict condition

                              v>1.                        (13)

Here is a purely symbolic proof, including v=1. The map z+1/z
is strictly decreasing on (0,1). Since b>b0 and c<=1/b,

 b+c<=b+1/b<b0+1/b0=sqrt(2)*(1+b0).

The last equality follows by dividing b0*N0=A by A. Also b0<5/8:
the polynomial z^2+(A+1)z-A at z=5/8 is65/64-3A/8>0,
because A<169/70<65/24. If v<=1, then

 C=A+2+b+c+v
   <169/70+3+(99/70)*(13/8)
   =4319/560<193/25.

The radical bound sqrt(2)<99/70 follows by squaring; the final
comparison has positive difference3/400. This proves (13).

Also c>4/5 on the threshold locus. Indeed a<3/7, v<10/7 and b<1 give

 c=C-4-a-b-v>Cstar-48/7=151/175>4/5,
 N=A+beta<31/7.

Consequently C*c>N, since C>7 and7*(4/5)>31/7.

We can now check ALL remaining terminal signs instead of leaving them
as unspecified conditions. Assume the equality equations, Lambda12>=0,
and nstar>=0 at C>=Cstar. The preceding derivations apply: (8) gives
Q>=0 directly from the full displayed matrix, and all formulas used in
(3)--(12) hold before imposing the three terminal coefficient signs.

Lambda21=2*Hpre+Lambda12+q13>0, hence Lambda24,Lambda42>0.
The following simplification follows from their original formulas:

        c*Wtail*Lambda42=2*N*Ppre-w*q13.                    (14)

If c>=1 then Lambda32=2*Ppre+q13+(c-1)*Lambda42>0.
If c<1, (14) and Lambda42>0 give

 Lambda32>2*Ppre*[1-(1-c)*N/(c*Wtail)]>0,

because c*Wtail-(1-c)*N=c*C-N>0.

The last moment and the definition of nstar give

             w*Lambda42+v*nstar=s*[2C-(C+1)v].              (15)

Using nstar>=0 in (15),

 2sC-Wtail*Lambda42
   >=(s*v/w)*[(C+1)*Wtail-2C]>0,

since Wtail=1+c+v>2 by (13). Thus Lambda43>0.
Finally

 Lambda34=nstar+s^2-c*Lambda42
          >s^2-2*N*Ppre/Wtail>0,

because Wtail>2, s=Ppre+v>Ppre, and Ppre=N+c>N.

Therefore, at threshold, the COMPLETE positive-energy matched locus
has only the following two remaining nontrivial sign/PSD tests:

                     Lambda12>=0, nstar>=0.                (16)

All other original signs have just been proved, not omitted. Conversely,
given eps=0,R>0,a=sqrt(2)-1,bc<=1,0<v<2,C>=Cstar, conditions (16)
imply the full twenty-sign/full-R5 certificate through (1) and the
complete flow check in Appendix A.

This equivalence is NOT a decision of (16). No parameter triple at
threshold is supplied, and no proof that (16) is impossible everywhere
is claimed.

## 6. An algebraic equality equation and its two genuine branches

For an alternative exact interface put

 Ueq=(N+c)*(N*Ecap-C*c), Tbase=Ueq/Wtail.

Direct expansion of the actual rows gives, for q13=Lambda13,

 M11=-D/beta^2+(b*w/beta)*q13,
 (e1)^T M(e3+e4)=-q13/2,
 (e3+e4)^T M(e3+e4)=Tbase-chi*q13.                         (17)

For the last expression, substitute (14) into
Tbase=N^2-c^2-2cN(N+c)/Wtail; multiplication by Wtail gives Ueq.
Equations (1),(3),(4) imply

 Tbase=(1+2chi*rho)*Tstar>0,
                         D=beta^2*rho^2*Tbase.             (18)

Thus the exact equality equation can be written

 (b*w*beta*Ueq-c*v*D)^2=beta^2*D*Ueq*Wtail,
 Ueq>0, b*w*beta*Ueq-c*v*D>0,                              (19)

and then rho=(b*w*beta*Ueq-c*v*D)/(beta^2*Ueq).
Conversely (19) yields (18) and (3) with the positive root, so the
same eps equality follows by reversing the identities above.

The squared equation in (19) is a genuine quadratic in Ueq:

 b^2*w^2*beta^2*Ueq^2
  -D*(2b*w*beta*c*v+beta^2*Wtail)*Ueq+c^2*v^2*D^2=0.       (20)

Both roots are positive. Its value at Ueq=0 is positive; at
Uzero=c*v*D/(b*w*beta)>0 it is
-beta^2*D*Uzero*Wtail<0; and the leading coefficient is positive.
Hence its two distinct positive roots lie one below and one above Uzero.
The lower root has b*w*beta*Ueq-c*v*D<0 and represents the forbidden
negative rho root. The upper root is the only branch with the original
Lambda13 sign. Keeping only the squared equation would admit a false
branch.

This is an elimination of a fixed original-array locus, not a new Gram
completion. The two tests (16) remain indispensable.

## 7. The positive-remainder formula is EXACTLY an older condition

This section does not assume eps=0. Its precisely stated domain is

 a=sqrt(2)-1, b,c>0, bc<=1, 0<v<2,

with Hbar,k,x,rho,xi defined by Section1 and (3), and with the actual
step expressions R,D,eps=kR-D. No matrix or multiplier is needed for
the algebraic equivalence in this section. In particular it applies
on the COMPLETE older necessary-system domain restricted to this
first-step slice, regardless of the other older tests.

Set kappa_eps=b*mu/(Hbar*beta)>0. The same expansion used in Section3,
now retaining eps, gives

 b^2*C^2*c^2
   =Ecap^2*[A^2+(1-xi^2)*D-kappa_eps*eps],

 Q=v^2*(2-v^2)
   -(v^2-1)^2*[(1-xi^2)*D-kappa_eps*eps]/A^2.              (21)

For v!=1 this proves the exact equivalence

 Q>=0
  if and only if
 eps >= [(1-xi^2)*D+A^2*(1-1/(v^2-1)^2)]/kappa_eps.        (22)

For v=1, Q=1 identically and no bound is obtained by dividing by
(v^2-1)^2. This boundary is retained explicitly.

If v>=sqrt(2) and D>0, the right side of (22) is strictly positive.
It is therefore an explicit positive-remainder escape condition on that
region of the SAME original step/matrix problem. However, (22) is
EXACTLY the unit-quadratic condition already retained in the complete
older system. Thus the entire older system implies it, and (22) implies
the older unit-quadratic test on its stated domain. There can be no
old-system survivor excluded solely by (22).

This is a proved redundancy statement, not an untested assertion of
independence. It does not claim that the stronger full-array test (8),
the two signs (16), or Appendix A's other positive-eps constraints are
redundant with the entire older system.

## 8. Zero-energy and limit boundaries

At eps=0,R=0 in the positive domain, D=0. The undivided positive-cost
identity in Appendix A forces the E and X columns to vanish; R=0 in
its repaired-cut identity forces the B column to vanish as well.
The original0 flow/moment then gives a=sqrt(2)-1. The two step
equations reduce to

 b*N=A, c*C=N*(1+v), 0<v<=sqrt(2).

The last inequality follows from the full quadratic identity
Q=1-(v^2-1)^2=v^2(2-v^2)>=0. Therefore

 N=N0=Omega(A,1), C=Omega(N0,1+v)<=Omega(N0,A),
 Omega(z,y)=(z+y+sqrt((z+y)^2+4zy))/2.

The positive root is the only nonnegative step root in each equation;
the second root in b or c is negative. The elementary rational bounds

 A<29/12,
 (201/50)^2-(29/12+1)*(201/50)-29/12=131/15000>0,
 (77/10)^2-(201/50+29/12)*(77/10)-(201/50)*(29/12)
                                                    =19/1500>0

give N0<201/50 and C<77/10<Cstar. Omega increases in each positive
argument by its displayed formula. Thus R=0 is fully excluded at
threshold; R<0 contradicts the repaired-cut energy identity directly.

There is no b=0 or c=0 threshold boundary of the positive-R locus.
At b=0, D=-A^2, whereas eps=0,R>=0 requires D>=0. At c=0,
Hbar=b, mu=w=1 and eps=A^2*(b+1)>0. The a=0 boundary is absent because
the matched locus forces a=sqrt(2)-1. At v>=2 the original terminal
moment/diagonal estimate in Appendix A already forbids full PSD.

In fact EVERY original four-step full certificate with a zero physical
step has C<15/2<Cstar, whether or not it is a limit of this locus.
Here is a direct necessary restriction argument, retaining the original
matrix as the hypothesis. Evaluate its full polynomial identity on the
linear subspace that sets the two gradients and values adjacent to a
zero step equal. The corresponding positions are already equal by GD.
Merge the two sample indices in each interpolation residual: residuals
between the merged pair are zero, and the other new coefficients are
sums of original nonnegative coefficients. The resulting matrix is the
pullback of the full original PSD matrix, hence PSD. This produces a
necessary compressed certificate with at most three positive steps.
It is only a necessary restriction of the original full-space identity;
no claim of sufficiency on the original space is made.

For a positive three-step compressed word (u1,z1,w1), both endpoints
are strictly below2. For the first endpoint, its moment forces a
positive outgoing total O and its diagonal is
u1*(2-u1)+(1-u1)*O, negative if u1>=2. For the last endpoint,
the terminal moment has weights at least w1 and its diagonal is
outgoing-s*(s+2)>=0. Thus w1<=2C/(C+1)<2.
If z1<=1, then C=1+u1+z1+w1<6.
If z1=1+b1 with b1>0, the actual scalar function with derivative
clamp(x,[-b1/(1+w1),1]), started at x0=1+u1, has successive
gradients 1,1,-b1/(1+w1),-b1/(1+w1) and terminal position equal
to its terminal gradient. Integrating this derivative with f(0)=0
gives a convex differentiable1-smooth function with a minimizer at0.
The saturated-value telescope in the original identity gives

 V=(1+u1)^2-C^2*b1^2/(1+w1)^2>=0.

Consequently C*b1<9, and C=2+u1+w1+b1<6+b1<6+9/C.
It follows that C<3+3sqrt(2)<15/2. With one or two positive
compressed steps the endpoint bounds already give C<3 or C<5;
the empty word has C=1. This proves the assertion for every original
zero physical step, without using the singular Lambda43 formula.

A finite limit of positive-R matched members with v<=1 also cannot
retain threshold capacity: the nondifferentiated bound C<=4319/560
proved in Section5 persists in that closure. All original formal
gradients remain distinct in the full-space hypothesis.

The boundary ell=0 in this parameter domain requires v=0,bc=1.
The undivided Appendix A identities remain valid there. It is excluded
from the entire original threshold cone by the zero-step bound, without
division by ell or by v. The R=0 and Q=0 cases above likewise did
not identify or discard formal columns.

One can also obtain a UNIFORM separation from v=sqrt(2) on the
threshold equality locus. Let

 Nmin=Cstar*(Cstar-A)/(Cstar+A), bmin=Nmin-A-1,
 Dmin=bmin^2*Nmin^2-A^2,
 cmin=Cstar-2A-2,
 chimin=cmin/(cmin+A),
 rmax=2/(1+sqrt(1+4chimin)),
 ximax=1-chimin*(1-rmax)/(1+chimin),
 Lmin=(1-ximax^2)*Dmin/A^2.

All these denominators are positive, bmin>b0 and Dmin>0. To see the
latter assertions, Cstar>Omega(N0,A), as just proved, is equivalent
to Nmin>N0. Since Ueq>0, Cc<NEcap; hence
N>C(C-Ecap)/(C+Ecap)>=Nmin. The last fraction increases with C
and decreases with Ecap. Thus b>bmin and D>Dmin.
Also c>cmin>4/5 and v>1 imply chi>chimin. Since bc<=1,
bw/beta<=1, so (3) gives rho<rmax<1. It follows that xi<ximax<1.

Using Q>=0 and (21) with eps=0 yields

                 v <= sqrt(1+1/sqrt(1+Lmin)) < sqrt(2).     (23)

This includes nstar=0; no positive terminal-energy lower bound is
assumed. It also shows that a threshold sequence cannot evade the
positive-energy analysis by taking v to sqrt(2), D to0, or c to0.
Again, (23) follows using the older Q test together with the equality
equations, and is not presented as an independent old-system separator.

## 9. Failed shortcuts and the remaining exact assignment

The bounds b0<b<bcap and0<v<sqrt(2), even together with bc<=1 and
D>0, DO NOT alone close threshold capacity. For a symbolic screening
family take

 b=b0+tau, c=1/b, v=sqrt(2)-tau, tau>0 sufficiently small.

At tau=0 its capacity tends to

 Cbar=2A+1+b0+1/b0=6+3a+sqrt(2)*b0>79/10>Cstar.

Here a>2/5, sqrt(2)>7/5 and b0>1/2 give the strict rational bound;
the last follows by evaluating b^2+(A+1)b-A at1/2.
Continuity, Fcap(b0)<0, and b0<1 prove the claimed partial conditions
for a whole sufficiently small positive interval of tau.
This is NOT a member of the equality locus and NOT a complete
older-system survivor: its unit-quadratic Q is negative near tau=0,
since Cbar>A^2 and a^2=1/A^2. Its purpose is to refute dropping the
remaining equations/signs, not to suggest a numerical seed.

The completed direct route gives (3)--(23), including the two
quadratic branches, full matrix (1), all terminal signs, the zero
boundaries, and a proved redundancy comparison. It does NOT decide
whether there exist simultaneous b,c,v with

 C>=Cstar, eps=0,R>0,a=sqrt(2)-1,bc<=1,0<v<2,
 Lambda12>=0,nstar>=0,

equivalently the original matched array and full PSD.
No high-capacity candidate exists in this output.

Two attempted global inferences remain unjustified: (i) that the
restricted b and v intervals alone cap C, refuted above; (ii) that
the positive-eps expression (22) improves the complete older system,
refuted by its exact equivalence to the retained Q test.

The next authorized round must decide the remaining TWO explicit
signs on the equality equation, or preserve a concrete reason why
that simultaneous sign problem remains stuck. It must not replace
the actual original matrix by an independent covariance choice.
The complete older conjunction, including all C82/C84, C83/C86,
clipping/return/lifted tests and the2948 first-row budget, is still
retained. No point passing all of it is supplied or silently assumed.

No fact_submit was authorized or called. The conditional lift
04219554e41d9750 still has no threshold seed, and the original
all-schedule exact-profile Goal remains OPEN.

## Appendix A. Reproduced original-row proof, for joint checking

The following mathematical argument is reproduced as proof text, not as
an accepted predecessor. References to its own section numbers are
internal to the reproduced argument. Its earlier global decision and
independence questions remain open except where the new analysis above
explicitly resolves a scoped part.

## 1. Original full interface and common identities

Let

    h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,v),  a,b,c>0, v>=0, bc<=1,
    u=1+a, beta=1+b, w=1+c,
    A=2+a, N=3+a+b, Hpre=2+a+b, H0pre=1+a+b,
    s=sum h, C=1+s=4+a+b+c+v,
    t=c/(1+v), mu=1+t,
    ell=a^2*b*(1-b*t), z=a*b*v*t,
    R=ell*N^2+mu-C^2*a^2*b*beta*t^2,
    D=N^2*b^2-A^2.

Let e_i be the five original standard columns, indexed0 through4,
e=sum e_i, and P_i=sum_(j<i)h_j. Define

    T_ii=1, T_ij=h_j for j<i, T_ij=0 for j>i,
    omega=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2s)^T,
    Dmat=diag(h0(2-h0),h1(2-h1),h2(2-h2),h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)),
    L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, M=Dmat+(LT+T^T L^T)/2.

The hypotheses are ALL twenty signs Lambda_ij>=0 for i!=j, zero
diagonal, L^T e=omega, and M>=0 on ALL of R^5. There is no support,
rank, symmetry, reversal, or duplicated-gradient restriction.

The accepted identity is

    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=sum_(i,j)M_ij<g_i,g_j>,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f4)+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                                 -s(s+1)||g4||^2, (1)

on formal data with x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i. Formal substitutions below
are used as polynomial identities; no unproved interpolation is assumed.

Te=e+P and the flow give e^TMe=0. Indeed e^TDmat e=-s^2-sum h_i^2
and omega^T(e+P)=s^2+sum h_i^2. Applying PSD to e+r*y for both
signs of the real number r gives Me=0. The same argument proves

    |<x,y>_M|^2<=||x||_M^2*||y||_M^2,
    ||x||_M^2=0 implies Mx=0,                           (2)

where <x,y>_M=x^TMy and ||x||_M^2=x^TMx. Under the flow, the
individual kernel equations are

    sum_j Lambda_ij(P_j-P_i)=2h_i(1+P_i), i<4,
    sum_(j<4)Lambda_4j(s-P_j)=2sC.                       (3)

These follow by expanding 2Me=2Dmat e+L(e+P)+T^T omega.

Put

    E=e0, J=e0+e1, B=e3+e4,
    m=||E||_M^2, S=||J||_M^2, Tcut=||B||_M^2, rJB=<J,B>_M,
    B0=Lambda30+Lambda40, B1=Lambda31+Lambda41,
    Avec=u*E-a*beta*J-a*b*mu*B,
    c03old=u*mu*z, c23old=a^2*b*beta*mu*v*t.

The three exact identities needed here are

    R=ell*mu^2*Tcut+mu*||Avec||_M^2
         +ell*mu*(Hpre*B0+beta*B1)+c03old*Lambda03+c23old*Lambda23, (4)

    D=beta*((b*w*H0pre-u)*B0+b^2*w*B1-2*b*w*rJB-beta*S
                     -u*Lambda20-b*v*c*(Lambda04+Lambda14)),       (5)

    2<E,B>_M=a*B0-Lambda03-(1+v)*Lambda04.                         (6)

To prove (4), use saturated values f_i=x_i*g_i-g_i^2/2 on

    x_base=(N,2+b,1,-c,-t), g_base=(1,1,1,-t,-t),
    x_aux =(1,-a,ab,-abc,-abt), g_aux=(1,-a,ab,-abt,-abt).

For these values Q_ij=(g_i-g_j)(x_j-g_j). In ell times the base
plus mu times the auxiliary, the complete nonzero residual list is

    Q30=Q40=-ell*mu*Hpre, Q31=Q41=-ell*mu*beta,
    Q03=-c03old, Q23=-c23old.

Every other ordered residual is zero. In particular Q13 cancels
because ell=a^2*b*(1-bt); at Q03 use ell+ab(1+abt)=ab*u, and
at Q23 use ell+a^2*b^2*mu=a^2*b*beta. Telescoping (1) gives
R; the two gradient columns modulo e are -mu*B and Avec, proving (4).

For (5), start the scalar two-step cut at x0=A with gradients
(1,1,-b), then extend FORMALLY with constant gradient -b and affine
values through the last two steps. Its energy is -D and its column
modulo e is beta*J. Its negative residuals are
Q20=Q30=Q40=-u*beta, and its positive residuals are
Q03=Q13=w*b*beta, Q04=Q14=(w+v)*b*beta. All others vanish.
Thus (1) gives

 D=b*beta*(w*(Lambda03+Lambda13)+(w+v)*(Lambda04+Lambda14))
                        -beta^2*S-u*beta*(Lambda20+B0).

Besides (6), direct multiplication of T gives

    2<e1,B>_M=beta*B0+b*B1-Lambda13-(1+v)*Lambda14.

Adding these rows, and using (w+v)-w(1+v)=-vc, gives (5).

The additional ORIGINAL row used this round is

    2<E,J+B>_M=-a*Lambda20+Lambda02+w*Lambda03+w*Lambda04.           (7)

In fact e2=e-J-B and
2M02=a*Lambda20-Lambda02-w*(Lambda03+Lambda04). This proves (7)
without any PSD quotient or new support assumption.

## 2. Why the old strictly capped equality ray cannot match

For reference, the earlier three-vector expansion used a positive k
and theta with

    F(k,theta)=b^2*w^2-z*theta+(a^2+1/(k*mu))*theta^2/4
                                                     -k*ell*mu^2,
    x=b*w-a*theta/2,
    Z=z-(a^2+1/(k*mu))*theta/2.

Here is the expansion, without importing the earlier draft as a premise.
Combining (4)--(6) and expanding two squares gives

 kR-D=||beta*J+x*B||_M^2+k*mu*||Avec-theta*B/(2*k*mu)||_M^2
       -F(k,theta)*Tcut
       +[k*ell*mu*Hpre-beta*(b*w*H0pre-u)+a*u*theta/2]*B0
       +beta*(k*ell*mu-b^2*w)*B1+beta*u*Lambda20
       +[k*c03old-u*theta/2]*Lambda03
       +[beta*b*v*c-u*(1+v)*theta/2]*Lambda04
       +beta*b*v*c*Lambda14+k*c23old*Lambda23.

For v>0, take the unique root obtained by minimizing F in
0<=theta<=Ucap:=2*beta*z/(a*u). The root lies strictly between
k0 and kold, defined below: the unrestricted minimum is already
positive at k0 and a small positive theta makes F negative at kold.
The restricted minimizing theta is min(theta(k),Ucap). Every displayed
cost is nonnegative. For the B0 cost use its value u*(1-bc) at
k0,theta=0; the other signs follow from k>k0,
theta<=theta(k)<2*k*mu*z, and theta<=Ucap.

On the strictly capped branch theta=Ucap<theta(k), so F=0 and Z>0.
If kR-D=0 and R>0, the positive costs and the two squares force

    ME=-(Z/u)*MB, MJ=-(x/beta)*MB,
    Lambda20=Lambda03=0.

Here x/beta<1 since bw<=beta and theta>0. Consequently

    M02=(Z/u)*(1-x/beta)*Tcut>0.

The two zero columns also give Tcut>0: otherwise both J and E would
have zero columns and (4), with its positive costs zero, would give
R=0. But the actual original row gives

    2M02=-Lambda02-w*Lambda04<=0.

This is a direct sign contradiction. The old relaxation could select
its Gram energies without satisfying this retained original entry.
The following quantitative argument uses (7) to remove its cap.

## 3. The unrestricted root and the new full-row remainder

Assume v>0 for now. Then ell>0, z>0 and 1-bt>0. Set

    k0=b^2*w/(ell*mu), kold=b^2*w^2/(ell*mu^2).

The minimum of F(k,theta) over all real theta is attained at

    theta(k)=2*z*k*mu/(1+a^2*k*mu).

It is strictly decreasing as a function of k: at each fixed theta,
partial F/partial k=-ell*mu^2-theta^2/(4*k^2*mu)<0, and the minimum
is attained continuously. At k0 the minimum divided by mu^2 is
b^2*(gamma-1)*(gamma+a^2*k0*mu)/(1+a^2*k0*mu)>0,
where gamma=w/mu>1. At kold it is negative, since F(kold,0)=0 and
the negative linear term improves it for small positive theta.
There is therefore a unique root k in (k0,kold).

Here is an explicit definition requiring no root search. Define

    Hbar=(2*b*w-beta
          +sqrt((beta-2*b*w)^2+4*b*w^2*(1-b*t)/mu))/(2*(1-b*t)),
    k=Hbar/(a^2*mu), theta=2*z*k*mu/(1+Hbar),
    x=b*w-a*theta/2=b*(w+Hbar*mu)/(1+Hbar), rho=x/beta,
    hcoef=theta/(2*k*mu), jcoef=a^2*theta/2,
    rstar=u*theta/2.

The positive Hbar solves

    (1-bt)*Hbar^2+(beta-2*bw)*Hbar-b*w^2/mu=0,

which follows by substituting theta(k) into F=0. Also

    0<rho<1, hcoef+jcoef=z,
    a*x-a*b*mu=hcoef, k*ell*mu^2+theta^2/(4*k*mu)
                                            =2*b*w*x-x^2.         (8)

The inequalities use mu<w and bw<=beta, with strictness supplied
by Hbar>0. The identities follow by expansion and F=0.

Full original feasibility itself forces v<2. Indeed the terminal
diagonal is n=||e4||_M^2=sum_(j<4)Lambda4j-s(s+2)>=0.
Every weight in the last row of (3) is at least v. Hence

    v*n<=s*(2C-(C+1)*v), so v<=2C/(C+1)<2.                       (9)

We now work on 0<v<2; v>=2 was already excluded by this original row.
Define the nonnegative correction

    qcorr=max(0,((1+v)*rstar-beta*b*v*c)
                         /(w+(1+v)*(1-rho))),
    rshift=rstar-qcorr*(1-rho),
    X=beta*J+x*B.

The EXACT original-array remainder is

    eps:=k*R-D
       =(1+Hbar)*||X||_M^2
         -2*(k*mu*u*a+qcorr/beta)*<E,X>_M+k*mu*u^2*m
         +cB*B0+c1*B1+c20*Lambda20+qcorr*Lambda02
         +c03*Lambda03+c04*Lambda04+c14*Lambda14+c23*Lambda23,      (10)

where

    cB=k*ell*mu*Hpre-beta*(b*w*H0pre-u)+a*rshift,
    c1=beta*(k*ell*mu-b^2*w), c20=beta*u-a*qcorr,
    c03=k*c03old-rshift+w*qcorr,
    c04=beta*b*v*c-(1+v)*rshift+w*qcorr,
    c14=beta*b*v*c, c23=k*c23old.

To check the identity, first combine (4) and (5). Its Gram part is

 beta^2*||J||_M^2+2*beta*b*w*<J,B>_M+k*ell*mu^2*Tcut
                                                 +k*mu*||Avec||_M^2.

Subtract 2*rshift*<E,B>_M+2*qcorr*<E,J+B>_M from that Gram part.
Restore them using (6) and (7); this gives precisely all costs in
(10). Since rshift=rstar-qcorr*(1-rho), the subtraction differs
from 2*rstar*<E,B> by 2*qcorr*<E,X>/beta. F=0 and (8) turn the
uncorrected Gram into ||X||^2+k*mu*||uE-aX||^2. This proves (10).

Every displayed cost is nonnegative; cB,c1,c20,c03,c14,c23 are
STRICTLY positive. Here is the full sign and PSD check.

Let

    qmax=beta*u*(sqrt(k*mu*(1+Hbar))-a*k*mu).

Then

    0<=qcorr<qmax<beta*u/(2a).                                  (11)

If qcorr=0 this follows from Hbar>0. Otherwise put
tau=sqrt(Hbar/(1+Hbar)) in (0,1), Vprod=vbc, and
Sden=2+b+c+v. Direct substitution gives

    qcorr=(beta/a)*Vprod*(u*tau^2-a*beta)
                              /(Sden-Vprod+Vprod*tau^2),
    qmax=(beta*u/a)*tau/(1+tau).

The numerator of qcorr is positive in this case. The inequality
qcorr<qmax is equivalent to

    Vprod*(1+tau)*(u*tau-a*beta)<u*tau*Sden.

Its left side is smaller than 2*Vprod*u*tau. Moreover
2*Vprod<=2v<2+b+c+v=Sden, using bc<=1 and v<2. This proves the
strict inequality. The upper bound on qmax follows from
tau/(1+tau)<1/2.

Also rshift>0: when qcorr>0 its numerator is less than
(1+v)*rstar, while its denominator is strictly larger than
(1+v)*(1-rho). Thus qcorr<rstar/(1-rho).
At k=k0 the part of cB before a*rshift equals u*(1-bc)>=0;
it strictly increases with k. Hence cB>0. Since k>k0, c1>0.
Equation (11) gives c20>beta*u/2>0. Finally
rstar<k*u*mu*z=k*c03old and rshift<=rstar, proving c03>0.
The definition of qcorr gives c04>=0, and c14,c23>0.

Set

    alpha=(k*mu*u*a+qcorr/beta)/(1+Hbar), Y=X-alpha*E,
    sigma=k*mu*u^2-(k*mu*u*a+qcorr/beta)^2/(1+Hbar)>0.

The positivity of sigma is exactly qcorr<qmax. Thus (10) is

    eps=sigma*m+(1+Hbar)*||Y||_M^2+the nonnegative costs in (10).  (12)

In particular every full original certificate obeys D<=kR. The old
cap theta<=2*beta*z/(a*u) is unnecessary once (7) is retained. On
the old strictly capped branch its root kstar is strictly larger
than this k, so an additional algebraic strip kR<D<=kstar*R is
excluded. This comparison is only with that relaxation, NOT with the
complete older scalar/triangle/curvature conjunction.

## 4. The actual quadratic column and joint first-row budget

Define

    pquad=(1,-a,ab,-abc,abc*(v-1))^T,
    Q=1-C^2*a^2*b^2*c^2*(v-1)^2,
    delta=u^2-2, W0=1+bc*(v-1).

Tp=e and Le=0. Telescoping h_i(2-h_i)p_i^2=p_i^2-p_(i+1)^2 gives

    ||pquad||_M^2=Q>=0,
    2<E,pquad>_M=u*(m-delta)
             +a^2*(beta*Lambda20+(1-bc)*Lambda30+W0*Lambda40).    (13)

For the row formula, let O0=sum_(j>0)Lambda0j and
I0=Lambda20+Lambda30+Lambda40. Then m=1-a^2-aO0 and node0 flow
gives Lambda10=O0+2u-I0. With Te0=u e-a e0 and
Mp=Dmat pquad+T^T L^T pquad/2 one obtains

 pquad^T L e0=uO0+2au-a*(beta*Lambda20+(1-bc)*Lambda30+W0*Lambda40),
 2<E,pquad>_M=2(1-a^2)-a*pquad^T L e0,

which gives (13). Both incoming weights 1-bc and W0 are nonnegative,
and 1-bc<=W0, including v<1.

By (12), eps>=sigma*m+c20*Lambda20+cB*B0. Define

    xi_cap=min(c20/(a^2*beta), cB/(a^2*W0)),

omitting the second term if W0=0. For every real xi with
xi<=xi_cap and xi<sigma/u,

    eps>=xi*u*delta-xi^2*Q/(sigma-xi*u).                         (14)

Subtract xi*u*delta using (13). All remaining multiplier coefficients
are nonnegative: for xi>=0 this is the definition of xi_cap and the
order of the incoming weights; for xi<0 the coefficients increase.
The remaining Gram expression after adding xi^2Q/(sigma-xi*u) is

    ||sqrt(sigma-xi*u)*E+xi*pquad/sqrt(sigma-xi*u)||_M^2>=0.

Thus this is the same ORIGINAL matrix and column, not independent
energy selections. Let Qplus=Q+u^2*delta. Sending xi to -infinity
shows Qplus>=0, and

    Qplus=a^2*(A^2-C^2*b^2*c^2*(v-1)^2).

That prerequisite is the old scalar-return condition and is not
counted as an independent improvement.

For completeness, when Q,Qplus>0 the free maximizing xi is
xi_free=(sigma/u)*(1-sqrt(Q/Qplus)). If xi_free<=xi_cap, (14)
has supremum

    Bcal=(sigma/u^2)*(sqrt(Qplus)-sqrt(Q))^2.

Otherwise its supremum is

    Bcal=xi_cap*u*delta-xi_cap^2*Q/(sigma-xi_cap*u).

The latter case automatically has xi_cap<sigma/u. For Q>0,Qplus=0,
the supremum is sigma*Q/u^2. For Q=0,Qplus>=0 it is
u*delta*min(xi_cap,sigma/u), and it is zero when Q=delta=0.
These statements follow on putting y=sigma-xi*u>0: the objective is

 sigma*delta+2*sigma*Q/u^2-(delta+Q/u^2)*y-Q*sigma^2/(u^2*y).

The earlier first-row budget is also retained, on its stated domain
0<a<1 and a*eta<1, where

    eta=b/(1+w+v), nu=(1+eta)*(1-a*eta)/a,
    Erow=a+3+1/a-(u*delta/a)*eta-C^2*eta^2.

Its exact identity is

 Erow=nu*(m+a*(Lambda20+B0))+(1+eta)^2*M11
                 +(1+eta)*eta*((w+v)*Lambda12+v*Lambda13).        (15)

Here is a direct derivation. For orthonormal physical vectors U,V set
gfirst=-eta*U+sqrt(nu)*V and use saturated values on

 Xphys=(U+u*gfirst,U,-b*U,-(1+v)*eta*U,-eta*U),
 Gphys=(gfirst,U,-eta*U,-eta*U,-eta*U).

The nonzero residuals are Q20=Q30=Q40=-a*nu,
Q12=-(1+eta)*(b-eta), and Q13=-(1+eta)*v*eta. Every other finite
residual is zero. Use ||gfirst||^2=[1+(1-a)*eta]/a and
<U,gfirst>+a||gfirst||^2=1-a*eta to check the target0 residuals.
The energy telescope gives Erow and the two gradient columns modulo
e are sqrt(nu)*E and (1+eta)*e1, proving (15).

In particular, for EVERY lambda>=0, (14) holds with its left side
eps+lambda*Erow, sigma replaced by sigma+lambda*nu, and cap replaced
by

 min((c20+lambda*nu*a)/(a^2*beta),
                         (cB+lambda*nu*a)/(a^2*W0)).              (16)

This follows by adding the TWO exact budgets before using (13), so
the same m and original incoming coefficients must pay for both.
It does not replace any of the older2948 conditions. For example,
putting Wrow=max(beta,W0), Krow=max(u,a*Wrow) in (13),(15),(2) gives

 u*delta<=Krow*Erow/nu+2*sqrt(Q*Erow/nu).

For delta>0, v>1, ab<=1 and c(v-1)<=1, Krow=u and Q<1. These imply
Erow>nu*delta^2/u^2 and

    b<(a+3)*(C-a-2)/((a+1)*(C+a+2)).

To see the latter equivalence, the direct factorization is

 u^2*(Erow-nu*delta^2/u^2)
    =((a+3)-(delta+u*C)*eta)*((a+3)+(u*C-delta)*eta).

The second factor is positive since C>A and u*A>delta. Substituting
eta=b/(C-A-b) in the positive first factor gives the displayed
bound. All its hypotheses remain.

## 5. A further matched full-column inequality near equality

In this section v>0 and all constants are the ones already defined.
Let

    dphys=a*b*c*v=(1+v)*z,
    nbar=s*(2C-(C+1)*v)/v>=0,
    rcoef=ell*mu^2+mu*hcoef^2>0,
    Lnorm=sqrt((u-a*alpha)^2/sigma+a^2/(1+Hbar)),
    Lterm=sqrt((alpha-beta)^2/sigma+1/(1+Hbar)),
    Kterm=(dphys/x)*Lterm*sqrt(nbar), Jfee=a/(2*x),
    Pcost=max(ell*mu*Hpre/cB,ell*mu*beta/c1,c03old/c03,1/k),
    Gcost=max(mu*Lnorm^2,Pcost).

All denominators are strictly positive. If nbar<0, the original
terminal budget (9) already excludes the word; it is never square-rooted.

Every full original array satisfies the following explicit scalar test.
Write y=sqrt(eps), which requires eps>=0, and set

 tau_max(y)=(sqrt(Q)+Lnorm*y
       +sqrt((sqrt(Q)+Lnorm*y)^2+4*jcoef*(Kterm*y+Jfee*y^2)))
                                                            /(2*jcoef),
 Psi(y)=rcoef*tau_max(y)^2
            +2*mu*hcoef*Lnorm*tau_max(y)*y+Gcost*y^2.

Then

                              R<=Psi(sqrt(eps)).                 (17)

Here is the full derivation retaining the actual quadratic column.
The vector identity, checked in all five coordinates, is

    pquad=uE-aX-jcoef*B+dphys*e4+ab*e.

The original terminal rows give

    2<E,e4>_M=a*Lambda40-Lambda04,
    2<J,e4>_M=2<E,e4>_M+beta*Lambda40+b*Lambda41-Lambda14.

Since X=Y+alpha E, they imply

 x<B,e4>_M=(alpha-beta)<E,e4>_M+<Y,e4>_M
                              -beta*(beta*Lambda40+b*Lambda41-Lambda14)/2.

Taking the inner product of pquad with B therefore gives

 <B,pquad>_M=(u-a*alpha)<E,B>_M-a<Y,B>_M-jcoef*Tcut
       +(dphys/x)*((alpha-beta)<E,e4>_M+<Y,e4>_M)
       -dphys*beta*(beta*Lambda40+b*Lambda41-Lambda14)/(2*x).       (18)

Let eps_G=sigma*m+(1+Hbar)||Y||_M^2 and let eps_C be the cost sum
in (12). Thus eps=eps_G+eps_C with both nonnegative. Weighted
Cauchy--Schwarz gives

 ||(u-a*alpha)E-aY||_M<=Lnorm*sqrt(eps_G),
 ||(alpha-beta)E+Y||_M<=Lterm*sqrt(eps_G).

Also ||e4||_M^2<=nbar by (9), and
c14=beta*dphys/a. Drop the negative Lambda40,Lambda41 terms in
(18), use Lambda14<=eps_C/c14, and use
<B,pquad>_M>=-sqrt(Tcut*Q). The result is

 jcoef*Tcut<=sqrt(Tcut*Q)
        +(Lnorm*sqrt(Tcut)+Kterm)*sqrt(eps_G)+Jfee*eps_C.          (19)

For the other direction (4) and Avec=(u-a*alpha)E-aY+hcoef*B give

 R<=rcoef*Tcut+2*mu*hcoef*Lnorm*sqrt(Tcut*eps_G)
                                    +mu*Lnorm^2*eps_G+Pcost*eps_C
  <=rcoef*Tcut+2*mu*hcoef*Lnorm*sqrt(Tcut*eps)+Gcost*eps.           (20)

The cost bound follows by comparing each cost in (4) to the cost of
the SAME multiplier in (12); their maximum ratio is precisely Pcost.
Thus costs and energies have not been chosen from different arrays.
Replacing eps_G,eps_C separately by eps in (19) gives, for
tcut=sqrt(Tcut)>=0,

 jcoef*tcut^2-(sqrt(Q)+Lnorm*y)*tcut-(Kterm*y+Jfee*y^2)<=0.

Its nonnegative root is tau_max(y). The right side of (20) increases
with tcut>=0, which proves (17).

This supplies a quantified positive-gap exclusion test. Psi is
continuous and strictly increasing on [0,infinity), tends to infinity,
and

    Psi(0)=rcoef*Q/jcoef^2.

Consequently, if R>rcoef*Q/jcoef^2, there is a unique y0>0 with
Psi(y0)=R, and every full array must have

                           eps>=y0^2>0.                         (21)

Strict increase follows from the explicit nondecreasing positive
coefficients in tau_max and Gcost>0. This is an algebraic definition
and proof; no numerical root finding is used. In particular Q=0,R>0
forces a positive eps, even when delta=0 and the first-row bound
(14) alone is zero. This prices a further original cross column.
No uniform lower bound as v,ell,R,Q or other factors vanish is claimed.

## 6. COMPLETE original-array matching on the new equality locus

Assume eps=0 and R>0, still with a,b,c>0,bc<=1,0<v<2. Equation(12)
forces ME=MY=0, hence MX=0, and

    B0=B1=Lambda20=Lambda03=Lambda14=Lambda23=0.

Equation(6) now gives Lambda04=0, and (7) gives Lambda02=0.
The first moment in (3) forces Lambda01=2; node0 flow then gives
Lambda10=2A. Its diagonal is m=1-a^2-2a, so

                         a=sqrt(2)-1, a*A=1.                    (22)

Set

    Tstar=R/rcoef>0, r13=2*rho*Tstar, Bphys=Hpre+w, s=Bphys+v.

On this locus EVERY original multiplier is forced as follows:

    Lambda01=2, Lambda10=2A, Lambda13=r13,
    Lambda12=(2*A*Hpre-(beta+w)*r13)/beta,
    Lambda21=2*Hpre+Lambda12+r13,
    Lambda24=(2*w*N+beta*Lambda21)/(w+v),
    Lambda42=Lambda24/c,
    Lambda32=2*Bphys+r13+(c-1)*Lambda42,
    Lambda43=(2*s*C-(w+v)*Lambda42)/v,
    Lambda34=(1-c)*Lambda42+Lambda43-2*s,                         (23)

and the other TEN off-diagonal coefficients are zero:

    02,03,04,14,20,23,30,31,40,41.

Let

    nstar=Lambda42+Lambda43-s*(s+2),
    vcol=(0,-rho,rho-1,1,0)^T, dcol=(0,0,0,-1,1)^T.

For this array the ENTIRE original matrix is

                  M=Tstar*vcol*vcol^T+nstar*dcol*dcol^T.          (24)

Thus a word on eps=0,R>0 has a full original certificate if and
only if (22) holds and the explicit array (23) has

    Lambda12,Lambda32,Lambda34,Lambda43>=0, nstar>=0.              (25)

All other surviving signs then follow from their displayed formulas.
In particular Lambda21>0, Lambda24>0, Lambda42>0. This is an
actual all-twenty-entry matching, not a supplied covariance-fiber test.

Here is the proof of necessity and sufficiency of these formulas.
From E=0 and X=0 in the M seminorm, MJ=-rho*MB. Equations(4),(8)
give Tcut=Tstar. The original row <e1,B> gives Lambda13=r13.
Node1's moment and flow give respectively Lambda12 and Lambda21
in (23). Since M14=0 and MJ=-rho MB with rho>0, MB has fourth
component zero. Hence M24=0 too, because e2=e-J-B. The original
entry 2M24=c*Lambda42-Lambda24 gives their displayed relation.
Node2's moment and flow give Lambda24 and Lambda32. Node4's
moment and flow give Lambda43 and Lambda34. There is no free
covariance or multiplier left.

Conversely impose (22), eps=0,R>0 and define (23), allowing real
coefficients temporarily. The node0,1,2,4 flows and moments hold by
construction. For node3 the flow follows by summing the other flows.
Its moment can be checked explicitly: substituting (23) gives

 -w*Lambda32+v*Lambda34
   =-2*w*Bphys-w*r13-c*(w+v)*Lambda42+2*s*(C-v)
   =-2*(w+N)*Bphys+2*s*(C-v)=2*v*(1+Bphys),

where c(w+v)Lambda42=2*N*Bphys-w*r13 and
w+N=C-v=1+Bphys. Thus the full flow and Me=0 hold.

The original first row vanishes because aA=1; also M14=M24=0,
M13=-rho*Tstar. Direct substitution of Lambda12 gives

    M11=-D/beta^2+2*b*w*rho*Tstar/beta=rho^2*Tstar.

For the constant term in this identity use A^2=2A+1:
1-b^2+2A-2*A*b*Hpre/beta=-D/beta^2.
For the second equality use eps=0 and (8), which give
D=(2*b*w*x-x^2)*Tstar. The kernel then determines M12.
Finally (4), with all its costs zero and E row zero, determines
Tcut=Tstar: its positive Tcut coefficient is
ell*mu^2+a^2*b^2*mu^3, and (8) gives exactly that same coefficient
after subtracting the known J terms from R=rcoef*Tstar.
Me=0 now fixes every remaining entry except M44=nstar and proves
(24). The vectors vcol and dcol are independent, so full PSD is
equivalent to nstar>=0. This proves sufficiency as well as necessity.

The matched quadratic calibration has no extra freely selectable term:

    Q=jcoef^2*Tstar+dphys^2*nstar.                              (26)

Indeed pquad^T vcol=-jcoef and pquad^T dcol=dphys, by (8). Equation(13)
applies to the flow-correct array, so (24) proves (26). Consequently
nstar>=0 is equivalently Q>=jcoef^2*R/rcoef. At Q=0 this is
impossible when R>0; the WHOLE zero quadratic column is retained.

No word at C>=193/25 is exhibited satisfying eps=0 and (22)--(25).
Nor is it proved here that their entire high-capacity region is empty.

## 7. Zero boundaries and the unresolved complete comparison

At v=0 with ell>0, z=theta=qcorr=0, k=k0=kold=b^2/ell. Identity
(10) still holds and its Gram has sigma>0, but some displayed costs
vanish. Strict-cost conclusions, Pcost, jcoef divisions, (17)--(26),
and the positive-gap equality classification above are NOT used there.
One retains the undivided identities (1)--(7),(10),(13),(15).

At ell=0 in this domain one necessarily has v=0,bc=1. Then pquad=Avec,
R=mu*Q, and the old undivided gap is b*beta*Q. No division by ell,
v,jcoef,c1,c03 or c23 is made on this boundary. No formal gradient
is deleted at v=0. At R=0 or Q=0, use the undivided norm identities;
zero M-energy always kills its WHOLE original column.

# The new positive-energy thirty-one-step state fails the full affine next join

Status: **COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED PROOF CANDIDATE**, worker max,
completed-round target 30. No numerical/model/solver evaluation, arithmetic
replay, installation, subordinate agent, or formal verifier call was used.
This is not a fact. The accepted mathematical premises are
`1252b8823bb5a290` (original interpolation inequalities and their constructive
realization) and `b98bb807d514e80d` (the original benchmark and scalar lower
bounds). The previous complete joint finding `7ffb4d0516ace32f` is a source
of defining data, not an accepted predecessor. Its full derivation is
included as Appendix A of the combined evidence file.

## 1. Result and exact scope

Take the COMPLETE nine-dimensional thirty-one-step data from the defining
construction in section 2, for any sigma in [9999/10000,1]. This includes
its two old value deficits, two newly lifted atomic blocks, nonzero
terminal residual when sigma<1, and positive terminal energy when sigma<1.
Use two copies as the children of the physical next word

    h_63=(h_31,1+r,h_31), r=lambda^2 A.

Allow independent similarities E,F with E^T E=ell I, F^T F=nu I,
ell,nu>=0; their entire cross Gram; the physical relative translation;
ALL function values; one common minimizer; and a free unqueried terminal
gradient. Impose all original interpolation rows and the full Gram PSD
condition. There is no restriction on ambient dimension, orientation,
transverse coupling, value re-selection, or relative child scale.

The candidate conclusion is that this full model has only its zero-
trajectory face. In particular it has no positive-gap sixty-three-step
parent with these fixed child vectors. More precisely, if ell>0 and all
original queried rows were nonpositive, they would imply

    Q_(36,30)+Q_(30,31)+Q_(31,39)
       +Q_(39,38)+Q_(38,37)+Q_(37,36)
          > ell gamma^2 e / 6 > 0.                         (1)

Here gamma^2 is the spatial-square scale of the child's LAST ATOM and
e=(K-1)eta>0 is its unscaled atomic receiver energy. Every index in (1)
is queried in the actual parent. The contradiction uses additional
original queried rows to price the translation and independent scales;
it does not claim the six-cycle is positive for arbitrary unconstrained
maps. At ell=0, two rows at the physical bridge force nu=0.

This resolves this particular released fixed-child affine extension at
the level of a complete UNVERIFIED joint argument. It does not exclude
arbitrary sixty-three-step trajectories or upper-bound W_63. The positive
energy and changed minimizer hull do not repair this fixed geometry: the
obstruction cancels every freely selected value before using an optimum.

The same proof isolates a local geometric motif that prevents closure:
the first and last seven-step pieces impose priced positive gradient
dependencies, an adverse initial-to-first-right-gradient correlation,
and a final residual difference parallel to the terminal gradient.
Section 11 gives its explicit necessary state inequality. Intervening
geometry and value choices do not remove that interface.

## 2. Complete defining data, including values and distance state

All norms and inner products are Euclidean; smoothness is one. Set

    s=sqrt(2), lambda=1+s, theta=s-1,
    a=(311s-16)/300, b=(590+8s)/300, rho=599/600,
    z=b-1, K=a+rho, m=a+b, eta=z/K,
    A=1+2a+b=(143+105s)/50,
    r_0=theta A, r_1=A, r_2=lambda A, r=lambda^2 A.

Take orthogonal U,V with ||U||=1, ||V||^2=eta. Define

    p=mU+KV, w_atom=rho V,
    g=(U,U,V,w_atom),
    x=(p,bU+KV,KV,w_atom),
    f_atom,j=<g_j,x_j>-||g_j||^2/2, 0<=j<=3,
    y_j=x_j-g_j, E_j=<g_j,y_j>.

The word is h_3=(a,b,a). In particular

    y_1=zU+KV, y_2=(K-1)V, y_3=0,
    <V-U,y_1>=0,
    e=E_2=(K-1)eta, d_atom=(1-rho)e=e/600,
    <w_atom-V,y_2>=-d_atom,
    R^2=||p||^2=m^2+Kz, N=||w_atom||^2=rho^2 eta,
    D=<p,w_atom>=rho z.                                (2)

Fix sigma in the stated interval and put

    delta=1-sigma, chi=delta/sigma,
    alpha=sigma r_0 sqrt(N)/R, k=alpha^2.

On the direct sum of one line and the orthogonal complements of
w_atom and p, define the isometries

    L u=(<u,w_atom/sqrt(N)>,u-<u,w_atom/sqrt(N)>w_atom/sqrt(N),0),
    T v=(-<v,p/R>,0,v-<v,p/R>p/R).

Thus <Lu,alpha Tv>=-sigma r_0<u,w_atom><p,v>/R^2.
Set t=-delta r_0 Lw_atom=chi alpha Tp. The seven-step samples are

    X_i=Lx_i, G_i=Lg_i, F_i=f_atom,i,                   i=0..3,
    X_(4+i)=t+alpha Tx_i, G_(4+i)=alpha Tg_i,
    F_(4+i)=k f_atom,i,                                i=0..3.

The word is h_7=(h_3,1+r_0,h_3). Write P_1=X_0, w_1=G_7.
The outgoing vector at bridge r_1 is d_1=t-r_1 w_1.
It equals alpha T(chi p-r_1 w_atom) and is nonzero: if chi>0
its U component before T is nonzero, and if chi=0 its V component
is nonzero. The same argument, followed by the positive similarity
B_1, shows t_2-r_2 w_2 below is nonzero.

For any child initial vector P_c of norm R and nonzero outgoing vector
d_c, define a source-matching pair of maps into a fresh direct sum by

    E_c u=(<u,d_c/||d_c||>,u-<u,d_c/||d_c||>d_c/||d_c||,0),
    B_c v=(||d_c||/R)(<v,P_c/R>,0,v-<v,P_c/R>P_c/R).

They satisfy B_c P_c=E_c d_c and

    <E_c u,B_c v>=<u,d_c><P_c,v>/R^2.                 (3)

Use (3) first with P_c=P_1,d_c=d_1, and denote its maps E_1,B_1
and squared right scale n=||d_1||^2/R^2. Set c_2=d_atom,
c_6=k d_atom, and c_i=0 otherwise. The fifteen-step data are

    Z15_i=E_1 X_i, J15_i=E_1 G_i, f15_i=F_i-c_i,       i=0..7,
    Z15_(8+i)=B_1 X_i, J15_(8+i)=B_1 G_i,
    f15_(8+i)=n F_i,                                  i=0..7.

Their word is h_15=(h_7,1+r_1,h_7), initial P_2=E_1 P_1,
terminal gradient w_2=B_1 w_1, and terminal residual t_2=B_1 t.
Use (3) again with P_c=P_2,d_c=t_2-r_2 w_2. Denote its maps E_2,B_2
and squared right scale q_scale=||t_2-r_2w_2||^2/R^2. Put

    eps_lift=chi k D/100,
    L_i=eps_lift for i=4..7,
    L_i=n eps_lift for i=12..15,
    L_i=0 for i=0..3,8..11.

The full CURRENT thirty-one-step child, denoted (Z_i,G_i,f_i), is

    Z_i=E_2 Z15_i, G_i=E_2 J15_i, f_i=f15_i,           i=0..15,
    Z_(16+i)=B_2 Z15_i, G_(16+i)=B_2 J15_i,
    f_(16+i)=q_scale(f15_i+L_i),                       i=0..15.
                                                                  (4)

These are the nine-dimensional vectors and the exact values in the
complete input, on h_31=(h_15,1+r_2,h_15). The source used the name
t_scale for q_scale; it is
renamed here to distinguish it from a vector translation. Define

    P=Z_0, w=G_31, Y_i=Z_i-G_i,
    gamma^2=q_scale n k>0, ||w||^2=gamma^2 N,
    H_i=f_i-<G_i,Z_i>+||G_i||^2/2,
    Delta_i=f_i-||G_i||^2/2.

In particular the COMPLETE value/residual transport is

    Y_i=E_2(Z15_i-J15_i),
    Delta_i=f15_i-||J15_i||^2/2,
    H_i=f15_i-<J15_i,Z15_i>+||J15_i||^2/2,            i=0..15;
    Y_(16+i)=B_2(Z15_i-J15_i),
    Delta_(16+i)=q_scale(f15_i-||J15_i||^2/2+L_i),
    H_(16+i)=q_scale(f15_i-<J15_i,Z15_i>+||J15_i||^2/2+L_i).
                                                                  (5)

Neither c_2,c_6 nor either new atomic offset has been deleted. The input
joint derivation in Appendix A proves all 1056 original rows for (4),
constructs the single static conjugate interpolant, and identifies

    argmin F_31=conv{0,E_2 E_1 t,E_2 B_1 t},
    dist(P,argmin F_31)=R.                              (6)

For sigma=1 the hull is {0}. For sigma<1 the endpoint has

    tau_res=chi D/N, nu_res=chi^2 R^2/N,
    Delta_31=tau_res gamma^2 N/100>0,
    H_31=-(99/100)tau_res gamma^2 N,
    <w,Y_31>/||w||^2=tau_res,
    ||Y_31||^2/||w||^2=nu_res.

The actual doubled-gap payoff is

    V31=gamma^2 N(1+tau_res/50)/R^2.                    (7)

Equations (5)-(7) are carried as part of the input, with the full joint
proof appended. The NEW exclusion below does not require input
interpolation feasibility, these input values, or the particular
minimizer in (6). It depends only on the explicit vectors (4), and
allows all values and the common minimizer to be reselected.

## 3. The local motifs retained by the entire new child

Put v=G_4 and set

    u_left=Y_31, u_right=Y_7, c_dep=sigma r_0,
    c_parallel=(K-1)/rho.

Every assertion in the following list follows from (2)-(4), since the
later containing maps are similarities and have zero relative translation:

    ||P||=R, ||v||^2=k,
    Y_3=Y_27=0,
    u_right=-delta r_0 G_3,
    u_left=-delta r_0 G_27,
    Y_30-u_left=c_parallel w,
    P=mG_0+aG_2+G_3,
    c_dep G_3+mG_4+aG_6+G_7=0,
    c_dep G_27+mG_28+aG_30+w=0.                    (8)

For the dependency, the first internal bridge is
t+alpha Tp=-r_0 Lw_atom, while alpha Tp=mG_4+aG_6+G_7
and t=-delta r_0 G_3. Moving terms gives its coefficient
c_dep=(1-delta)r_0. The last seven-step piece is an exact scaled
copy, so the identical calculation gives the last dependency.

There are isometric atom maps J_first,J_last with

    (G_4,G_6,G_7,u_right)
       =alpha J_first(U,V,w_atom,chi p),
    (G_28,G_30,w,u_left)
       =gamma J_last(U,V,w_atom,chi p).              (9)

The signs and scales in (9) follow from t=chi alpha Tp. In particular
G_27=-gamma J_last p/c_dep. Define the nonnegative anchor price

    Danchor=chi(D+R^2/c_dep).                        (10)

Then

    <w-G_27,u_left>=gamma^2 Danchor,
    <G_7-G_3,u_right>=k Danchor.                     (11)

Both quantities vanish at sigma=1 and must be retained otherwise.
They replace the false equality of old zero-residual anchor heights.

For the residual-shifted right initial vector define

    Pshift=P-u_right=P+delta r_0 G_3,
    Rshift^2=||Pshift||^2
            =R^2+2delta r_0 D+delta^2 r_0^2 N,
    Dshift=-<Pshift,v>
          =m(sigma r_0 D/R^2+chi k)>0.              (12)

Indeed <P,v>=-sigma r_0 Dm/R^2 by the first cross identity, whereas
<u_right,v>=chi k m. Thus the adverse signed correlation is changed
and explicitly paid; it is not copied from a zero-residual child.

## 4. The full released affine model and its normalization

Take arbitrary similarities E,F from R^9 into any common Euclidean
space, with E^T E=ell I_9,F^T F=nu I_9 and ell,nu>=0. Define

    t_join=E(u_left-rw)-FP.

The actual parent samples have

    X_i=EZ_i, g_i=EG_i,                                  i=0..31,
    X_(32+j)=t_join+FZ_j, g_(32+j)=FG_j,                  j=0..30,
    X_63=t_join+FZ_31, g_63=g_free.

The bridge X_32=E(u_left-rw) is exactly the step 1+r from X_31.
All other queried updates follow from the actual child word. All
parent values f_parent,i are free. Add one free common optimum
(z_opt,0,0). Impose every distinct original ordered row

    Q_ij=f_parent,i-f_parent,j+<g_i,X_j-X_i>
                                      +||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.      (13)

There are 4160 such rows on {star,0,...,63}. A Gram description uses
the 9 columns of E, 9 columns of F, z_opt and g_free. Its diagonal
frame blocks are ell I_9 and nu I_9; ALL other blocks are free and the
ENTIRE twenty-by-twenty Gram is PSD. In particular every singular
block range condition is retained. This is equivalent to the
Euclidean formulation above by Gram factorization.

Normalize by an actual common-minimizer distance ||EP-z_opt||, or by
the nearest-minimizer distance of the resulting static interpolant
when constructing a witness. The family's free scale is closed under
rescaling to distance one. No inherited surrogate radius replaces
that choice. Necessity of (13) follows from accepted
`1252b8823bb5a290`; sufficiency would give ONE static convex smooth
function for the entire run. Our contradiction arises entirely in
the queried rows, before either normalization or the optimum is used.

## 5. Residual shifting is a change of coordinates, with its costs kept

Subtract the common vector E u_left from ALL parent positions. Write
the left intrinsic child coordinates as Z_i-u_left, and the right
ones as Z_j-u_right. The new relative translation is the exact vector

    tbar=t_join+F u_right-E u_left
        =-r Ew-F Pshift.                              (14)

The actual problem has not been restricted. This affine rewrite is
invertible, preserves each original row, and shifts the common
optimum too. In these coordinates left sample 31 and right local
sample 7 have residual zero. Earlier anchors 27 and local 3 generally
DO NOT have zero residual.

Define intrinsic parent heights, using the free parent values, by

    hL_i=f_parent,i-<EG_i,E(Z_i-u_left)>+ell||G_i||^2/2,
    hR_j=f_parent,32+j-<FG_j,F(Z_j-u_right)>+nu||G_j||^2/2.

The right definition deliberately excludes tbar. Direct expansion of
(13) gives all within-copy rows and both cross orientations:

    Q_(i,k)=hL_i-hL_k+ell<G_i-G_k,Y_k-u_left>,
    Q_(32+j,32+l)=hR_j-hR_l+nu<G_j-G_l,Y_l-u_right>,
    Q_(i,32+j)=hL_i-hR_j+<EG_i,tbar>
                         +<EG_i-FG_j,F(Y_j-u_right)>,
    Q_(32+j,i)=hR_j-hL_i-<FG_j,tbar>
                         +<FG_j-EG_i,E(Y_i-u_left)>.
                                                                  (15)

Assume all original queried rows are nonpositive and put

    H_L=hL_31, H_R=hR_7, DeltaH=H_R-H_L.

No relation between H_L and H_R is imposed. In particular their
equality is NOT inherited from either input endpoint value.

## 6. Exact return paths and the new anchor-height interval

Apply the two opposite original rows between left 27 and 31. Since
Y_31-u_left=0 and Y_27-u_left=-u_left, (11) and (15) give

    H_L-ell gamma^2 Danchor <= hL_27 <= H_L.          (16)

The right pair at global 35 and 39 gives

    H_R-nu k Danchor <= hR_3 <= H_R.                 (17)

The left reverse atomic path consists of the actual rows
Q_(31,30),Q_(30,29),Q_(29,28). In shifted coordinates its receiving
residuals are gamma J_last y_2,gamma J_last y_1,gamma J_last y_0.
The first normal is -ell gamma^2 d_atom; the next two are zero by
(2) and the duplicate U gradients. Thus

    hL_28,hL_30 >= H_L-ell gamma^2 d_atom.            (18)

The right reverse path Q_(39,38),Q_(38,37),Q_(37,36) likewise gives

    hR_4,hR_6 >= H_R-nu k d_atom.                    (19)

For the FIRST right atom, use Q_(35,34),Q_(34,33),Q_(33,32).
Its residuals have been shifted by -u_right. Telescoping those shifts
without dropping them gives

    hR_j >= hR_3-nu d_atom+nu<G_j-G_3,u_right>, j=0,2.     (20)

For example Q_(35,34) has normal
-nu d_atom-nu<G_3-G_2,u_right>; its height inequality is exactly
the j=2 case of (20). The next two steps have unshifted normals zero
and telescope the gradient-difference terms to give the j=0 case.

Define

    Cdep=A+c_dep, Kdep=Cdep-1,
    Jprice=(A-1)d_atom+c_dep Danchor,
    Vprice=(a d_atom+c_dep Danchor)/m,
    Aplus=A+delta r_0,
    Bprice=(A-1)d_atom+Aplus k Danchor.                (21)

All are nonnegative; Cdep,Kdep are positive. The additional c_dep
Danchor term is the precise new price required by the changed child.

## 7. What the original rows force on translation and scales

The cross rows into global 39 and into left 31 have zero receiving
residuals in (15). They therefore imply, at every queried source used
below,

    <EG_i,tbar> <= H_R-hL_i,
    <FG_j,tbar> >= hR_j-H_L.                         (22)

Multiply the first inequalities by the positive coefficients of the
LAST dependency in (8), and use (16),(18). Multiply the second by
the coefficients of the FIRST dependency in (8), and use (17),(19).
Both vector sums are zero. The resulting bounds are

    -ell gamma^2 Jprice/Cdep <= DeltaH
                             <= nu k Jprice/Cdep.    (23)

Use -w=c_dep G_27+mG_28+aG_30 in the first part of (22):

    <Ew,tbar> >= -Kdep DeltaH-ell gamma^2 Jprice
              >= -Kdep nu k Jprice/Cdep-ell gamma^2 Jprice.
                                                                  (24)

Use -m v=aG_6+G_7+c_dep G_3 in the second part:

    <Fv,tbar> <= -(a+1+c_dep)DeltaH/m+nu k Vprice
              <= [(a+1+c_dep)/m]ell gamma^2 Jprice/Cdep
                                                        +nu k Vprice.
                                                                  (25)

Finally Pshift=mG_0+aG_2+(1+delta r_0)G_3. Multiply (20) and
the height for source 3 by these coefficients, using (17). The
shift correction in the weighted sum is

    <P-A G_3,u_right>=delta r_0(A N-D)>=0,

because A N>D from the bounds proved in section 8. Dropping only
this NONNEGATIVE term yields

    <F Pshift,tbar> >= Aplus DeltaH-nu Bprice.        (26)

All these bounds hold for arbitrary free values, correlations, and
scales. The zero-optimum rows were not used. The changed endpoint
energy has not been presumed zero: all free values remain in the
heights until (23)-(26) are deduced from original rows.

## 8. Rational estimates, proved from the current data

Squaring positive rationals proves 14142/10000<s<14143/10000.
Substitution into section 2 gives

    7/5<a<3/2, 341/100<m<171/50,
    241/100<K<121/50, 1<z<201/200,
    29/5<A<6, 12/5<r_0<49/20<5/2,
    14<R^2<71/5, 2/5<N<21/50,
    99/100<D<11/10, 1/2<e<3/5,
    rho>99/100, 0<=chi<=1/9999, delta<=1/10000.      (27)

For the less direct bounds: R^2=m^2+Kz is between
(341/100)^2+241/100 and (171/50)^2+(121/50)(201/200).
The latter is 14.1285<71/5. Also N=rho^2 z/K is greater than
(99/100)/(121/50)>2/5 and smaller than (201/200)/(241/100)<21/50.
Finally e=z(1-1/K) lies between 1-100/241>1/2 and
(201/200)(71/121)<3/5. All decimal expressions here are exact
terminating rationals, not numerical evaluations.

It follows that

    c_dep>239/100, c_dep<5/2, Cdep>8, Kdep<8,
    0<k<1/5, 841/25<r<36,
    0<c_parallel<3/2, 1+c_parallel/r<21/20,
    (a+1+c_dep)/m<3/2,
    0<=Danchor<1/1400,
    0<Jprice/e<1/84, 0<Jprice<1/140,
    0<=Vprice/e<1/560,
    Aplus<61/10, 0<Bprice<3/500,
    14<Rshift^2<71/5,
    Dshift/Rshift^2>1/25.                            (28)

Here are explicit checks of every price estimate. The bound on k
uses (5/2)^2(21/50)/14<1/5. Since lambda^2>29/5 and
lambda<5/2, the bounds on r follow from A>29/5 and r=lambda^2 A;
the upper r<36 follows more tightly from lambda^2<6 and A<6.
The bounds on c_parallel and the ratio 1+c_parallel/r follow from
K<121/50,rho>99/100,r>841/25. The displayed ratio involving m is
less than 5/(17/5)<3/2.

Since R^2/c_dep<(71/5)/(239/100)<6, (10) gives
Danchor<(71/10)/9999<1/1400. With e>1/2 this gives
Danchor/e<1/700. Hence

    Jprice/e < 5/600+(5/2)/700=1/84,
    Vprice/e < [(3/2)/600+(5/2)/700]/(17/5)=1/560.

Thus Jprice<(3/5)/84=1/140. Also

    Bprice<5/1000+(61/10)(1/5)(1/1400)<3/500.

The extra terms in Rshift^2 beyond R^2 are less than
2(1/10000)(5/2)(11/10)+(1/10000)^2(5/2)^2(21/50).
Adding this bound to 14.1285 gives less than 71/5, and
Rshift^2>=R^2>14. Finally (12) and (27) give

    Dshift/Rshift^2
       >= sigma r_0 Dm/(R^2 Rshift^2)
       > [(999/1000)(12/5)(99/100)(341/100)]/(71/5)^2
       > 1/25.                                      (29)

For the final rational comparison its numerator is
8.09405784>8.0656=(71/5)^2/25. The discarded chi k term in
Dshift is nonnegative. The inequality A N>D used in section 7
follows from A N>(29/5)(2/5)>11/10>D.

Equations (24)-(25) now give two convenient coarse estimates:

    <Ew,tbar> >= -nu e/420-ell gamma^2 Jprice,
    <Fv,tbar> <= (3/16)ell gamma^2 Jprice+nu e/2800,

where the upper bound remains valid if the actual left-hand side
is negative. Multiplying the latter by the positive number
1+c_parallel/r<21/20 and using the nonnegative displayed upper bound,

    <Ew,tbar>-(1+c_parallel/r)<Fv,tbar>
                  >= -nu e/360-ell gamma^2 e/70.      (30)

The rational checks are

    1/420+(21/20)/2800=463/168000<1/360,
    [1+(21/20)(3/16)]/84=383/26880<1/70.

These are charges for the NONZERO new residuals. No old equality of
anchor heights is used in (30).

## 9. Both independent scales are forced into a bounded ratio

Assume ell>0 and put T_aff=nu/(ell gamma^2). The exact bridge (14)
gives

    nu Rshift^2=r^2 ell gamma^2 N+2r<Ew,tbar>+||tbar||^2.

Use (24), before its coarse version, and the upper bound in (23).
Nonnegativity of the last norm gives

    T_aff >= [r^2 N-2r Jprice]
               /[Rshift^2+2r Kdep k Jprice/Cdep].     (31)

The numerator is positive. In fact

    2Jprice/(rN)<1/800,
    2r Kdep k Jprice/(Cdep Rshift^2)<3/400.

For the first, use Jprice<1/140, r>841/25, N>2/5.
For the second, use Kdep/Cdep<1,r<36,k<1/5,
Jprice<1/140,Rshift^2>14, giving 9/1225<3/400.
Consequently

    T_aff > (799/806)r^2 N/Rshift^2
          > (99/100)r^2 N/Rshift^2>0.                (32)

In particular a nonzero left copy cannot have nu=0. Conversely
||F Pshift+tbar||^2=r^2 ell gamma^2 N, combined with (26), gives

    nu Rshift^2 <= r^2 ell gamma^2 N-2Aplus DeltaH+2nu Bprice.

Use the lower bound in (23) to obtain

    T_aff <= [r^2N+2Aplus Jprice/Cdep]
                    /[Rshift^2-2Bprice] < 40.         (33)

Indeed its numerator is less than
36^2(21/50)+2(61/10)(1/140)/8<545; its denominator is
greater than 14-6/500>139/10. Their ratio is less than
5450/139<40. Every step used a full Euclidean norm identity;
there is no relaxation of PSD or an imposed scale matching rule.

## 10. The exact original six-cycle and its strict sign

Sum the six original rows in (1), using (15). Every free height
cancels. The receiving residuals at 31 and right local 7 are zero
in the SHIFTED coordinates; the right return path costs exactly
-nu k d_atom. Using Y_30-u_left=c_parallel w gives the exact identity

    S_cycle=<Fv,E(gamma J_last y_2)>-ell gamma^2 e
                                  +<Ew-Fv,tbar>-nu k d_atom
           =(c_parallel Dshift/r)nu-ell gamma^2 e
                +<Ew,tbar>-(1+c_parallel/r)<Fv,tbar>
                                                   -nu k d_atom.   (34)

In the second equality use gamma J_last y_2=c_parallel w and
r Ew=-tbar-F Pshift. This equality retains every translation term
from the actual new child. There is no perpendicular error term,
